Characteristic Gluing with 𝚲 \Lambda : II. Linearised equations in higher dimensions Preprint: UWThPh-2024-2
Wan Cong, Piotr T. Chruściel, and Finnian Gray

Abstract
We prove a gluing theorem for any finite number of derivatives for linearised vacuum gravitational fields in Bondi gauge on a class of characteristic hypersurfaces in static vacuum ( n + 1 ) (n+1) -dimensional backgrounds with cosmological constant Λ ∈ ℝ \Lambda\in\mathbb{R} , n ≥ 4 n\geq 4 . This provides the key step for a full nonlinear characteristic gluing for the vacuum Einstein equations near the family of metrics considered. Our work extends, in the linearised case, the pioneering analysis of Aretakis, Czimek and Rodnianski, carried-out for two derivatives on light cones in four-dimensional Minkowski spacetime, as well as our previous work on four-dimensional spacetimes.



1
Introduction

Mathematical general relativity is concerned with mathematical properties of physically relevant solutions of Einstein equations. The ultimate goal is to understand all key features of physically relevant spacetimes. This requires the ability to construct generic solutions, as well as special solutions exhibiting significant features.
Because of the nature of the Einstein equations, solutions can be constructed by solving various Cauchy problems. The celebrated work of Yvonne Fourès-Bruhat
ChBActa
provided the first key tool for this, by showing well posedness of the general relativistic spacelike Cauchy problem. An alternative is provided by the characteristic Cauchy problem
RendallCIVP
(compare
ChPaetz
;
CCG
),
which provides a construction of spacetimes by evolving characteristic data. The importance and usefulness of this Cauchy problem has been growing, starting with Christodoulou’s proof of cosmic censorship for spherically symmetric gravitating scalar fields
ChristodoulouAnnals99
, followed by Christodoulou’s monumental treatise on trapped-surface formation
ChristodoulouBHFormation
.

An important new insight into the characteristic Cauchy problem has been provided recently by Aretakis, Czimek and Rodnianski
ACR2
;
ACR3
, who showed how to glue together characteristic initial data sets, together with two transverse derivatives, near a four-dimensional Minkowskian light cone. This led to significant improvements
ACR3
;
CzimekRodnianski
in previous gluing constructions for spacelike Cauchy data, and to the construction of dynamical black hole spacetimes
KehleUnger2
;
KehleUnger1
;
KehleUnger3
with interesting properties.

A serious shortcoming of these constructions is the gluing of only two transverse derivatives on the characteristic hypersurface. This leads to poor differentiability of the resulting spacetimes, see e.g.
ChristodoulouMzH
;
MzHAIHP
or
LukRodnianski
in a small data setting.
In a recent work
ChCong1
we showed how to do the linearised gluing with any number of transverse derivatives in the setting of
ACR2
;
ACR3
. We further included a cosmological constant in the analysis, and allowed for non-spherical section of the characteristic hypersurface.

The aim of this work is to extend these results to all higher dimensions.

The analysis here provides the key step to a full nonlinear gluing, which is carried-out in the accompanying paper
ChCongGray2
.

The work here differs from that in
ChCong1
in several respects. First, the constraint equation for
\partial_{u}h_{AB}

(Equation (
3.100
) below),
contains a term which vanishes when
n=3
, with the new term forcing a different approach. Next, the kernels of some operators, such as the divergence operator acting on trace-free two-covariant symmetric tensors, are now infinite dimensional, which requires considerably more care. Last but not least,
there is a qualitative difference
in the analysis when
n>3
,
in that two separate cases arise:

1.

either
n
is even, or
n
is odd and the number of
u
-derivatives to be glued is strictly less than
\frac{n-3}{2}
; or

2.

n
is odd, and the number of
u
-derivatives to be glued exceeds the threshold
\frac{n-3}{2}
.

In the former case,
the constraint equations involving
\partial_{u}^{i}h_{uA}
and
\partial_{u}^{\ell}h_{AB}
are decoupled, in the sense that they involve different free “gluing fields”,
\overset{[j]}{\varphi}
(roughly speaking these correspond to
\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}s^{-j}h_{AB}ds
, see (
5.11
)) — the indices
j
for
\partial_{u}^{i}h_{uA}
are always integers,
while those for
\partial_{u}^{i}h_{AB}
are always half-integers. In case 2., for general values of the background mass parameter
m
, the equations involving
\partial_{u}^{i}h_{uA}
and
\partial_{u}^{\ell}h_{AB}
are coupled, calling for a more involved strategy as compared to case 1.

To make things precise, we consider the linearisation of the vacuum Einstein equations at a metric

{g}=-\big({\varepsilon}-\frac{2\Lambda r^{2}}{n(n-1)}-\frac{2m}{r^{n-2}}\big)du^{2}-2du\,dr+r^{2}{\mathring{\gamma}}_{AB}dx^{A}dx^{B}\,,\quad n>3\,,\quad m\in\mathbb{R}\,,

(1.1)

where

{\mathring{\gamma}}_{AB}dx^{A}dx^{B}
is a
u
- and
r
-independent
Einstein metric
with scalar curvature equal to
(n-1)(n-2){\varepsilon}
, with
{\varepsilon}\in\{0,\pm 1\}
.
As in
ChCong1
, the question addressed here is the following: given two smooth linearised solutions of the vacuum Einstein equations defined near the null hypersurfaces
\{u=0\,,\ r<r_{1}\}
and
\{u=0\,,\ r>r_{2}\}
, where
r_{2}>r_{1}
, can we find characteristic initial data on the missing region
\{u=0\,,\ r_{1}\leq r\leq r_{2}\}
which,
when evolved to a solution of the linearised Einstein equations, provide a linearised metric perturbation which coincides on
\{u=0\}
, together with
u
-derivatives up to order
k
, with the original data?
We refer to this construction as the
C^{k}_{u}\,C^{\infty}_{(r,x^{A})}
-gluing.

Our results can be summarised by the following theorem:

Theorem
1.1
.

A
C^{k}_{u}\,C^{\infty}_{(r,x^{A})}
-linearised vacuum data set on
{\mycal N}_{(r_{0},r_{1}]}
can be smoothly glued to another such set on
{\mycal N}_{[r_{2},r_{3})}
up to gauge if and only if the obstructions listed in
Tables
1.1
-
1.3
are satisfied.

Remark
1.2
.

In
(
ChCong1
, Table 4.1)
, we have similarly listed the obstructions in four spacetime dimensions and
m=0
. However,
some of
the obstructions there were not
gauge-invariant
,
as needed for the non-linear analysis in
ChCongGray2
. Table
1.3
provides a complete list of gauge-invariant obstructing radial charges which also applies for
n=3
;
see Appendix
A.3
.

) and

in (

).

This theorem is the main ingredient of the nonlinear gluing
ChCongGray2
, where a suitable implicit function theorem is used. In fact, particular care has been taken
here to do the linearised gluing in a way which can be promoted to a nonlinear one.

It was found by Aretakis et al., in the case
k=2
,
n=3
,
\Lambda=0
and
{{\varepsilon}}=1
, that there exists a ten-parameter family of obstructions to do such a gluing, when requiring continuity of two
u
-derivatives of the metric components along the null-hypersurface.
Our analysis shows that the result is affected by the dimension, the cosmological constant, the topology of sections of the level sets of
u
(which we assume to be compact), the mass, and the number of transverse derivatives which are required to be continuous.

We note that some introductory material below is essentially identical to that in
ChCong1
, except for minor modifications related to the change of dimensions.

Unless explicitly indicated otherwise, we assume throughout that the Lorentzian metrics relevant for the problem at hand are
(n+1)
-dimensional with
n>3
.

Acknowledgements.
The first two authors are grateful to the Beijing Institute for Mathematical Sciences and Applications for hospitality and support during part of the work on this paper.

2
Notation

An index of notation has been added at the end of this work for the convenience of the reader.

Unless explicitly indicted otherwise we use the notations of
ChCong1
, see Section 2 there, and of
ACR2
. In particular, on a
d
-dimensional sphere
S^{d}
, the notation
t^{[=\ell]}
will denote the
L^{2}
-orthogonal projection of a tensor
t
on the space of
\ell
-spherical harmonics, with

t^{[\leq\ell]}=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell}t^{[=i]}\,,\qquad t^{[>\ell]}=t-t^{[\leq\ell]}\,,

(2.1)

and with obvious similar definition of
t^{[<\ell]}
, etc.
As in
ChCong1

we denote by
\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(1)}}
the divergence operator on vector fields,

\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(1)}}\xi:=\mathring{D}_{A}\xi^{A}\,,

(2.2)

and by
\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}
that on two-symmetric trace-free tensors:

(\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}h)_{A}:=\mathring{D}^{B}h_{AB}\,.

(2.3)

However, the operator
\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}}
is now defined to be

\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}}:=\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(1)}}\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\,.

(2.4)

The Laplace operator of the metric
{\mathring{\gamma}}
is denoted by
\mathring{\Delta}
.

We let
H^{k}(\mathbf{S})
denote the Hilbert space of tensor fields whose derivatives of order less than or equal to
k
are in
L^{2}(\mathbf{S})
.
Given any tensor subspace
X\subseteq H^{k}(\mathbf{S})
and tensor field
T\in H^{k}(\mathbf{S})
, we will write
T^{[X]}
for the
L^{2}
-orthogonal projection of
T
on
X
. In particular, given any linear differential operator
\hat{D}
acting on
H^{k}(\mathbf{S})
, we will write
T^{[\ker\hat{D}]}
for the
L^{2}
-orthogonal projection of
T
on the kernel of
\hat{D}
, and
T^{[(\ker\hat{D})^{\perp}]}
for the projection on
(\ker\hat{D})^{\perp}
;
T^{[\text{im}\hat{D}]}
and
T^{[(\text{im}\hat{D})^{\perp}]}
are defined similarly.

The formal
L^{2}
-adjoint of an operator
\hat{D}
will be denoted by
\hat{D}^{\dagger}
.

We define the subspaces
S,V\subset H^{k}({\mathbf{S}})
of vector fields on
\mathbf{S}
as

S=\{\xi_{A}:\xi_{A}={\mathring{D}}_{A}\phi\,,\phi\in H^{k+1}(\mathbf{S})\}\,,\quad V=\{\xi_{A}\in H^{k}({\mathbf{S}}):{\mathring{D}}^{A}\xi_{A}=0\}\,,

where the differentiability index
k
should be clear from the context.
When
\mathbf{S}
is compact and boundaryless, the spaces
S
and
V
are
L^{2}
-orthogonal.
Any vector field
\xi\in H^{k}({\mathbf{S}})
,
k\geq 1
, can thus be decomposed into its “scalar” and “vector” parts (cf. Appendix
C.3
, p.
C.3
), which we shall denote by

\displaystyle\xi=\xi^{[S]}+\xi^{[V]}\,.

(2.5)

We denote the decomposition of a symmetric traceless two-covariant tensor field
h
into its “scalar”, “vector”, and “tensor” part as

h=h^{[S]}+h^{[V]}+h^{[{\mathrm{TT}}]}\,,

(2.6)

where
h^{[{\mathrm{TT}}]}
is a transverse-traceless (
{\mathrm{TT}}
) tensor,
h^{[V]}
is the Killing operator acting on a divergence-free vector, and
h^{[S]}
is the trace-free part of the Hessian of a function (cf. Appendix
C.2
).

Note that we use the notation
S
both for the space of “scalar vectors”
\xi^{[S]}
and “scalar tensors”
h^{[S]}
, similarly for
V
, hoping that the distinction will be clear from the context.

As already pointed out, different strategies are needed when the number
k
of transverse derivatives that one wants to glue exceeds the threshold
k=(n-3)/2
for odd values of
n
. To address this we will use the following terminology: consider a pair
(n,k)\in\mathbb{N}^{2}
, where
n>3
stands for the space-dimension and
k
for the number of transverse derivatives that are glued. We say that
(n,k)
is

convenient
if
n
is even and
k
is arbitrary, or if
n
is odd and
k<\frac{n-3}{2}
. Otherwise the pair
(n,k)
will be said to be
inconvenient
.

3
Characteristic constraint equations in Bondi coordinates

Let
(\mathcal{M},g)
be an
(n+1)
-dimensional spacetime. Locally near a null hypersurface with non-vanishing divergence scalar one can assign Bondi-type coordinates
(u,r,x^{A})
in which the metric takes the form (see
MaedlerWinicour
and references therein)

\displaystyle g_{\alpha\beta}dx^{\alpha}dx^{\beta}

\displaystyle=-\frac{V}{r}e^{2\beta}du^{2}-2e^{2\beta}dudr

\displaystyle\qquad+r^{2}\gamma_{AB}\Big(dx^{A}-U^{A}du\Big)\Big(dx^{B}-U^{B}du\Big)\,,

(3.1)

with
\gamma_{AB}
satisfying

\det[\gamma_{AB}]=\det[\mathring{\gamma}_{AB}]\,,

(3.2)

where
\det[\mathring{\gamma}_{AB}]
is
r
- and
u
-independent. This implies in particular

\gamma^{AB}\partial_{r}\gamma_{AB}=0\,,\qquad\gamma^{AB}\partial_{u}\gamma_{AB}=0\,.

(3.3)

The inverse metric reads

g^{\sharp}=e^{-2\beta}\frac{V}{r}\,\partial_{r}^{2}-2e^{-2\beta}\,\partial_{u}\partial_{r}-2e^{-2\beta}U^{A}\,\partial_{r}\partial_{A}+\frac{1}{r^{2}}\gamma^{AB}\,\partial_{A}\partial_{B}\,.

(3.4)

In these coordinates, each level set of
u
is a null hypersurfaces with normal field
\partial_{r}
, while
r
is a parameter which varies along the null generators. The
x^{C}
’s are local coordinates on the codimension two surfaces of constant
(u,r)
, which foliate each constant
u
null hypersurface.

The restriction of the Einstein equations to a level set of
u
gives rise to a set of transport equations for the metric functions
(V,\beta,U^{A},\gamma_{AB})
. In this paper we consider the linearised equations around a
Birmingham-Kottler background, which includes the Minkowski, anti-de Sitter, and de Sitter background.
In Bondi coordinates the background metrics can be written as

{\mathring{g}}\equiv{\mathring{g}}_{\alpha\beta}dx^{\alpha}dx^{\beta}=\mathring{g}_{uu}du^{2}-2du\,dr+r^{2}{\mathring{\gamma}}_{AB}dx^{A}dx^{B}\,,

(3.5)

with

\mathring{g}_{uu}:=-\left({\varepsilon}-\alpha^{2}r^{2}-{\frac{2m}{r^{n-2}}}\right)\,,\quad{\varepsilon}\in\{0,\pm 1\}\,,\quad\alpha\in\{0,\sqrt{\frac{2|\Lambda|}{n(n-1)}},\sqrt{\frac{2|\Lambda|}{n(n-1)}}i\}\,,

(3.6)

and where

{\mathring{\gamma}}_{AB}dx^{A}dx^{B}
is a
u
- and
r
-independent
Einstein metric of scalar curvature equal to
(n-1)(n-2){\varepsilon}
, with the associated Ricci tensor, which we denote by
\mathring{R}_{AB}
, thus equal to

\mathring{R}_{AB}=(n-2){{\varepsilon}}\,{\mathring{\gamma}}_{AB}\,.

(3.7)

We emphasise that
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}\cup i\mathbb{R}
, with a purely imaginary value of
\alpha
allowed to accommodate for a cosmological constant
\Lambda<0
. Finally, the parameter
m
is related to the total mass of the spacetime. The inverse background metrics read

\mathring{g}^{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\alpha}\partial_{\beta}=-2\partial_{u}\partial_{r}-\mathring{g}_{uu}(\partial_{r})^{2}+r^{-2}\mathring{\gamma}^{AB}\partial_{A}\partial_{B}\,.

Consider now a perturbation of the metric of the form

\mathring{g}_{\mu\nu}\rightarrow\mathring{g}_{\mu\nu}+\epsilon h_{\mu\nu}\,.

(3.8)

The conditions on the linearised fields such that the perturbed metric is still in the Bondi form to
O(\epsilon)
are,

h_{rA}=h_{rr}=\mathring{\gamma}^{AB}h_{AB}=0\,.

(3.9)

We shall sometimes denote the metric perturbations by
\{\delta V,\delta\beta,\delta U_{A}\}
. These correspond respectively to

\{\delta V+2V\delta\beta,\delta\beta,\delta U_{A}\}\equiv\{-rh_{uu},-h_{ur}/2,-h_{uA}/r^{2}\}\,.

(3.10)

We will also use the notation

\check{h}_{\mu\nu}:=\frac{h_{\mu\nu}}{r^{2}}\,.

(3.11)

3.1
The linearised
C^{k}_{u}\,C^{\infty}_{(r,x^{A})}
-gluing problem

Let
{\mycal N}_{I}
be a null hypersurface
\{u=u_{0},r\in I\}
, where
I
is an interval in
\mathbb{R}
, and let
\mathbf{S}
be a cross-section of
{\mycal N}_{I}
, i.e. a two-dimensional submanifold of
{\mycal N}_{I}
meeting each null generator of
{\mycal N}_{I}
precisely once.
Let
2\leq k\in\mathbb{N}
be the number of derivatives of the metric that we want to control at
\mathbf{S}
. Using the Bondi parameterisation of the metric, we define
linearised Bondi
cross-section data
of order
k
as the collection of fields

x=(\partial_{u}^{j}h_{AB}|_{\mathbf{S}},\,\partial_{r}^{j}h_{AB}|_{\mathbf{S}},\,\partial_{u}^{j}\delta\beta|_{\mathbf{S}},\,\partial_{u}^{j}\delta U^{A}|_{\mathbf{S}},\,\partial_{r}\delta U^{A}|_{\mathbf{S}},\,\delta V|_{\mathbf{S}})_{0\leq j\leq k}\,.

(3.12)

and we denote the set of all possible data as
\delta\Psi_{\mathrm{Bo}}[\mathbf{S},k]
.
The data
\delta\Psi_{\mathrm{Bo}}[\mathbf{S},k]
correspond to (an optimised version of) the linearised data
\Psi_{\mathrm{Bo}}[\mathbf{S},k]
of
(
ChCong0
, Section 5)
.

Unless explicitly indicated otherwise we assume that all the fields in (
3.12
)
are smooth, though a finite sufficiently large degree of differentiability would suffice for our purposes, as can be verified by chasing the number of derivatives in the relevant equations.

In the linearised
C^{k}_{u}\,C^{\infty}_{(r,x^{A})}
-gluing problem we start with two cross-sections
\mathbf{S}_{1}
and
\mathbf{S}_{2}
, each equipped with some linearised Bondi cross-section data of order
k
, which we denote as
x_{1}\in\delta\Psi_{\mathrm{Bo}}[\mathbf{S}_{1},k]
and

x_{2}\in\delta\Psi_{\mathrm{Bo}}[\mathbf{S}_{2},k]
. The goal is to construct linearised fields

\displaystyle y:=(\partial^{\ell}_{u}\delta V,\partial^{\ell}_{u}\delta\beta,\partial^{\ell}_{u}\delta U^{A},\partial^{\ell}_{u}h_{AB})

(3.13)

for
0\leq\ell\leq k
which interpolate between
x_{1}
and
x_{2}
along a null hypersurface
{\mycal N}_{[r_{1},r_{2}]}
such that (i)
x_{1}
agrees with the restriction to
r_{1}
of
y
; (ii)
x_{2}
agrees with the restriction to
r=r_{2}
of
y
; and (iii)
y
satisfy the linearised null constraint equations.

Since linearised Bondi data are defined up to linearised gauge transformations, we shall use these transformations to remove part of the obstructions to the gluing.

3.2
Gauge Freedom

Recall that linearised gravitational fields are defined up to a gauge transformation

h\mapsto h+{\mathcal{L}}_{\zeta}g

(3.14)

determined by a vector field
\zeta
. Once the metric perturbation have been put into Bondi gauge, there remains the freedom to make gauge transformations which preserve this gauge:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{\zeta}g_{rr}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0\,,

(3.15)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{\zeta}g_{rA}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0\,,

(3.16)

\displaystyle g^{AB}{\mathcal{L}}_{\zeta}g_{AB}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0\,.

(3.17)

For the metric (
3.5
) this is solved by (cf., e.g.,
ChHMS
)

\displaystyle\zeta^{u}(u,r,x^{A})

\displaystyle=\xi^{u}(u,x^{A})\,,

(3.18)

\displaystyle\zeta^{B}(u,r,x^{A})

\displaystyle=\xi^{B}(u,x^{A})-\frac{1}{r}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\xi^{u}(u,x^{A})\,,

(3.19)

\displaystyle\zeta^{r}(u,r,x^{A})

\displaystyle=-\frac{r}{n-1}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\xi^{B}(u,x^{A})+\frac{1}{n-1}\mathring{\Delta}\xi^{u}(u,x^{A})\,,

(3.20)

for some fields
\xi^{u}(u,x^{A})
,
\xi^{B}(u,x^{A})
, and where
{\mathring{D}}_{A}
and
\mathring{\Delta}
are respectively the covariant derivative and the Laplacian operator associated with the
(n-1)
-dimensional metric
{\mathring{\gamma}}_{AB}
appearing in (
3.5
).

We will use the symbol

\mathring{\mathcal{L}}_{\zeta}

to denote Lie-derivation in the
x^{A}
-variables
with respect to the vector field
\zeta^{A}\partial_{A}
.

The transformation (
3.14
) can be viewed as a result of linearised coordinate transformation to new coordinates
\tilde{x}^{\mu}
such that

x^{\mu}=\tilde{x}^{\mu}+\epsilon\zeta^{\mu}(\tilde{x}^{\mu})\,,

(3.21)

where
\epsilon
is as in (
3.8
).
Recalling that
\mathring{g}_{uu}
reads

\mathring{g}_{uu}=-{\varepsilon}+\alpha^{2}r^{2}+{\frac{2m}{r^{n-2}}}\,,

under (
3.21
) the linearised metric transforms as

\displaystyle h_{uA}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{uA}=h_{uA}+{\mycal L}_{\zeta}g_{uA}

(3.22)

\displaystyle=h_{uA}+\partial_{A}(\mathring{g}_{uu}\zeta^{u}-\zeta^{r})+r^{2}{\mathring{\gamma}}_{AB}\partial_{u}\zeta^{B}

\displaystyle=h_{uA}-\frac{1}{n-1}\partial_{A}\,\big[\,(\mathring{\Delta}\xi^{u}+(n-1){\varepsilon}\xi^{u})-r({\mathring{D}}_{B}\xi^{B}-(n-1)\partial_{u}\xi^{u})\big]

\displaystyle\quad+r^{2}({\mathring{\gamma}}_{AB}\partial_{u}\xi^{B}+(\alpha^{2}+{2m}{r^{-n}})\partial_{A}\xi^{u})\,,

\displaystyle h_{ur}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{ur}=h_{ur}+{\mycal L}_{\zeta}g_{ur}=h_{ur}-\partial_{u}\zeta^{u}+\mathring{g}_{uu}\partial_{r}\zeta^{u}-\partial_{r}\zeta^{r}

(3.23)

\displaystyle=h_{ur}-\partial_{u}\xi^{u}+\frac{1}{n-1}{\mathring{D}}^{A}\xi_{A}\,,

\displaystyle h_{uu}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{uu}=h_{uu}+{\mycal L}_{\zeta}g_{uu}=h_{uu}+\zeta^{r}\partial_{r}\mathring{g}_{uu}+2\partial_{u}(\mathring{g}_{uu}\zeta^{u}-\zeta^{r})

(3.24)

\displaystyle=h_{uu}-2\big({\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{n-1}\mathring{\Delta}\big)\partial_{u}\xi^{u}+\frac{2r}{n-1}\Big({\mathring{D}}_{B}\partial_{u}\xi^{B}+\big(\alpha^{2}-\frac{(n-2)m}{r^{n}}\big)\mathring{\Delta}\xi^{u}\Big)

\displaystyle+2r^{2}\left[\left(\alpha^{2}+\frac{2m}{r^{n}}\right)\partial_{u}\xi^{u}-\frac{1}{n-1}\left(\alpha^{2}-\frac{(n-2)m}{r^{n}}\right){\mathring{D}}_{B}\xi^{B}\right]\,,

\displaystyle h_{AB}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{AB}=h_{AB}+{\mycal L}_{\zeta}g_{AB}=h_{AB}+2r\zeta^{r}{\mathring{\gamma}}_{AB}+r^{2}\mathring{\mathcal{L}}_{\zeta}{\mathring{\gamma}}_{AB}

(3.25)

\displaystyle=h_{AB}+r^{2}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[\mathring{\mathcal{L}}_{\zeta}{\mathring{\gamma}}_{AB}]\,,

with

\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[X_{AB}]:=\frac{1}{2}\big(X_{AB}+X_{BA}-\frac{2}{n-1}{\mathring{\gamma}}^{CD}X_{CD}{\mathring{\gamma}}_{AB}\big)

denoting
the traceless symmetric part of a tensor on a section
\mathbf{S}
.

Given a section

\mathbf{S}_{u_{0},r_{0}}:=\{u=u_{0},r=r_{0}\}

of a null hypersurface
{\mycal N}_{I}
together with linearised Bondi sphere data
x\in\delta\Psi_{\mathrm{Bo}}[\mathbf{S}_{u_{0},r_{0}},k]
, Equations (
3.22
)-(
3.25
) and their

u
- and
r
-derivatives provide a set of order-
k
cross-section data
\tilde{x}\in\delta\Psi_{\mathrm{Bo}}[\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{u_{0},r_{0}},k]
on

\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{u_{0},r_{0}}:=\{\tilde{u}=u_{0},\tilde{r}=r_{0}\}=\{u=u_{0}+\epsilon\zeta^{u}(u_{0},r_{0},x^{A}),r=r_{0}+\epsilon\zeta^{r}(u_{0},r_{0},x^{A})\}\,,

which is a small deformation of the original
\mathbf{S}_{u_{0},r_{0}}
, in terms of the gauge fields

z:=\{\partial^{\ell}_{u}\xi^{B}|_{u=u_{0}},\partial^{\ell}_{u}\xi^{u}|_{u=u_{0}}\}_{0\leq\ell\leq k+1}

as well as the original data
x
.
We shall write the gauge transformation as a map
z^{*}
such that

\displaystyle\tilde{x}=z^{*}(x)\,,

(3.26)

with
z^{*}(x)
given by the Equations (
3.22
)-(
3.25
) and their

u
- and
r
-derivatives

Equation (
3.23
) shows that we can always choose
z
so that

\tilde{h}_{ur}=0\,.

(3.27)

After having done this, we are left with a residual set of gauge transformations, defined by a
u
-parameterised family of vector fields
\xi^{A}(u,\cdot)
on
\mathbf{S}
, together with

\zeta^{r}(u,\cdot)
and

\partial_{u}\xi^{u}(u,x^{A})=\frac{{\mathring{D}}_{B}\xi^{B}(u,x^{A})}{n-1}\,.

(3.28)

Taking into account (
3.28
), the transformed fields now read

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{uA}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h_{uA}-\frac{1}{n-1}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\mathring{\Delta}\xi^{u}+\mathring{g}_{uu}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi^{u}+r^{2}\partial_{u}\xi_{A}

(3.29)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h_{uA}-\frac{1}{n-1}\partial_{A}\,[\,(\mathring{\Delta}\xi^{u}+(n-1){\varepsilon}\xi^{u})]

\displaystyle+r^{2}({\mathring{\gamma}}_{AB}\partial_{u}\xi^{B}+(\alpha^{2}+{{2m}{r^{-n}}})\partial_{A}\xi^{u})\,,

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{uu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h_{uu}-\frac{2}{n-1}\Big({\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{n-1}\mathring{\Delta}{-\frac{nm}{r^{n-2}}}\Big){\mathring{D}}_{B}\xi^{B}

(3.30)

\displaystyle+\frac{2r}{n-1}\Big(\big(\alpha^{2}-{\frac{(n-2)m}{r^{n}}}\big)\mathring{\Delta}\xi^{u}+{\mathring{D}}_{B}\partial_{u}\xi^{B}\Big)\,,

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{AB}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h_{AB}+2r^{2}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi_{B}]-2r\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\xi^{u}]\,,

(3.31)

where
\xi_{A}:={\mathring{\gamma}}_{AB}\xi^{B}
.

In the linearised gluing problem, we shall allow for such gauge perturbations to the data.
That is, we consider gluing along a null hypersurface of the perturbed data, which we will denote as
\tilde{x}_{1}\in\delta\Psi_{\mathrm{Bo}}[\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{1},k]
and
\tilde{x}_{2}\in\delta\Psi_{\mathrm{Bo}}[\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{2},k]
, with the freedom of choosing gauge fields to achieve the gluing.

To simplify notation we will write

\displaystyle{\operatorname{L_{1}}}(\xi^{u})_{A}

\displaystyle:=-\frac{1}{n-1}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\,[\,\mathring{\Delta}\xi^{u}+(n-1){\varepsilon}\xi^{u}]=-\frac{1}{n-2}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\xi^{u}]\,,

(3.32)

\displaystyle C(\zeta)_{AB}

\displaystyle:=\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}\zeta_{B}]\,,

(3.33)

\displaystyle{\operatorname{L_{2}}}(\xi)

\displaystyle:=-\frac{2}{n-1}\left({\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{n-1}\mathring{\Delta}\right){\mathring{D}}_{B}\xi^{B}\,.

(3.34)

For further convenience we note the transformation laws, in this notation,

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{uA}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h_{uA}+{\operatorname{L_{1}}}(\xi^{u})_{A}+r^{2}\left(\partial_{u}\xi_{A}+\left(\alpha^{2}{+{2m}{r^{-n}}}\right){\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi^{u}\right)\,,

(3.35)

\displaystyle\partial_{u}^{i}\tilde{h}_{uA}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{u}^{i}h_{uA}+\frac{1}{n-1}{\operatorname{L_{1}}}({\mathring{D}}_{B}\partial_{u}^{i-1}\xi^{B})_{A}+r^{2}(\partial_{u}^{i+1}\xi_{A}+\frac{\alpha^{2}{+2mr^{-n}}}{n-1}{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\partial_{u}^{i-1}\xi^{B})\,,\ i\geq 1\,,\phantom{xxxxxx}

(3.36)

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{uu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h_{uu}+\frac{2r}{n-1}\big((\alpha^{2}{-(n-2)mr^{-n}})\mathring{\Delta}\xi^{u}+{\mathring{D}}_{B}\partial_{u}\xi^{B}{+nmr^{-n+1}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\xi^{B}}\big)+{\operatorname{L_{2}}}(\xi)\,,

(3.37)

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{AB}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h_{AB}+2r^{2}C(\zeta)_{AB}

(3.38)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h_{AB}+2r^{2}C(\xi)_{AB}-2r\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\xi^{u}]\,,

\displaystyle\partial_{u}^{i}\tilde{h}_{AB}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{u}^{i}h_{AB}+2r^{2}C(\partial_{u}^{i}\xi)_{AB}-\frac{2r}{n-1}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}{\mathring{D}}_{C}\partial_{u}^{i-1}\xi^{C}]\,,\ i\geq 1\,,

(3.39)

\displaystyle{\mathring{D}}^{A}\tilde{h}_{uA}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathring{D}}^{A}h_{uA}-\frac{1}{n-1}\mathring{\Delta}\,[\,(\mathring{\Delta}\xi^{u}+(n-1){\varepsilon}\xi^{u})]+r^{2}({\mathring{D}}_{A}\partial_{u}\xi^{A}+\left(\alpha^{2}{+{2m}{r^{-n}}}\right)\mathring{\Delta}\xi^{u})\,,

(3.40)

\displaystyle{\mathring{D}}^{B}\tilde{h}_{AB}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}+r^{2}\big((\mathring{\Delta}+(n-2){\varepsilon}){\mathchoice{\delta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B}}}_{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\delta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B}}}_{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\delta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B}}}_{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\delta^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01392pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.675pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.625pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{B}}}_{{{A}\mathchoice{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.383pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.96252pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.83037pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}}+\frac{n-3}{n-1}{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\big)\xi_{B}+2r{(n-2)}{\operatorname{L_{1}}}(\xi^{u})_{A}\,,

(3.41)

\displaystyle{\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\tilde{h}_{AB}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}+r^{2}\bigg[\frac{2(n-2)}{n-1}\mathring{\Delta}+2(n-2){\varepsilon}\bigg]{\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi^{A}-\frac{2r{(n-2)}}{n-1}\mathring{\Delta}\big(\mathring{\Delta}+(n-1){\varepsilon}\big)\xi^{u}

(3.42)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}-(n-1)(n-2)r^{2}{\operatorname{L_{2}}}(\xi)-\frac{2r{(n-2)}}{n-1}\mathring{\Delta}\big(\mathring{\Delta}+(n-1){\varepsilon}\big)\xi^{u}\,.

3.3
Null Constraint Equations

We now turn our attention to the Einstein equations,

G_{\mu\nu}:=R_{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{2}g_{\mu\nu}R=8\pi T_{\mu\nu}-\Lambda g_{\mu\nu}\,,

(3.43)

and their linearisation, in Bondi coordinates,
at a Birmingham-Kottler metric (
3.5
)-(
3.6
). We shall see that the restrictions of the Einstein equations on a null hypersurface
{\mycal N}_{[r_{1},r_{2}]}
can be written as
transport equations
for various linearised metric functions in
r
, that is, ordinary differential equations in the variable
r
, with source terms depending on
h_{AB}
.
In particular, given
x_{r_{1}}\in\delta\Psi_{\mathrm{Bo}}[\mathbf{S}_{r_{1}},k]
and any
h_{AB}(r)|_{{\mycal N}_{[r_{1},r_{2}]}}
,
C^{k}
in the
r
variable, compatible with
x_{r_{1}}
,
the linearised Einstein equations can be solved on
{\mycal N}_{[r_{1},r_{2}]}
by integrating the transport equations, such as e.g. (
3.51
) below. Thus the characteristic gluing problem amounts to solving for a field
h_{AB}|_{{\mycal N}}
that can interpolate continuously between the given
x_{r_{1}}
and
x_{r_{2}}\in\delta\Psi_{\mathrm{Bo}}[\mathbf{S}_{r_{2}},k]
up to gauge. More details will be provided in the following sections.

3.3.1
h_{ur}

The
G_{rr}
component of the Einstein tensor (compare
MaedlerWinicour
), reads:

\frac{r}{2(n-1)}G_{rr}=\partial_{r}\beta-\frac{r}{8(n-1)}\gamma^{AC}\gamma^{BD}(\partial_{r}\gamma_{AB})(\partial_{r}\gamma_{CD})\,.

(3.44)

Since the second term on the right-hand side of (
3.44
) is quadratic in
\partial_{r}\gamma_{AB}
, after linearising in vacuum we find that the transport equation of
\delta\beta
is given by

\partial_{r}\delta\beta=0\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\delta\beta=\delta\beta(u,x^{A})\,.

(3.45)

Using a terminology somewhat similar to that of
ACR2
, we thus obtain a pointwise radial conservation law for
\delta\beta
, and a seeming obstruction to gluing: two linearised Bondi cross-section data can be glued together if and only if their Bondi functions
\delta\beta
coincide.

However, it follows from (
3.27
) that we can always choose a gauge so that
\delta\beta\equiv 0
. Thus, (
3.45
) does not lead to an obstruction to gluing-up-to-gauge. The vanishing of
\delta\beta
will be assumed when gluing.

But, in the current section we will
not
assume
\delta\beta=0

unless explicitly indicated otherwise.

3.3.2
h_{uA}

From the
G_{rA}
-component of the Einstein equations one has

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\left[r^{n+1}e^{-2\beta}\gamma_{AB}(\partial_{r}U^{B})\right]=2r^{2(n-1)}\partial_{r}\Big(\frac{1}{r^{n-1}}{D}_{A}\beta\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad-r^{n-1}\gamma^{EF}{D}_{E}(\partial_{r}\gamma_{AF})+16\pi r^{n-1}T_{rA}\,.

(3.46)

The linearisation of
G_{rA}
at a Birmingham-Kottler metric gives

\displaystyle 2r^{n-1}\delta G_{rA}=\partial_{r}\left[r^{n+1}{\mathring{\gamma}}_{AB}(\partial_{r}\delta U^{B})\right]-2r^{2(n-1)}\partial_{r}\Big(\frac{1}{r^{n-1}}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\delta\beta\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+r^{n-1}\partial_{r}\left(r^{-2}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}\right)\,.

(3.47)

The linearised vacuum Einstein equation thus gives

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\left(r^{n+1}\partial_{r}\check{h}_{uA}-r^{n-3}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}\right)

\displaystyle=-2r^{2(n-1)}\partial_{r}\Big(\frac{1}{r^{n-1}}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\delta\beta\Big)-(n-1)r^{n-4}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}\,.

(3.48)

Using (
3.45
), we can rewrite this as

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\left(r^{n+1}\partial_{r}\check{h}_{uA}-2{r^{n-1}}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\delta\beta-r^{n-3}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}\right)=-(n-1)r^{n-4}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}\,.

(3.49)

We write this as a transport equation for a field
\overset{(*)}{H}_{uA}
(the rationale behind this notation will become clear in Section
4
):

\displaystyle\overset{(*)}{H}_{uA}:=r^{n+1}\partial_{r}\check{h}_{uA}-r^{n-3}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}-2{r^{n-1}}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\delta\beta\,,\quad\partial_{r}\overset{(*)}{H}_{uA}=-(n-1)r^{n-4}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}\,.

(3.50)

Integrating, one obtains a representation formula for
\overset{(*)}{H}_{uA}
on
{\mycal N}_{[r_{1},r_{2}]}
which reads

\displaystyle\overset{(*)}{H}_{uA}(r,\cdot)=\overset{(*)}{H}_{uA}(r_{1},\cdot)-(n-1)\int_{r_{1}}^{r}\hat{\kappa}_{4-n}(s){\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}(s,\cdot)\,ds\,,

(3.51)

with

\displaystyle\hat{\kappa}_{-(n-4)}(s):=s^{n-4}\,,

(3.52)

and where the change of sign in the exponent, compared to the sign of the index of
\hat{\kappa}
, is motivated by consistency of notation
with
ChCong1
.

Now, the kernel of
\operatorname{(\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)})^{\dagger}}
is spanned by the space, which we denote by
{\mathrm{CKV}}
, of conformal Killing vectors of
\mathbf{S}
, i.e. solutions of the system

\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}\pi_{B}]=0\,,

(3.53)

with
\pi_{A}=\pi_{A}(u,x^{B})
.
On
S^{n-1}
the dimension of
{\mathrm{CKV}}
is
\frac{n(n+1)}{2}
.

A classical theorem in conformal geometry shows that, for all remaining compact Riemannian Einstein manifolds, conformal Killing vectors are Killing vectors.
(This follows e.g. from
(
Kuehnel
, Theorem 24)
, since the flow of a proper conformal Killing vector, i.e., a conformal Killing vector which is not a Kiling vector, provides the conformal factor in this theorem, which cannot be constant on compact manifolds.)
On a
(n-1)
-dimensional torus
\mathbb{T}^{n-1}
, solutions of (
3.53
) belong to the
(n-1)
-dimensional space of covariantly constant vectors. Finally, the space of solutions of (
3.53
) on a
(n-1)
-dimensional negatively curved compact manifold is trivial;
compare Appendix
C.1
.

The projection of the transport Equation (
3.50
) onto
\pi_{A}\in{\mathrm{CKV}}
gives

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\int_{\mathbf{S}}\pi^{A}\overset{(*)}{H}_{uA}\,d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}=-(n-1)\int_{\mathbf{S}}\pi^{A}r^{n-4}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}\,d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}=0\,,

(3.54)

and thus

\displaystyle\overset{[1]}{Q}(\pi^{A}):=\int_{\mathbf{S}}\pi^{A}\overset{(*)}{H}_{uA}=\int_{\mathbf{S}}\pi^{A}\left[r^{n+1}\partial_{r}(r^{-2}h_{uA})-2{r^{n-1}}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\delta\beta\right]\,d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}

(3.55)

forms a family of
conserved radial charges
, with

\partial_{r}\overset{[1]}{Q}(\pi^{A})=0\,,\quad\pi^{A}\in{\mathrm{CKV}}\,,

along any
u=
constant null hypersurfaces with the gauge choice
\delta\beta=0
. Whenever useful we shall denote the dependence of

\overset{[1]}{Q}
on
x\in\delta\Psi_{\mathrm{Bo}}[\mathbf{S},k]
as
\overset{[1]}{Q}[x]
.
Two data sets
x_{r_{1}}\in\delta\Psi_{\mathrm{Bo}}[\mathbf{S}_{1},k]
and
x_{r_{2}}\in\delta\Psi_{\mathrm{Bo}}[\mathbf{S}_{2},k]
can only be glued-up-to-gauge if one can find gauge transformations
z_{r_{1}}
and
z_{r_{2}}
such that

\overset{[1]}{Q}[z_{r_{1}}^{*}(x_{r_{1}})](\pi^{A})=\overset{[1]}{Q}[z_{r_{2}}^{*}(x_{r_{2}})](\pi^{A})\,.

(3.56)

Under gauge transformations
\overset{(*)}{H}_{uA}
transforms as

\displaystyle\overset{(*)}{H}_{uA}\rightarrow\overset{(*)}{H}_{uA}+2{\mathring{D}}^{B}\big(r^{n-2}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\xi_{u}]-r^{n-1}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi_{B}]\big)-2(r^{n-2}{\operatorname{L_{1}}}(\xi_{u})+nm{\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi_{u})\,;

(3.57)

recall that
{\operatorname{L_{1}}}
has been defined in (
3.32
).
Upon projection onto
{\mathrm{CKV}}
, only the last term on the right-hand side of (
3.57
) survives and thus
\overset{[1]}{Q}
transforms as

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbf{S}}\pi^{A}\overset{(*)}{H}_{uA}\,d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}\to

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbf{S}}\big(\pi^{A}\overset{(*)}{H}_{uA}+{2mn{\mathring{D}}_{A}\pi^{A}\xi^{u}}\big)\,d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}\,.

(3.58)

(Either by an explicit calculation, or by general considerations, the last two formulae remain the same in the gauge
\delta\beta=0
when using gauge transformations preserving this gauge.)

If
m=0
we see that
\overset{[1]}{Q}
is gauge invariant, hence

\overset{[1]}{Q}[\tilde{x}_{r_{1}}]=\overset{[1]}{Q}[\tilde{x}_{r_{2}}]\quad\iff\quad\overset{[1]}{Q}[x_{r_{1}}]=\overset{[1]}{Q}[x_{r_{2}}]\,,

(3.59)

and the equality on the left in (
3.59
) has to hold in this case to achieve gluing-up-to-gauge. We will call gauge-invariant radial charges “
obstructions to gluing-up-to-gauge
”.

However, if
m\neq 0
, the gauge field
\xi^{u}
can be used to overcome the obstruction associated to (
3.56
) when
{\mathring{D}}_{A}\pi^{A}
is non-vanishing (see Section
5
below). This is possible only when
\mathbf{S}
is the round sphere and
\pi^{A}
is a proper conformal Killing vector of
S^{n-1}
. In all other topologies, the radial charges
\overset{[1]}{Q}(\pi^{A})
are gauge-invariant independently of whether or not the mass parameter
m
vanishes.

Next, we can rewrite (
3.49
) with
\delta\beta=0
as a transport equation involving the field
h_{uA}
:

\displaystyle\partial_{r}(r^{n+1}\partial_{r}\check{h}_{uA})=(n+1)r^{n}\partial_{r}\check{h}_{uA}+r^{n+1}\partial^{2}_{r}\check{h}_{uA}=r^{n-1}\partial_{r}(r^{-2}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB})

\displaystyle\implies\partial_{r}\bigg(\underbrace{n\check{h}_{uA}+r\partial_{r}\check{h}_{uA}-\frac{1}{r^{3}}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}}_{=:\overset{(0)}{H}_{uA}}\bigg)=\frac{1}{r^{4}}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}\,.

(3.60)

Integrating this equation gives

\displaystyle\overset{(0)}{H}_{uA}(r,\cdot)=\overset{(0)}{H}_{uA}(r_{1},\cdot)+\int_{r_{1}}^{r}\frac{1}{s^{4}}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}\ ds\,.

(3.61)

Under gauge transformations
preserving
\delta\beta=0

we have

\displaystyle\overset{(0)}{H}_{uA}\rightarrow

\displaystyle\overset{(0)}{H}_{uA}-\frac{2}{r}{\mathring{D}}^{B}C(\xi)_{AB}+n\partial_{u}\xi_{A}{-\frac{n-2}{r^{2}}}{\operatorname{L_{1}}}(\xi^{u})_{A}+\alpha^{2}n{\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi^{u}\,.

(3.62)

Remark
3.1
.

For the purpose of Section
5
we will need to analyse the regularity of the fields resulting from our construction. In the linearised case there are many ways to obtain a consistent setup, using Hölder spaces, or Sobolev spaces, possibly with a non-integer regularity index. Strong constraints arise from the requirement of compatibility with the nonlinear analysis, carried out in the accompanying paper
ChCongGray2
. For simplicity and definiteness, here we will only consider
L^{2}
-based Sobolev spaces, which are natural for the evolution problem. The diligent reader will note that all the results below apply in functional spaces where the Agmon-Douglis-Nirenberg type estimates are available, in particular in Hölder spaces, or in
L^{p}
-based Sobolev spaces with
p\in(1,\infty)
.

Regularity.

Let
H^{\ell}(\mathbf{S})
denote the Hilbert space of tensor fields whose derivatives of order less than or equal to
\ell
are in
L^{2}(\mathbf{S})
.

Let
k_{\gamma}\in\mathbb{N}
. A tensor field
A
will be said to be in
H^{k_{\gamma};+}_{{{\mycal N}}}
if
it holds that

A|_{r=r_{1}}\in H^{k_{\gamma}}(\mathbf{S})\ \mbox{and}\ \partial_{r}A\in H^{k_{\gamma}}([r_{1},r_{2}]\times\mathbf{S})\,.

(3.63)

One easily checks that

\forall\ j\in\mathbbm{Z}\cap[1,k_{\gamma}]\qquad\partial^{j}_{r}H^{k_{\gamma};+}_{{{\mycal N}}}\subset H^{k_{\gamma}-j;+}_{{{\mycal N}}}\,,

(3.64)

and

\forall\ j\in\mathbbm{Z}\cap[0,k_{\gamma}]\,,\ r\in[r_{1},r_{2}]\qquad\partial_{r}^{j}A(r,\cdot)\in H^{k_{\gamma}-j}(\mathbf{S})\,.

(3.65)

Indeed, (
3.65
) with
j=0
is obtained by integration in
r
(cf.
(
ChCongGray2
, Proposition 3.2)
).
When
j=1
we have

\partial_{r}A(r_{1},\cdot)\in H^{k_{\gamma}-1/2}(\mathbf{S})\subset H^{k_{\gamma}-1}(\mathbf{S})\,,

where “
\in
” is obtained from a standard trace theorem (cf., e.g.,
Adams:Sobolev
).
This proves (
3.64
) with
j=1
, and (
3.65
) again by integration. For
j>1
the result is obtained by induction.

We have the trivial observation: for all
s\in\mathbb{R}

A\in H^{k_{\gamma};+}_{{{\mycal N}}}\qquad\Longleftrightarrow\qquad r^{s}A\in H^{k_{\gamma};+}_{{{\mycal N}}}\,.

(3.66)

From (
3.51
) and (
3.61
), we have:

\displaystyle h_{AB}\in H^{k_{\gamma};+}_{{{\mycal N}}}\,,\ h_{uA}|_{r=r_{1}}\in H^{k_{\gamma}-1}({\mathbf{S}})\,,\ \partial_{r}h_{uA}|_{r=r_{1}}\in H^{k_{\gamma}-1}({\mathbf{S}})

(3.67)

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\Big(h_{uA}\in H^{k_{\gamma}-1;+}_{{{\mycal N}}}\ \Longleftrightarrow\ r^{n}\check{h}_{uA}\in H^{k_{\gamma}-1;+}_{{{\mycal N}}}\ \Longleftrightarrow\ \overset{(0)}{H}_{uA}\in H^{k_{\gamma}-1;+}_{{{\mycal N}}}

\displaystyle\phantom{\Longrightarrow\Big(}\Longleftrightarrow\ \overset{(*)}{H}_{uA}\in H^{k_{\gamma}-1;+}_{{{\mycal N}}}\Big)\,.

We also note that the differentiability class
\overset{(0)}{H}_{uA}\in H^{k_{\gamma}-1;+}_{{{\mycal N}}}
is preserved under gauge transformations (
3.62
) if

\xi^{u}\in H^{k_{\gamma}+2}({\mathbf{S}})\,,\ \xi^{A}\in H^{k_{\gamma}+1}({\mathbf{S}})\,,\ \partial_{u}\xi^{A}\in H^{k_{\gamma}-1}({\mathbf{S}})\,.

(3.68)

3.3.3
h_{uu}

To obtain the transport equation for the function
V
occurring in the Bondi form of the metric, it turns out to be convenient to consider the expression for
2G_{ur}+2U^{A}G_{rA}-V/r\,G_{rr}
:

\displaystyle r^{2}e^{-2\beta}(2G_{ur}+2U^{A}G_{rA}-V/r\,G_{rr})=R[\gamma]-2\gamma^{AB}\Big[{D}_{A}{D}_{B}\beta+({D}_{A}\beta)({D}_{B}\beta)\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{e^{-2\beta}}{r^{2(n-2)}}{D}_{A}\Big[\partial_{r}(r^{2(n-1)}U^{A})\Big]-\frac{1}{2}r^{4}e^{-4\beta}\gamma_{AB}(\partial_{r}U^{A})(\partial_{r}U^{B})-\frac{(n-1)}{r^{n-3}}e^{-2\beta}\partial_{r}(r^{n-3}V)\,.

(3.69)

(It follows directly from the definition of
G_{\mu\nu}
and the Bondi parametrisation of the metric that
r^{2}e^{-2\beta}(2G_{ur}+2U^{A}G_{rA}-V/r\,G_{rr})
can equivalently be written as
r^{2}g^{AB}R_{AB}
.)

In vacuum one thus obtains

\displaystyle 2\Lambda r^{2}=R[\gamma]-2\gamma^{AB}\Big[{D}_{A}{D}_{B}\beta+({D}_{A}\beta)({D}_{B}\beta)\Big]+\frac{e^{-2\beta}}{r^{2(n-2)}}{D}_{A}\Big[\partial_{r}(r^{2(n-1)}U^{A})\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{2}r^{4}e^{-4\beta}\gamma_{AB}(\partial_{r}U^{A})(\partial_{r}U^{B})-\frac{(n-1)}{r^{n-3}}e^{-2\beta}\partial_{r}(r^{n-3}V)\,.

(3.70)

Recall that
\mathring{R}_{AB}=(n-2){\varepsilon}{\mathring{\gamma}}_{AB}

denotes the Ricci tensor of the metric
{\mathring{\gamma}}_{AB}
. As
h_{AB}
is
{\mathring{\gamma}}
-traceless we have

\displaystyle r^{2}\delta(R[\gamma])|_{\gamma={\mathring{\gamma}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-{\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}_{A}({\mathring{\gamma}}^{BC}h_{BC})+{\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}-\mathring{R}^{AB}h_{AB}

(3.71)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}\,.

Linearising (
3.70
) around the Birmingham-Kottler background and rearranging terms thus gives

\displaystyle\partial_{r}(r^{n-3}\delta V

\displaystyle-\frac{r^{n-1}}{n-1}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\delta U^{A})=\Big(2r^{n-3}(n-2){{\varepsilon}}-\frac{4\Lambda r^{n-1}}{(n-1)}\Big)\delta\beta

\displaystyle+\frac{r^{n-3}}{(n-1)}\Big\{{\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\check{h}_{AB}-2\gamma^{AB}{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\delta\beta\Big\}+r^{n-2}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\delta U^{A}\,.

(3.72)

We note that since
\delta(G_{ur}+U^{A}G_{rA})=\delta G_{ur}
,
Equation (
3.72
) is equivalent to the equation
2r^{n-1}/(n-1)(\delta G_{ur}-\Lambda h_{ur})=0
.

Let us show that (
3.72
) gives another
family of conserved radial charges:

\displaystyle\overset{[2]}{Q}(\lambda)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbf{S}}\lambda\Big[r^{n-3}\delta V-\frac{r^{n-2}}{n-1}\partial_{r}\big(r^{2}{\mathring{D}}^{A}\delta U_{A}\big)-\tfrac{2r^{n-2}}{n-1}\mathring{\Delta}\delta\beta-2r^{n-3}V\delta\beta\Big]d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}\,,

(3.73)

where the functions
\lambda(x^{A})\in\ker(\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}}^{\dagger})
are solutions of the equation

\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\lambda]=0\,.

(3.74)

(Recall that the operator
\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}}
is defined as:

\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}}:=\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(1)}}\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}.)

(3.75)

The only solutions of (
3.74
) on a compact Einstein manifold are constants, except on a round
S^{n-1}
, in which case such
\lambda
’s are linear combinations of
\ell=0
or
\ell=1
spherical harmonics
(
Kuehnel
, p. 127)
; cf. also
(
BoucettaArxiv
, Section 3)
.

When
\lambda
is a constant the conservation equation
\partial_{r}\overset{[2]}{Q}=0
is essentially straightforward, as several terms both in
\overset{[2]}{Q}
and in
\partial_{r}\overset{[2]}{Q}
integrate out to zero, and using
\partial_{r}\delta\beta=0
(cf. (
3.45
)). For general
\lambda
’s satisfying (
3.74
) the
r
-independent of
\overset{[2]}{Q}
follows
from the following calculation,
where the linearised Einstein equation

\partial_{r}\delta\beta=0
has again been used:

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\bigg[r^{n-3}\delta V

\displaystyle-\frac{r^{n-2}}{n-1}\partial_{r}\bigg(r^{2}{\mathring{D}}^{A}\delta U_{A}\bigg)\bigg]=\partial_{r}(r^{n-3}\delta V)-\partial_{r}\bigg(\frac{r^{n-2}}{n-1}\partial_{r}\bigg(r^{2}{\mathring{D}}^{A}\delta U_{A}\bigg)\bigg)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\partial_{r}(r^{n-3}\delta V)-2r^{n-2}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\delta U^{A}-\frac{r^{n-1}}{n-1}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\partial_{r}\delta U^{A}}_{\tfrac{r^{n-3}}{(n-1)}\Big({\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\check{h}_{AB}-2\mathring{\Delta}\delta\beta\Big)+\Big(2r^{n-3}(n-2){{\varepsilon}}-\frac{4\Lambda r^{n-1}}{(n-1)}\Big)\delta\beta\text{ by \eqref{27III2022.3b}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\underbrace{-\frac{1}{n-1}(r^{n}\partial_{r}^{2}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\delta U^{A}+(n+1)r^{n-1}\partial_{r}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\delta U^{A})}_{\tfrac{1}{(n-1)r}\partial_{r}\big(2r^{n-1}\mathring{\Delta}\delta\beta\big)+\tfrac{r^{n-2}}{n-1}\partial_{r}\big({\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\check{h}_{AB}\big)\text{ by \eqref{24VII22.1c} }}

\displaystyle={\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\bigg[\frac{r^{n-3}}{n-1}\check{h}_{AB}+\tfrac{r^{n-2}}{n-1}\partial_{r}\check{h}_{AB}\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad+\partial_{r}\bigg[\bigg(\tfrac{2r^{n-2}}{n-1}\left((n-1)\epsilon+\mathring{\Delta}\right)-\frac{4\Lambda r^{n}}{(n-1)n}\bigg)\delta\beta\bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n-1}{\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\big[\partial_{r}(r^{n-2}\check{h}_{AB})-(n-3)r^{n-3}\check{h}_{AB}\big]

\displaystyle\quad+\partial_{r}\bigg[\bigg(\tfrac{2r^{n-2}}{n-1}\left((n-1)\epsilon+\mathring{\Delta}\right)-\frac{4\Lambda r^{n}}{(n-1)n}\bigg)\delta\beta\bigg]\,.

(3.76)

Collecting some
r
-derivatives on one side, we find

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\Big[r^{n-3}\delta V-\frac{r^{n-2}}{n-1}\partial_{r}\Big(

\displaystyle r^{2}{\mathring{D}}^{A}\delta U_{A}\bigg)-\bigg(\tfrac{2r^{n-2}}{n-1}\big((n-1)\epsilon+\mathring{\Delta}\big)-\frac{4\Lambda r^{n}}{(n-1)n}\Big)\delta\beta\Big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n-1}{\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\big[\partial_{r}(r^{n-2}\check{h}_{AB})-(n-3)r^{n-3}\check{h}_{AB}\big]\,.

(3.77)

Adding
4\partial_{r}(m\ \delta\beta)=0
to the left-hand side allows one to rewrite this in a more condensed and, as we will see shortly, gauge-invariant form:

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\Big[r^{n-3}\delta V-\frac{r^{n-2}}{n-1}\partial_{r}\big(

\displaystyle r^{2}{\mathring{D}}^{A}\delta U_{A}\big)-\tfrac{2r^{n-2}}{n-1}\mathring{\Delta}\delta\beta-2r^{n-3}V\delta\beta\Big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n-1}{\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\big[\partial_{r}(r^{n-2}\check{h}_{AB})-(n-3)r^{n-3}\check{h}_{AB}\big]\,.

(3.78)

Hence

\partial_{r}\overset{[2]}{Q}=\frac{1}{n-1}\int_{\mathbf{S}}\lambda\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}}\big[\partial_{r}(r^{n-1}\check{h}_{AB})-(n-3)r^{n-3}\check{h}_{AB}\big]\,d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}=0\,,

(3.79)

and the condition, for
x_{r_{1}}\in\delta\Psi_{\mathrm{Bo}}[\mathbf{S}_{1},k]
and for
x_{r_{2}}\in\delta\Psi_{\mathrm{Bo}}[\mathbf{S}_{2},k]

\overset{[2]}{Q}[x_{r_{1}}]=\overset{[2]}{Q}[x_{r_{2}}]

(3.80)

provides another family of obstructions to linearised characteristic gluing.

In the gauge
\delta\beta=0
,
and under gauge transformations preserving this, it holds that

\displaystyle\overset{[2]}{Q}[x](\lambda)

\displaystyle\to\int_{\mathbf{S}}\lambda\Bigg[r^{n-3}\delta V+2r^{n-2}\left({\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{n-1}\mathring{\Delta}\right)\left(\frac{1}{n-1}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\xi^{B}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{2r^{n-1}}{n-1}({\mathring{D}}_{B}\partial_{u}\xi^{B}+\left(\alpha^{2}{-2m(n-2)r^{-n}}\right)\mathring{\Delta}\xi^{u}){+nmr^{n-3}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\xi^{B}})

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{r^{n-2}}{n-1}\bigg({\mathring{D}}^{A}\partial_{r}h_{uA}+2r({\mathring{D}}_{B}\partial_{u}\xi^{B}+\left(\alpha^{2}{-2m(n-2)r^{-n}}\right)\mathring{\Delta}\xi^{u})\bigg)\Bigg]\,d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\overset{[2]}{Q}[x](\lambda)+\int_{\mathbf{S}}\lambda\bigg[2r^{n-2}\left({\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{n-1}\mathring{\Delta}{-\frac{nm}{r^{n-2}}}\right)\left(\frac{1}{n-1}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\xi^{B}\right)\bigg]\,d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}\,.

(3.81)

It can be verified that in the general Bondi gauge with
\delta\beta\neq 0
, the gauge transformation of
\overset{[2]}{Q}
continues to be given by (
3.81
).

Taking
{\mathring{D}}^{A}
of (
3.74
) gives,

\bigg({1}-\frac{1}{n-1}\bigg){\mathring{D}}_{B}\mathring{\Delta}\lambda=-\mathring{R}_{AB}{\mathring{D}}^{A}\lambda\equiv-(n-2){{\varepsilon}}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\lambda\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\frac{1}{n-1}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\mathring{\Delta}\lambda=-{{\varepsilon}}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\lambda\,.

(3.82)

This shows that
when
m=0
, or when
(\mathbf{S},{\mathring{\gamma}}
) is
not
the round sphere, the second integral in (
3.81
) vanishes and hence
\overset{[2]}{Q}{}
is gauge invariant.
When
m\neq 0
,
on a round sphere the radial charges
\overset{[2]}{Q}{(\lambda^{[=1]})}{}
can be gauged-away using
({\mathring{D}}_{B}\xi^{B})^{[=1]}
.

Equation (
3.76
) in the gauge
\delta\beta=0
can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\bigg[r^{n-3}\delta V-\frac{r^{n-2}}{n-1}\partial_{r}\bigg(r^{2}{\mathring{D}}^{A}\delta U_{A}\bigg)-\frac{r^{n-2}}{n-1}{\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\check{h}_{AB}\bigg]

\displaystyle=-\frac{(n-3)r^{n-5}}{n-1}\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}}(h_{AB})\,,

(3.83)

which gives a representation formula for
\delta V
after integration.

From (
3.72
) we have, setting
\delta\beta=0
,

\displaystyle-\frac{r^{n}}{n-1}\partial_{r}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\delta U^{A}

\displaystyle=-r\partial_{r}(r^{n-3}\delta V)+2r^{n-1}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\delta U^{A}+\frac{r^{n-2}}{n-1}{\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\check{h}_{AB}\,.

(3.84)

We use this to rewrite the term in the square brackets of (
3.83
), in the gauge
\delta\beta=0
, as

\displaystyle-\chi

\displaystyle:=r^{n-3}\delta V-\frac{r^{n-2}}{n-1}\partial_{r}\bigg(r^{2}{\mathring{D}}^{A}\delta U_{A}\bigg)-\frac{r^{n-2}}{n-1}{\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\check{h}_{AB}

\displaystyle=\frac{2(n-2)r^{n-1}}{n-1}{\mathring{D}}^{A}\delta U_{A}-r^{2}\partial_{r}(r^{n-4}\delta V)=-\frac{2(n-2)r^{n-3}}{n-1}{\mathring{D}}^{A}h_{uA}+r^{2}\partial_{r}(r^{n-3}h_{uu})\,.

(3.85)

Under gauge transformations preserving
\delta\beta=0
, the function
\chi
transforms as

\displaystyle\chi

\displaystyle\rightarrow\chi+\frac{2r^{n-2}(n-3)}{n-1}\bigg(\frac{1}{n-1}\mathring{\Delta}+{\varepsilon}\bigg){\mathring{D}}_{B}\xi^{B}{+}\frac{2nm}{n-1}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\xi^{B}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{2r^{n-3}(n-2)}{(n-1)^{2}}\mathring{\Delta}(\mathring{\Delta}+(n-1){\varepsilon})\xi^{u}\,.

(3.86)

Regularity.

It holds that

\displaystyle\delta V|_{r=r_{1}}\in H^{k_{\gamma}-2}({\mathbf{S}})\,,

\displaystyle h_{AB}\in H^{k_{\gamma};+}_{{{\mycal N}}}\,,\ h_{uA}\in H^{k_{\gamma}-1;+}_{{{\mycal N}}}

(3.87)

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\quad\Big(\delta V\in H^{k_{\gamma}-2;+}_{{{\mycal N}}}\ \Longleftrightarrow\ \chi\in H^{k_{\gamma}-2;+}_{{{\mycal N}}}\Big)\,.

The differentiability class
\chi\in H^{k_{\gamma}-2;+}_{{{\mycal N}}}
is preserved under gauge transformations (
3.3.3
) if

\xi^{u}\in H^{k_{\gamma}+2}({\mathbf{S}})\,,\ \xi^{A}\in H^{k_{\gamma}+1}({\mathbf{S}})\,,

(3.88)

consistently with (
3.68
).

3.3.4
\partial_{u}h_{AB}

We continue with the equation involving
\partial_{u}h_{AB}
:

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}\Big[e^{2\beta}R[\gamma]_{AB}+r^{(5-n)/2}\partial_{r}[r^{\frac{n-1}{2}}(\partial_{u}\gamma_{AB})]-\frac{1}{2}r^{3-n}\partial_{r}[r^{n-2}V(\partial_{r}\gamma_{AB})]

(3.89)

\displaystyle-2e^{\beta}{D}_{A}{D}_{B}e^{\beta}+\frac{1}{r^{n-3}}\gamma_{CA}{D}_{B}[\partial_{r}(r^{n-1}U^{C})]-\frac{1}{2}r^{4}e^{-2\beta}\gamma_{AC}\gamma_{BD}(\partial_{r}U^{C})(\partial_{r}U^{D})

\displaystyle+\frac{r^{2}}{2}(\partial_{r}\gamma_{AB})({D}_{C}U^{C})+r^{2}U^{C}{D}_{C}(\partial_{r}\gamma_{AB})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}rV\gamma^{CD}\partial_{r}\gamma_{AC}\partial_{r}\gamma_{BD}-\frac{1}{2}r^{2}\gamma^{CD}(\partial_{r}\gamma_{BD}\partial_{u}\gamma_{AC}+\partial_{u}\gamma_{BD}\partial_{r}\gamma_{AC})

\displaystyle-r^{2}(\partial_{r}\gamma_{AC})\gamma_{BE}({D}^{C}U^{E}-{D}^{E}U^{C})+\Lambda e^{2\beta}g_{AB}-8\pi e^{2\beta}T_{AB}\Big]=0.

It is convenient to rewrite this equation as

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\Big[r^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\partial_{u}\gamma_{AB}-\frac{1}{2}r^{\frac{n-3}{2}}V\partial_{r}\gamma_{AB}-\frac{n-1}{4}r^{\frac{n-5}{2}}V\gamma_{AB}\Big]

(3.90)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{n-1}{4}\partial_{r}(r^{\frac{n-5}{2}}V)\gamma_{AB}-\frac{1}{2}r^{\frac{n-3}{2}}V\gamma^{CD}\partial_{r}\gamma_{AC}\partial_{r}\gamma_{BD}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}r^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\gamma^{CD}(\partial_{r}\gamma_{BD}\partial_{u}\gamma_{AC}+\partial_{u}\gamma_{BD}\partial_{r}\gamma_{AC})

\displaystyle-r^{\frac{n-5}{2}}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}\Big[e^{2\beta}R[\gamma]_{AB}-2e^{\beta}{D}_{A}{D}_{B}e^{\beta}

\displaystyle+r^{3-n}\gamma_{CA}{D}_{B}[\partial_{r}(r^{n-1}U^{C})]-\frac{1}{2}r^{4}e^{-2\beta}\gamma_{AC}\gamma_{BD}(\partial_{r}U^{C})(\partial_{r}U^{D})

\displaystyle+\frac{r^{2}}{2}(\partial_{r}\gamma_{AB})({D}_{C}U^{C})+r^{2}U^{C}{D}_{C}(\partial_{r}\gamma_{AB})

\displaystyle-r^{2}(\partial_{r}\gamma_{AC})\gamma_{BE}({D}^{C}U^{E}-{D}^{E}U^{C})-8\pi e^{2\beta}T_{AB}\Big]\,.

Let us define the operators
P
and
\mathring{\mathscr{R}}
acting on two-covariant tensors as

P(h)_{AB}:=\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}^{C}h_{BC}]\,,\quad\mathring{\mathscr{R}}(h)_{AB}:=\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[\mathring{R}_{A}{}^{C}{}_{B}{}^{D}h_{CD}]\,,

(3.91)

where
\mathring{R}_{ABCD}
is the curvature tensor of
{\mathring{\gamma}}
.
We note that for any tensor
X_{AB}
we have

\mathring{\mathscr{R}}(X)_{AB}=\mathring{\mathscr{R}}\big(\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}(X)\big){}_{AB}\,.

(3.92)

If
h
is symmetric and trace-free it holds that

\mathring{\mathscr{R}}(h)_{AB}=\mathring{R}_{A}{}^{C}{}_{B}{}^{D}h_{CD}\,,

(3.93)

and if moreover
{\mathring{\gamma}}
is a space form we have

\mathring{\mathscr{R}}(h)_{AB}=-{{\varepsilon}}h_{AB}\,.

(3.94)

Denoting by
\mathring{R}
the Ricci scalar of
{\mathring{\gamma}}
,
we find

\displaystyle\delta\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[R[\gamma]_{AB}]

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[\delta R[\gamma]_{AB}]-\frac{1}{n-1}\mathring{R}h_{AB}

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[-\frac{1}{2}\mathring{\Delta}h_{AB}+{\mathring{D}}^{C}{\mathring{D}}_{A}h_{BC}]-(n-2){{\varepsilon}}h_{AB}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\mathring{\Delta}h_{AB}+P(h)_{AB}-\mathring{\mathscr{R}}(h)_{AB}\,.

(3.95)

Thus
the linearisation of (
3.90
) around a Birmingham-Kottler background, in vacuum, reads,

\displaystyle 0=r^{\frac{n-5}{2}}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[\delta G_{AB}]

(3.96)

\displaystyle=\partial_{r}\Big[r^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\partial_{u}\check{h}_{AB}-\frac{r^{\frac{n-3}{2}}}{2}V\partial_{r}\check{h}_{AB}-\frac{n-1}{4}r^{\frac{n-5}{2}}V\check{h}_{AB}-{r^{\frac{n-1}{2}}}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}\big[{\mathring{D}}_{A}\check{h}_{uB}]\Big]

\displaystyle+\frac{n-1}{4}\partial_{r}(r^{\frac{n-5}{2}}V)\check{h}_{AB}-r^{\frac{n-5}{2}}\Big(2{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\delta\beta+\frac{n-1}{2}r\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}\big[{\mathring{D}}_{A}\check{h}_{uB})\big]\Big)

\displaystyle+r^{(n-9)/2}(-\frac{1}{2}\mathring{\Delta}h_{AB}+P(h)_{AB}-\mathring{\mathscr{R}}(h)_{AB})\,.\phantom{xxx}

To obtain a transport equation involving
\partial_{u}h_{AB}
, with source terms depending only on the field
h_{AB}
, we take
\frac{2}{(n+1)r^{(n+1)/2}}\times C
of
(
3.49
) with
\delta\beta=0
, giving

\displaystyle\frac{2}{(n+1)r^{(n+1)/2}}\partial_{r}\left[r^{n+1}\partial_{r}(r^{-2}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{B}h_{uA}])\right]-\frac{2}{n+1}r^{\frac{n-3}{2}}\partial_{r}(r^{-2}P(h)_{AB})

\displaystyle=0\,.

(3.97)

Subtracting (
3.97
) from (
3.96
) with
\delta\beta=0
leads to the desired equation:

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\Big[r^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\partial_{u}\check{h}_{AB}-\frac{r^{\frac{n-3}{2}}}{2}V\partial_{r}\check{h}_{AB}-\frac{n-1}{4}r^{\frac{n-5}{2}}V\check{h}_{AB}-\frac{2}{(n+1)r^{(n+1)/2}}\partial_{r}(r^{n-1}TS\big[{\mathring{D}}_{A}h_{uB}])\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad=-\frac{n-1}{4}\partial_{r}(r^{\frac{n-5}{2}}V)\check{h}_{AB}-\frac{2}{n+1}r^{\frac{n-3}{2}}\partial_{r}(r^{-2}P(h)_{AB})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-r^{(n-9)/2}(-\frac{1}{2}\mathring{\Delta}h_{AB}+P(h)_{AB}-\mathring{\mathscr{R}}(h)_{AB})\,.

(3.98)

Setting

\displaystyle q_{AB}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle r^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\partial_{u}\check{h}_{AB}-\frac{r^{\frac{n-3}{2}}}{2}V\partial_{r}\check{h}_{AB}-\frac{n-1}{4}r^{\frac{n-5}{2}}V\check{h}_{AB}

(3.99)

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{(n+1)r^{(n+1)/2}}\partial_{r}(r^{n-1}TS\big[{\mathring{D}}_{A}h_{uB}])+\frac{2r^{\frac{n-3}{2}}}{n+1}P(\check{h})_{AB}\,,

Equation (
3.98
)
can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\partial_{r}q_{AB}=

\displaystyle-\frac{n-1}{4}\partial_{r}(r^{\frac{n-5}{2}}V)\check{h}_{AB}+\frac{n-3}{n+1}r^{(n-9)/2}P(h)_{AB}

\displaystyle-r^{(n-9)/2}\big(-\frac{1}{2}\mathring{\Delta}h_{AB}+P(h)_{AB}-\mathring{\mathscr{R}}(h)_{AB}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \frac{1}{8}\left(n^{2}-1\right)\alpha^{2}r^{\frac{n-5}{2}}h_{AB}-\frac{(n-1)^{2}}{4}mr^{-(n+5)/2}h_{AB}

\displaystyle-r^{(n-9)/2}\Big[\frac{4}{n+1}P(h)_{AB}-\mathring{\mathscr{R}}(h)_{AB}-\big(\frac{1}{2}\mathring{\Delta}-\frac{(n-3)(n-1){{\varepsilon}}}{8}\big)h_{AB}\Big]\,.

(3.100)

Under a gauge transformation
preserving the gauge condition
\delta\beta=0
,
the field
q_{AB}
transforms as

\displaystyle q_{AB}\mapsto\

\displaystyle q_{AB}

\displaystyle-r^{\frac{n-3}{2}}\left(\frac{2}{n-1}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}{\mathring{D}}_{C}\xi^{C}]-\left(\frac{4}{n+1}P-\frac{n-1}{2}({\varepsilon}-r^{2}(\alpha^{2}+\ {2mr^{-n}})\right)C(\xi)_{AB}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{r^{\frac{n-5}{2}}}{2}\left((n-3){\varepsilon}-(n+1)r^{2}\alpha^{2}\ {-\frac{(n-1)^{2}}{{n+1}}2mr^{-n+2}}-\frac{4(n-3)}{(n-2)(n+1)}P\right)\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\xi^{u}]\,.

(3.101)

Regularity.

Equation (
3.100
) shows that

\displaystyle q_{AB}|_{r=r_{1}}\in H^{k_{\gamma}-2}({\mathbf{S}})\,,\ h_{AB}\in H^{k_{\gamma};+}_{{{\mycal N}}}\,,\ h_{uA}\in H^{k_{\gamma}-1;+}_{{{\mycal N}}}

(3.102)

\displaystyle\phantom{xxxxxxxxxxxx}\Longrightarrow\quad\Big(q_{AB}\in H^{k_{\gamma}-2;+}_{{{\mycal N}}}\ \Longleftrightarrow\ \partial_{u}h_{AB}\in H^{k_{\gamma}-2;+}_{{{\mycal N}}}\Big)\,.

The differentiability class
q_{AB}\in H^{k_{\gamma}-2;+}_{{{\mycal N}}}
is preserved under gauge transformations (
3.101
) if

\xi^{u}\in H^{k_{\gamma}+2}({\mathbf{S}})\,,\ \xi^{A}\in H^{k_{\gamma}+1}({\mathbf{S}})\,,

(3.103)

again consistently with (
3.68
).

3.4
The remaining Einstein equations

In order to appreciate what follows the reader is invited to consult the argument involving the Bianchi identities presented at the beginning of Section 3.5 of
ChCong1
. The reasoning presented there applies as is in all dimensions, with irrelevant dimension-dependent changes in the equations, and therefore will not be repeated here.

3.4.1
\partial_{u}\partial_{r}h_{uA}

The set of equations
\mycal E_{uA}=0
can be found in
ChCongGray2
and
is too long to be usefully displayed here.
Its linearisation
\delta\mycal E_{uA}\equiv-\delta\mycal E^{r}{}_{A}+(\epsilon-\alpha^{2}r^{2})\delta\mycal E_{rA}
in vacuum reads

\displaystyle 2\delta\mycal E_{uA}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}\Bigg[\bigg(\frac{V}{r}(n-1)(n-2)+2r(n-2)\partial_{r}\left(\frac{V}{r}\right)+r^{2}\partial^{2}_{r}\left(\frac{V}{r}\right)-R[\gamma]-{\mathring{D}}^{B}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\bigg){h}_{uA}

(3.104)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+{\mathring{D}}^{B}{\mathring{D}}_{A}{h}_{uB}+\partial_{u}{\mathring{D}}^{B}{h}_{AB}+r^{5-n}\partial_{r}(r^{n-4}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\delta V)

\displaystyle\quad\quad-r^{2}\Big(\frac{V}{r^{n-2}}\partial_{r}(r^{n-3}\partial_{r}h_{uA})-r^{2}\partial_{r}\partial_{u}\left(\frac{h_{uA}}{r^{2}}\right)\Big)-2r\left(r\partial_{u}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\delta\beta\right)\Bigg]+2\Lambda h_{uA}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}\bigg[{\mathring{D}}^{B}{\mathring{D}}_{A}{h}_{uB}-{\mathring{D}}^{B}{\mathring{D}}_{B}{h}_{uA}-2(n-2)(r^{2}\alpha^{2}+\frac{2m}{r^{n-2}})h_{uA}+\partial_{u}{\mathring{D}}^{B}{h}_{AB}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+r^{5-n}\partial_{r}(r^{n-4}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\delta V)-r^{2}\Big(\frac{V}{r^{n-2}}\partial_{r}(r^{n-3}\partial_{r}h_{uA})-r^{2}\partial_{r}\partial_{u}\left(\frac{h_{uA}}{r^{2}}\right)\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\ 2r^{2}\partial_{u}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\delta\beta\bigg]\,.

Assuming
\delta G_{rA}=0=\delta\beta
, using the transport equation (
3.49
)
to eliminate
\partial_{r}^{2}\check{h}_{uA}
and
the identity (
3.85
) to eliminate
\partial_{r}h_{uu}
, we can rewrite (
3.104
) as

\displaystyle-r^{n+1}\partial_{r}\partial_{u}\left(\frac{h_{uA}}{r^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=r^{n-3}{\mathring{D}}^{B}{\mathring{D}}_{A}{h}_{uB}-r^{n-3}{\mathring{D}}^{B}{\mathring{D}}_{B}{h}_{uA}-2(n-2)r^{n-1}(\alpha^{2}+{2mr^{-n}})h_{uA}

\displaystyle\quad+r^{n-3}\partial_{u}{\mathring{D}}^{B}{h}_{AB}-r^{2}\partial_{r}(r^{n-3}{\mathring{D}}_{A}{h}_{uu})

\displaystyle\quad-r^{(n-2)}({\varepsilon}-r^{2}(\alpha^{2}+{2mr^{-n}}))\underbrace{((n-3)\partial_{r}h_{uA}+r\partial^{2}_{r}h_{uA})}_{=r{\mathring{D}}^{B}\partial_{r}\check{h}_{AB}+2(n-2)/rh_{uA}}

\displaystyle=r^{n-3}{\mathring{D}}^{B}{\mathring{D}}_{A}{h}_{uB}-r^{n-3}{\mathring{D}}^{B}{\mathring{D}}_{B}{h}_{uA}+r^{n-3}\partial_{u}{\mathring{D}}^{B}{h}_{AB}

\displaystyle\quad-r^{2}\partial_{r}(r^{n-3}{\mathring{D}}_{A}{h}_{uu})-r^{n-1}({\varepsilon}-r^{2}(\alpha^{2}+{2mr^{-n}})){\mathring{D}}^{B}\partial_{r}\check{h}_{AB}

\displaystyle\quad-2(n-2)r^{n-3}{\varepsilon}h_{uA}

\displaystyle=r^{n-3}\bigg({-{2}}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{B}h_{uA}]+\partial_{u}{\mathring{D}}^{B}{h}_{AB}\bigg)+{\mathring{D}}_{A}\chi

\displaystyle\quad-r^{n-1}({\varepsilon}-r^{2}(\alpha^{2}+{2mr^{-n}})){\mathring{D}}^{B}\partial_{r}\check{h}_{AB}\,.

(3.105)

Regularity.

We can use (
3.105
) to determine algebraically
\partial_{r}\partial_{u}h_{uA}|_{\mathbf{S}_{1}}
in terms of the remaining fields. We obtain

\displaystyle h_{AB}\in H^{k_{\gamma};+}_{{{\mycal N}}}\,,\ h_{uA}|_{r=r_{1}}\in H^{k_{\gamma}-1}({\mathbf{S}})\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\partial_{r}\partial_{u}h_{uA}|_{r=r_{1}}\in H^{k_{\gamma}-3}(\mathbf{S})\,.

(3.106)

3.4.2
\partial_{u}h_{uu}

The equation
\mycal E_{uu}=0
, to be found in
ChCongGray2
, is likewise too long to be usefully displayed here.
Its linearised version is shorter and reads

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\delta\mycal E_{uu}

(3.107)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}\bigg[2\partial_{u}{\mathring{D}}^{A}{h}_{uA}+\partial_{r}\left(\frac{V}{r}\right){\mathring{D}}^{A}{h}_{uA}-\frac{2V}{r^{n-1}}\partial_{r}\bigg(r^{n-2}{\mathring{D}}^{A}{h}_{uA}\bigg)+\frac{V}{r^{3}}\bigg({\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}-\mathring{R}^{AB}\bigg)h_{AB}

\displaystyle+\left(r(n-1)(\partial_{u}-\partial_{r}\left(\frac{V}{r}\right))+\mathring{R}+{\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\right)\frac{\delta V}{r}-\frac{(n-1)V}{r^{2n-4}}\partial_{r}(r^{2n-5}\delta V)

\displaystyle-\frac{2V}{r}\big({\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}_{A}-(n-1)({\varepsilon}(n-2)+V\partial_{r}-r\partial_{u})\delta\beta\bigg]+2\Lambda h_{uu}\,.

This must be satisfied by all
x_{r_{1}}\in\delta\Psi_{\mathrm{Bo}}[\mathbf{S}_{1},k]
and
x_{r_{2}}\in\delta\Psi_{\mathrm{Bo}}[\mathbf{S}_{2},k]
when the linearised vacuum Einstein equations hold,
and allows us to determine in particular
\partial_{u}V
at
r=r_{1}
in terms of the remaining fields there.

4
Further
u
-derivatives

Assuming now that the Einstein equations

\mycal E_{rr}=0
,
\mycal E_{rA}=0
, and
\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[\mycal E_{AB}]=0

(4.1)

are satisfied, we can obtain transport equations involving
\partial_{u}^{i}h_{uA}
and
\partial_{u}^{i+1}h_{AB}
for
i\geq 1
by combining suitably the transport equation (
3.60
) involving
h_{uA}
with (
3.100
) involving
\partial_{u}h_{AB}
and with higher
u
-derivatives of these equations. We present the transport equations in this section; a detailed derivation can be found in Appendix
A
. In this section, we assume the gauge
\delta\beta=0
.

4.1
Transport equation involving
\partial_{u}^{i}h_{uA}

Recall that in the gauge
\delta\beta=0
(cf. (
3.60
))

\displaystyle\overset{(0)}{H}_{uA}=n\check{h}_{uA}+r\partial_{r}\check{h}_{uA}-\frac{1}{r^{3}}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}\,,\ \ \mbox{with}\ \

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\overset{(0)}{H}_{uA}=\frac{1}{r^{4}}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}\,.

(4.2)

Assuming (
4.1
), for all
i\geq 1
the equations
\partial_{u}^{i}\mycal E_{rA}=0
are equivalent
to the transport equations

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\overset{(i)}{H}_{uA}

\displaystyle={\mathring{D}}^{B}\overset{(i)}{\chi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,r^{-(i+4)}h_{AB}+m^{i}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\overset{(i)}{\chi}_{[m]}r^{-(4+i(n-1))}h_{AB}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j,\ell}^{i_{*}}m^{j}\alpha^{2\ell}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\overset{(i)}{\chi}_{j,\ell}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,r^{-(i+4)-j(n-2)+2\ell}h_{AB}\,,

(4.3)

where
\sum_{j,\ell}^{i_{*}}
denotes the sum over

j,\ell\in\mathbb{N}
satisfying

1\leq j\leq i-1\,,\ j+\ell\leq i\,,\ \text{and }0\leq 2\ell\leq i+j(n-2)\,.

(4.4)

When
\alpha=0
, this sum reduces to

\sum_{j,\ell}^{i_{*}}\Big|_{\alpha=0}=\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}\,.

(4.5)

We also have

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\chi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)

\displaystyle=\prod_{j=1}^{i}\mathcal{K}\left(-(j+3),\mathring{\Delta},P\right)\,,\quad\overset{(0)}{\chi}(\mathring{\Delta},P):=1\,,

(4.6)

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\chi}_{[m]}

\displaystyle=\prod_{j=1}^{i}\mathcal{K}_{[m]}(-(4+(j-1)(n-1)))\,,\quad\overset{(0)}{\chi}_{[m]}:=0\,,

(4.7)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}\left(k,\mathring{\Delta},P\right)

\displaystyle:=-\frac{1}{7-n+2k}\bigg[\frac{2(n-1)P}{(3+k)(3-n+k)}+2\mathring{\mathscr{R}}+\mathring{\Delta}-(n-4-k)(2+k){{\varepsilon}}\bigg]\,,

(4.8)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{[m]}(k)

\displaystyle:=\frac{2(4-n+k)^{2}}{7-n+2k}\,;

(4.9)

with

\framebox{$k\in\mathbb{Z}$ satisfying $k\not\in\{-3,n-3,\frac{n-7}{2}\}$}

(4.10)

(in fact the numbers
k=-3
or
k=n-3
do not occur in (
4.6
)-(
4.7
), but these values can occur in (
4.21
) below). Thus the operators
\overset{(i)}{\chi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)
, respectively
\overset{(i)}{\chi}_{j,\ell}(\mathring{\Delta},P)
, are polynomials in
\mathring{\Delta}
and
P
of order
i
, respectively
i-j-\ell\leq i-1
.
Next, the fields
\overset{(i)}{H}_{uA}
take the form

\overset{(i)}{H}_{uA}=n\partial^{i}_{u}\check{h}_{uA}+r\partial_{r}\partial_{u}^{i}\check{h}_{uA}\,+
terms which depend on

(r,\partial_{u}^{j-1}h_{uA},\partial_{r}\partial_{u}^{j-1}h_{uA},h_{AB},\partial^{j}_{u}h_{AB})_{j=1}^{i}
,
and are defined recursively by the equations

\displaystyle\overset{(1)}{H}_{uA}

\displaystyle=\partial_{u}\overset{(0)}{H}_{uA}-{\mathring{D}}^{B}\hat{q}^{(-4)}_{AB}\,,

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{H}_{uA}

\displaystyle=\partial_{u}\overset{(i-1)}{H}_{uA}-{\mathring{D}}^{B}\overset{(i-1)}{\chi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\hat{q}^{(-i-3)}_{AB}-m^{i-1}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\overset{(i-1)}{\chi}_{\!\!\![m]\,}\hat{q}^{-(4+(i-1)(n-1))}_{AB}

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{j,\ell}^{(i-1)_{*}}m^{j}\alpha^{2\ell}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\overset{(i-1)}{\chi}_{j,\ell}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\hat{q}^{-(i+3)-j(n-2)+2\ell}_{AB}

\displaystyle\quad-\alpha^{2}\mathcal{K}_{[\alpha]}(-(i+3))\mathcal{\tilde{K}}\left(-(i+2),\mathring{\Delta},\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}C\right)\overset{(i-2)}{H}_{uA}\,,\quad i\geq 2\,,

(4.11)

where:

1.

the notation
U(\mathring{\Delta},P)\mapsto\tilde{U}(\mathring{\Delta},\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C)
(a tilde over an operator
U
) denotes the replacement in
U
of all appearances of the operator
P:=C\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}
, respectively
\mathring{\mathscr{R}}
, by the operator
\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C
, respectively
1/2(n-2){{\varepsilon}}
;

2.

and where

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{[\alpha]}(k)

\displaystyle:=\frac{(k+4)(n-k-4)}{n-7-2k}\,;

(4.12)

3.

the field
\hat{q}_{AB}^{(k)}
is defined as

\displaystyle\hat{q}_{AB}^{(k)}

\displaystyle:=\ E_{k}r^{k+1}\partial_{u}h_{AB}+G_{k}r^{5-n+k+\frac{n-1}{k+4-n}}\partial_{r}(r^{\frac{n-1}{n-k-4}+n-3}C(h_{uA}))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+B_{k}r^{k-\frac{n-7}{2}}\tilde{q}_{AB}-H_{k}r^{k}P(h)_{AB}+\frac{r^{k}}{2}({\varepsilon}-\alpha^{2}r^{2}-\frac{2m}{r^{n-2}})h_{AB}\,,

(4.13)

\displaystyle\tilde{q}_{AB}

\displaystyle:=\frac{r^{\frac{n-3}{2}}}{2}V\partial_{r}\check{h}_{AB}-\frac{n-1}{4}r^{\frac{n-5}{2}}V\check{h}_{AB}+\frac{2r^{\frac{n-3}{2}}}{n+1}P\check{h}_{AB}\,,

(4.14)

for some non-zero numbers
E_{k}
,
G_{k}
,
B_{k}
and
H_{k}
to be found in (
A.9
), the precise values of which being irrelevant at this stage.

Regularity.

We see that

\displaystyle\text{if }\ h_{AB}\in H^{k_{\gamma};+}_{{{\mycal N}}}\,,\ h_{uA}\in H^{k_{\gamma}-1;+}_{{{\mycal N}}}\,,\ V\in H^{k_{\gamma}-2;+}_{{{\mycal N}}}\,,

then we have the equivalences

(4.15)

\displaystyle\Big(\hat{q}^{(k)}_{AB}\in H^{k_{\gamma}-2;+}_{{{\mycal N}}}\ \Longleftrightarrow\ q_{AB}\in H^{k_{\gamma}-2;+}_{{{\mycal N}}}\ \Longleftrightarrow\ \partial_{u}h_{AB}\in H^{k_{\gamma}-2;+}_{{{\mycal N}}}\Big)\,.

Furthermore

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{H}_{uA}|_{r=r_{1}}\in H^{k_{\gamma}-2i-1}({\mathbf{S}})\,,\ h_{AB}\in H^{k_{\gamma};+}_{{{\mycal N}}}\qquad\Longrightarrow\qquad\overset{(i)}{H}_{uA}\in H^{k_{\gamma}-2i-1;+}_{{{\mycal N}}}\,.

(4.16)

4.2
Transport equation involving
\partial_{u}^{i}h_{AB}

For
1\leq i
the equations
\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[\partial_{u}^{i-1}\mycal E_{AB}]=0
are equivalent
to the transport equations

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\overset{(i)}{q}_{AB}

\displaystyle=\overset{(i)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,r^{(n-7-2i)/2}h_{AB}+\alpha^{2i}\overset{(i)}{\psi}_{[\alpha]}r^{(n-7+2i)/2}h_{AB}+m^{i}\overset{(i)}{\psi}_{[m]}r^{\frac{n-7-2i(n-1)}{2}}h_{AB}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j,\ell}^{i_{**}}m^{j}\alpha^{2\ell}\overset{(i)}{\psi}_{j,\ell}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,r^{\frac{n-7}{2}-i-j(n-2)+2\ell}h_{AB}\,,

(4.17)

where
\sum_{j,\ell}^{i_{**}}
denotes the sum over
j,\ell
with

\begin{cases}1\leq j\leq i-1\,,\ j+\ell\leq i\,,&\mbox{if $n$ is even}\\
1\leq j\leq i-1\,,\ j+\ell\leq i\,,\ \frac{n-7}{2}-i-j(n-2)+2\ell\leq-4,&\mbox{if $n$ is odd.}\end{cases}

(4.18)

When
\alpha=0
, this sum reduces to

\sum_{j,\ell}^{i_{**}}\quad\overset{\alpha=0}{=}\quad\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}\,.

(4.19)

In (
4.17
), the field
\overset{(i)}{q}_{AB}
is of
the form
\overset{(i)}{q}_{AB}=r^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\partial_{u}^{i}\check{h}_{AB}\,+
terms which depend on fields of lower
u
-derivatives, specifically,

(r,\partial^{j-1}_{u}h_{AB},\partial_{u}^{j-1}h_{uA},\partial_{r}\partial_{u}^{j-1}h_{uA})_{j=1}^{i}
; the operators

\overset{(i)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)
and
\overset{(i)}{\psi}_{j,\ell}(\mathring{\Delta},P)
are polynomials in
\mathring{\Delta}
and
P
of orders
i
and
i-j-\ell
respectively;
\overset{(i)}{\psi}_{[\alpha]}
and
\overset{(i)}{\psi}_{[m]}
are constants. For
i\geq 2
these are given by the recursion relations

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{q}_{AB}

\displaystyle=\partial_{u}\overset{(i-1)}{q}_{AB}-\overset{(i-1)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\hat{q}_{AB}^{(n-5-2i)/2}-\alpha^{2(i-1)}\overset{(i-1)}{\psi}_{[\alpha]}\hat{q}_{AB}^{(n-9+2i)/2}

\displaystyle\quad-m^{i-1}\overset{(i-1)}{\psi}_{[m]}\hat{q}^{(\frac{n-7-2(i-1)(n-1)}{2})}_{AB}-\alpha^{2}\mathcal{\widehat{K}}(2(i-1),P)\overset{(i-2)}{q}_{AB}

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{j,\ell}^{(i-1)_{**}}m^{j}\alpha^{2\ell}\overset{(i-1)}{\psi}_{j,\ell}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\hat{q}^{(\frac{1}{2}(n-7-2(i-1)-2j(n-2)+4\ell))}_{AB}\,,

(4.20)

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)

\displaystyle=\prod_{j=2}^{i}\mathcal{K}\left(\tfrac{n-5-2j}{2},\mathring{\Delta},P\right)\overset{(1)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\,,\quad\overset{(i)}{\psi}_{[\alpha]}=\prod_{j=2}^{i}\mathcal{K}_{[\alpha]}(\tfrac{n-9+2j}{2})\overset{(1)}{\psi}_{[\alpha]}\,,

(4.21)

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\psi}_{[m]}

\displaystyle=\prod_{j=2}^{i}\mathcal{K}_{[m]}(\tfrac{n-7-2(j-1)(n-1)}{2})\overset{(1)}{\psi}_{[m]}\,,

(4.22)

with the initiating functions

\displaystyle\overset{(0)}{q}_{AB}

\displaystyle=0\,,\quad\overset{(1)}{q}_{AB}=q_{AB}\,,

(4.23)

\displaystyle\overset{(1)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)

\displaystyle=-\bigg[\frac{4}{n+1}P-\mathring{\mathscr{R}}-\frac{1}{2}\mathring{\Delta}+\frac{(n-3)(n-1){{\varepsilon}}}{8}\bigg]\,,

(4.24)

\displaystyle\overset{(1)}{\psi}_{[\alpha]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{8}(n^{2}-1)\,,\quad\overset{(1)}{\psi}_{[m]}=-\frac{(n-1)^{2}}{4}\,,

(4.25)

and where

\displaystyle\mathcal{\widehat{K}}(2i,P)

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{K}\left(\tfrac{n-5-2i}{2},\mathring{\Delta},P\right)\mathcal{K}_{[\alpha]}(\tfrac{n-7-2i}{2})\,.

(4.26)

Equations (
4.20
)-(
4.22
) holds for all
i\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
when
n

is even
and until
i=\frac{n-1}{2}
when
n>3

is odd
, with
\hat{q}{}^{(-3)}_{AB}
being given by (
A
) where necessary. The case
n=3
is special and has been analysed in
ChCong1
.

When
n\neq 3

is odd
, at order
i=(n+1)/2
, we have

\displaystyle\overset{(\frac{n+1}{2})}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)=\mathring{\mathcal{K}}\left(-3,\mathring{\Delta},P\right)\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2})}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,,\quad\overset{(\frac{n+1}{2})}{\psi}_{[\alpha]}=0\,,

(4.27)

with the second equation agreeing with the second equation of (
4.21
) after noting that
\mathcal{K}_{[\alpha]}(n-4)=0
. For
i\geq(n+1)/2
, the recursion formula for
\overset{(i)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)
and
\overset{(i)}{\psi}_{[\alpha]}
is as given in (
4.21
) (thus
\overset{(i)}{\psi}_{[\alpha]}=0
), but with
\mathcal{K}\left(-3,\mathring{\Delta},P\right)
replaced by
\mathring{\mathcal{K}}\left(-3,\mathring{\Delta},P\right)
where necessary.
Meanwhile,

\overset{(i)}{q}_{AB}
is given by

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{q}_{AB}

\displaystyle=\partial_{u}\overset{(i-1)}{q}_{AB}-\overset{(i-1)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\hat{q}_{AB}^{(n-5-2i)/2}-m^{i-1}\overset{(i-1)}{\psi}_{[m]}\hat{q}^{(\frac{n-7-2(i-1)(n-1)}{2})}_{AB}

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{j,\ell}^{(i-1)_{**}}m^{j}\alpha^{2\ell}\overset{(i-1)}{\psi}_{j,\ell}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\hat{q}^{(\frac{1}{2}(n-7-2(i-1)-2j(n-2)+4\ell))}_{AB}\,.

(4.28)

The following proposition, which we prove in Appendix
C.5
, p.
C.5
, will play a key role in what follows:

Proposition
4.1
.

We have, for
n\geq 5
odd and
j\geq 0
, and for any vector field
W
and symmetric two-covariant tensor field
h
,

\displaystyle\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2}+j)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,h^{[S]}\equiv 0\,,\quad\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2}+j)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,h^{[V]}\equiv 0\,,

(4.29)

\displaystyle\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2}+j)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\circ\,C(W)\equiv 0\,,

(4.30)

\displaystyle\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2}+j)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\circ P\,(h)\equiv 0\,.

(4.31)

Regularity.

We have

\displaystyle h_{AB}\in H^{k_{\gamma};+}_{{{\mycal N}}}\,,\ \forall\ 0\leq i\leq\ell\ \overset{(i)}{q}_{AB}|_{r=r_{1}}\in H^{k_{\gamma}-2i}({\mathbf{S}})\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\overset{(\ell)}{q}_{AB}\in H^{k_{\gamma}-2\ell;+}_{{{\mycal N}}}\,,

(4.32)

as well as the equivalence

\displaystyle\partial_{u}^{i}h_{AB}\in H^{k_{\gamma};+}_{{{\mycal N}}}\qquad\Longleftrightarrow\qquad\overset{(i)}{q}_{AB}\in H^{k_{\gamma}-2i;+}_{{{\mycal N}}}

(4.33)

whenever all the previous equations hold and

\displaystyle h_{AB}\in H^{k_{\gamma};+}_{{{\mycal N}}}\,,\ h_{uA}\in H^{k_{\gamma}-1;+}_{{{\mycal N}}}\,,\ \delta V\in H^{k_{\gamma}-2;+}_{{{\mycal N}}}\,,\ \text{and }

\displaystyle\partial_{u}^{j}h_{AB}|_{r=r_{1}}\in H^{k_{\gamma}-2j}({\mathbf{S}})\ \text{ for $0\leq j\leq i$}\,.

(4.34)

Radial charges.

We end this section by introducing two further radial charges in the
case
m=0
.
The first arises from taking a combination of the fields
\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{q}_{AB}
and
\chi
. From their respective transport equations (
4.17
), with
i=\frac{n-3}{2}
, and (
3.83
), it can be readily seen that we have,

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\underbrace{\bigg(\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}}\big(\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{q}{}^{[\ker\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{\psi}]})+\alpha^{n-3}\frac{n-1}{n-3}\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{\psi}_{[\alpha]}\chi\bigg)}_{=:\overset{[3]}{Q}}=0\,.

(4.35)

While relatively straightforward, we note that in arriving at (
4.35
), we made use of the
facts
1
1

1

Thus we could have replaced
\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}}\big(\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{q}{}_{AB}^{[\ker\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{\psi}]})
in (
4.35
) by
\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}}\big(\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{q}{}_{AB})
, but we chose to keep the projection to emphasise that the first term on the right-hand side of (
4.17
), with
i=\frac{n-3}{2}
, will not contribute to the right-hand side of (
4.35
).

\displaystyle\Big(\underbrace{h\in(\ker\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{\psi})^{\perp}\Rightarrow h=h^{[V]}+h^{[{\mathrm{TT}}]}}_{\text{cf.\ \eqref{16X.f1m}}}\,,\quad\text{and}\quad

\displaystyle\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}}(h^{[V]})=0=\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}}(h^{[{\mathrm{TT}}]})\Big)

\displaystyle\implies\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}}(h^{[\ker\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{\psi}]})=\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}}(h)\,.

(4.36)

The next radial charge arises from taking a combination of the fields
\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2})}{q}_{AB}
and
\overset{(*)}{H}_{uA}
. From their respective transport equations (
4.17
), with
i=\frac{n-1}{2}
, and (
3.50
),
again
in the case
m=0
, it can be readily seen that we have,

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\underbrace{\bigg({\mathring{D}}^{B}\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2})}{q}{}_{AB}+\frac{\alpha^{n-1}}{n-1}\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2})}{\psi}_{[\alpha]}\overset{(*)}{H}_{uA}\bigg)}_{=:\overset{[4]}{Q}_{A}}=0\,,

(4.37)

where we note that (
4.30
) with
j=0
was used for the vanishing contribution of the first term on the right-hand side of (
4.17
) to the right-hand side of (
4.37
).

4.3
Gauge transformations

The recursion formula (
4.17
) gives another family of radially conserved charges for
i\in\mathbbm{Z}^{+}

when
m=0=\alpha
:

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\int_{\mathbf{S}}\overset{(i)}{\mu}{}^{AB}\overset{(i)}{q}_{AB}d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}=0\,,

(4.38)

where
\overset{(i)}{\mu}
satisfies

\displaystyle 0=\overset{(i)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)^{\dagger}(\overset{(i)}{\mu})\equiv\overset{(i)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)(\overset{(i)}{\mu})\,.

(4.39)

We now move on to determine the gauge dependence of these radial charges. In the rest of this section, unless otherwise indicated, we set
m=0
. Dimension considerations show that the
r
-dependence of gauge fields in the gauge transformation of
\overset{(i)}{q}_{AB}

when
\alpha=0
is determined by terms of the form

\displaystyle r^{\frac{n-3}{2}-i}\xi^{u}\,\quad\text{and}\quad r^{\frac{n-3}{2}-j}\partial_{u}^{i-1-j}\xi^{A}

(4.40)

for
i\geq 1
and
0\leq j\leq i-1
, up to differential operators acting in the
x^{A}
