-variables. However, it follows from the radial conservation of these charges that their gauge transformations must be
r
-independent. Clearly, from (
4.40
), when
n
is even,
no gauge term in the gauge transformation of
\overset{(i)}{q}_{AB}
will contain
r
-independent terms,
whatever
i
. This implies in particular that we must have: when
n
is even, under gauge transformations,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbf{S}}(\overset{(i)}{\mu})^{AB}\overset{(i)}{q}_{AB}d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}\rightarrow\int_{\mathbf{S}}(\overset{(i)}{\mu})^{AB}\overset{(i)}{q}_{AB}d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}\,,

(4.41)

and (
4.38
) gives a family of gauge-invariant radially conserved charges.
The transformation (
4.41
) remains true for
\alpha\neq 0
by a similar argument when
n
is even.

When
n

is odd
, the smallest
i
for which an
r
-independent gauge transformation is possible is
i=\frac{n-3}{2}
. In what follows, we will look at the transformations for
\overset{(i)}{q}{}^{[\ker\overset{(i)}{\psi}]}_{AB}
in the cases
i\in\{\frac{n-3}{2}\,,\frac{n-1}{2}\,,\frac{n+1}{2}\}
and
i>\frac{n+1}{2}
for odd
n>3
.

Transformation when
i=\frac{n-3}{2}
.

For this value of
i
it follows from (
4.40
) that the transformation depends only on the gauge field
\xi^{u}

when
\alpha=0
. From the recursion formula (
4.20
) of
\overset{(i)}{q}_{AB}
,
i\geq 2
, and the gauge transformation (
A.51
), p.
A.51
below, of

\hat{q}_{AB}^{(k)}
, the dependence on
\xi^{u}
only comes from the term
\overset{(i-1)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\hat{q}^{((n-5-2i)/2)}_{AB}
. This gives for
i=\frac{n-3}{2}
,
i\geq 2
(and hence
n>5
),

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbf{S}}\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2}))}{\mu}{}_{AB}

\displaystyle\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{q}{}^{AB}d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}\rightarrow\int_{\mathbf{S}}\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{\mu}{}^{AB}\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2}))}{q}_{AB}d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}+\int_{\mathbf{S}}\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{\mu}{}^{AB}{\operatorname{\!\!\check{\,\,L}}_{n}}(\xi^{u})_{AB}d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}\,,

(4.42)

where

\displaystyle\operatorname{\!\!\check{\,\,L}}_{n}(\xi^{u})_{AB}:=-\frac{n-4}{(n-5)(n-2)^{2}}\overset{(\frac{n-5}{2})}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\bigg((n-1)P-2(n-2)^{2}{{\varepsilon}}\bigg)\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\xi^{u}]\,.

(4.43)

In the case
n=5
we have
i=\frac{n-3}{2}=1
, so we must use the gauge transformation formula (
3.101
) of
\overset{(1)}{q}_{AB}=q_{AB}
. This leads to

\operatorname{\!\!\check{\,\,L}}_{5}(\xi^{u})_{AB}:=-\left(\frac{2}{9}P-{{\varepsilon}}\right)\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\xi^{u}]\,.

(4.44)

It follows from (
4.42
) and the definition (
4.35
) of
\overset{[3]}{Q}
that
when
m=0
and for any
\alpha
, under a gauge transformation, we have

\displaystyle\overset{[3]}{Q}\rightarrow\overset{[3]}{Q}+\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}}\circ\operatorname{\!\!\check{\,\,L}}_{n}(\xi^{u})\,.

(4.45)

Recall that
\alpha
has the same dimension as
r^{-1}
, and that the fields
\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{q}_{AB}
, and
\xi^{u}
both have the same dimension as
r
, while field
\xi_{A}
is dimensionless. Since
\alpha
appears in the gauge transformations (cf. Section
3.2
)
and in the recursion formulae (cf. Section
4
) with positive powers only,
we see that there will be no additional
r
-independent contributions to (
4.44
) and (
4.45
)
when
\alpha\neq 0
.

For later convenience, we define

\underline{\operatorname{\!\!\check{\,\,L}}}_{n}(\xi_{A}):=\begin{cases}-\left(\frac{2}{9}P-{{\varepsilon}}\right)\circ\,C(\xi)\,,&n=5\\
-\frac{n-4}{(n-5)(n-2)^{2}}\overset{(\frac{n-5}{2})}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\bigg((n-1)P-2(n-2)^{2}{{\varepsilon}}\bigg)\circ\,C(\xi)\,,&n>5\,,\end{cases}

(4.46)

so that

\operatorname{\!\!\check{\,\,L}}_{n}(\xi^{u})\equiv\underline{\operatorname{\!\!\check{\,\,L}}}_{n}({\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi^{u})\,.

(4.47)

Clearly,
{\mathrm{CKV}}\in\ker\underline{\operatorname{\!\!\check{\,\,L}}}_{n}
.

Transformation when
i=\frac{n-1}{2}
.

A similar analysis as the above for
i=\frac{n-1}{2}
with
n\geq 5
gives

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbf{S}}(\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2})}{\mu})^{AB}\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2})}{q}_{AB}d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}\rightarrow

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbf{S}}(\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2})}{\mu})^{AB}\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2})}{q}_{AB}d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}+\int_{\mathbf{S}}(\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2})}{\mu})^{AB}\operatorname{L_{n}}(\xi)_{AB}d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}\,,

(4.48)

where

\displaystyle\operatorname{L_{n}}(\xi)_{AB}

\displaystyle:=-\overset{(\frac{n-5}{2})}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\bigg[\tfrac{\mathcal{K}_{n}(\mathring{\Delta},P)}{(n-3)(n-1)}\big(2(2-n)(2P+(1-n){{\varepsilon}})C(\xi)_{AB}+4\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}{\mathring{D}}_{C}\xi^{C}]\big)\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+{\frac{1}{n-1}\operatorname{\!\!\check{\,\,L}}_{n}({\mathring{D}}_{C}\xi^{C})_{AB}}\,,

(4.49)

with

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{n}(\mathring{\Delta},P):=\begin{cases}\overset{(1)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,,&n=5\\
\mathcal{K}\left(-1,\mathring{\Delta},P\right)\,,&\text{otherwise\,.}\end{cases}

(4.50)

Remark
4.2
.

For further use we note that (
4.49
) can be simplified to

\operatorname{L_{n}}=\overset{(\frac{n-5}{2})}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\underline{\operatorname{L_{n}}}\,,

(4.51)

with
\overset{(0)}{\psi}:=1
and

\displaystyle\underline{\operatorname{L_{n}}}(\xi)_{AB}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{8}(\mathring{\Delta}+2\mathring{\mathscr{R}}-4{{\varepsilon}})(\mathring{\Delta}+2\mathring{\mathscr{R}}-6{{\varepsilon}})C(\xi)_{AB}&\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad-\frac{1}{6}(\mathring{\Delta}+2\mathring{\mathscr{R}}-5{{\varepsilon}})\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}{\mathring{D}}_{C}\xi^{C}]\,,&n=5\\
\frac{1}{(n-1)(n-5)}\bigg((\mathring{\Delta}+2\mathring{\mathscr{R}}-2(n-2){{\varepsilon}})(\mathring{\Delta}+2\mathring{\mathscr{R}}+(1-n){{\varepsilon}})C(\xi)_{AB}&\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad-\frac{2(n-3)}{n-2}(\mathring{\Delta}+2\mathring{\mathscr{R}}+(5-2n){{\varepsilon}})\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}{\mathring{D}}_{C}\xi^{C}]\bigg)\,,&n\neq 5\,.\end{cases}

(4.52)

Clearly (cf. Lemma
C.3
), we have
{\mathrm{CKV}}\in\ker\underline{\operatorname{L_{n}}}
.

It follows from (
4.48
) and the definition of
\overset{[4]}{Q}
in (
4.37
) that
when
m=0
and for any
\alpha
, under a gauge transformation we have

\displaystyle\overset{[4]}{Q}\rightarrow\overset{[4]}{Q}+{\mathring{D}}^{A}\operatorname{L_{n}}(\xi)_{AB}\,.

(4.53)

This is justified by a similar argument to that below (
4.45
) when
\alpha\neq 0
.

Transformation when
i=\frac{n+1}{2}
.

Returning to our main line of thought, we continue with the case
i=\frac{n+1}{2}
,
n\geq 5
, which gives

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbf{S}}

\displaystyle(\overset{((n+1)/2)}{\mu})^{AB}\overset{((n+1)/2)}{q}_{AB}d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}\rightarrow\int_{\mathbf{S}}(\overset{((n+1)/2)}{\mu})^{AB}\overset{((n+1)/2)}{q}_{AB}d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbf{S}}(\overset{((n+1)/2)}{\mu})^{AB}\Big(\operatorname{L_{n}}(\partial_{u}\xi)_{AB}+\big(n-\frac{2}{n}-\frac{2}{n-1}\big)\underbrace{\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2})}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)C(\partial_{u}\xi)_{AB}}_{=0}\Big)\,d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}\,,

(4.54)

where the underbraced term arises from (
A.52
), p.
A.52
and
vanishes by Proposition
C.11
, p.
C.11
below.

Transformation when
i>\frac{n+1}{2}
.

Finally for
i=\frac{n+3}{2}+j
with
j\geq 0
,
n\geq 5
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbf{S}}(\overset{((n+3)/2+j)}{\mu})^{AB}\overset{((n+3)/2+j)}{q}_{AB}d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}\rightarrow

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbf{S}}(\overset{((n+3)/2+j)}{\mu})^{AB}\overset{((n+3)/2+j)}{q}_{AB}d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbf{S}}(\overset{((n+3)/2+j)}{\mu})^{AB}\operatorname{L_{n}}(\partial^{j+2}_{u}\xi)_{AB}d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}\,.

(4.55)

The fields
\overset{(i)}{H}_{uA}
.

We continue with an analysis of the fields
\overset{(i)}{H}_{uA}
. We shall work out the
r
-independent parts of the associated gauge transformations, which is relevant for gluing. Recall that,
for any
m
and
\alpha
, the field
\overset{(1)}{H}_{uA}
is given by (see (
4.11
))

\overset{(1)}{H}_{uA}=\partial_{u}\overset{(0)}{H}_{uA}-{\mathring{D}}^{B}\hat{q}^{(-4)}{}_{AB}\,.

(4.56)

After making use of the gauge transformation law (
3.62
) of
\overset{(0)}{H}_{uA}
, and that of
\hat{q}_{AB}^{(-4)}
in (
A.51
), we obtain,
for any
m
and
\alpha
,

\displaystyle\overset{(1)}{H}_{uA}\rightarrow\overset{(1)}{H}_{uA}

\displaystyle+n\partial_{u}^{2}\xi_{A}+\alpha^{2}n\underbrace{\big(\frac{1}{n-1}{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\xi^{B}+\frac{2}{n+1}{\mathring{D}}^{B}C(\xi)_{AB}\big)}_{=:\overset{(1,1)}{D}(\xi)_{A}}

\displaystyle+\text{ $r$-dependent terms}\,.

(4.57)

Making use of (
4.11
), it can be shown inductively that for
i\geq 1
and
any
m
and
\alpha
,

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{H}_{uA}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\overset{(i)}{H}_{uA}+n\partial^{i+1}_{u}\xi_{A}+\alpha^{2}n\underbrace{\Big(\overset{(1)}{D}+\sum_{j=1}^{i}\mathcal{K}_{[\alpha]}(-(j+3))\mathcal{\tilde{K}}\left(-(j+2),\mathring{\Delta},\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C\right)\Big)(\partial^{i-1}_{u}\xi)_{A}}_{=:\overset{(i,1)}{D}(\partial^{i-1}_{u}\xi)_{A}}

\displaystyle\quad+O(\alpha^{4})+\text{ $r$-dependent terms}\,.

(4.58)

The
O(\alpha^{4})
terms depend on the fields
\xi^{u}
and
\partial_{u}^{j}\xi^{A}
with
1\leq j\leq i-3
,
and their explicit form is not needed for the arguments that follow.

4.4
Summary on regularity

From what has been said so far about the regularity of the fields, assuming

\displaystyle h_{AB}\in H^{k_{\gamma};+}_{{{\mycal N}}}\,,

(4.59)

the following regularity at
r=r_{1}
will be preserved by the various transport equations along
{\mycal N}
: for
0\leq i\leq k_{\gamma}/2-1
,

\displaystyle\partial_{u}^{i}\partial_{r}h_{uA},\partial_{u}^{i}h_{uA}

\displaystyle\in H^{k_{\gamma}-2i-1}({\mathbf{S}})

(4.60)

\displaystyle\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\beta\,,\partial_{u}^{i}h_{AB}

\displaystyle\in H^{k_{\gamma}-2i}({\mathbf{S}})\,,\quad\partial_{u}^{i}\delta V\in H^{k_{\gamma}-2i-2}(\mathbf{S})\,.

(4.61)

In addition these will be preserved under gauge transformations by the following regularity of the gauge fields: for
0\leq i\leq k_{\gamma}/2-1
,

\partial_{u}^{i}\xi^{u}\in H^{k_{\gamma}-2i+2}(\mathbf{S})\,,\ \partial_{u}^{i}\xi^{A}\in H^{k_{\gamma}-2i+1}(\mathbf{S})\,.

(4.62)

Recall the definition of linearised Bondi sphere data from (
3.12
):

\displaystyle x

\displaystyle=(\partial_{u}^{j}h_{AB}|_{\mathbf{S}},\,\partial_{r}^{j}h_{AB}|_{\mathbf{S}},\,\partial_{u}^{j}\delta\beta|_{\mathbf{S}},\,\partial_{u}^{j}\delta U^{A}|_{\mathbf{S}},\,\partial_{r}\delta U^{A}|_{\mathbf{S}},\,\delta V|_{\mathbf{S}})_{0\leq j\leq k}\in\delta\Psi_{\mathrm{Bo}}[\mathbf{S},k]\,.

(4.63)

In view of the above, we define the following spaces for linearised Bondi sphere data and gauge fields:

\displaystyle H^{k_{\gamma}}_{\mathrm{Bo}}({\mathbf{S}})

\displaystyle:=\prod_{j\in[0,k]}\bigg(H^{k_{\gamma}-2j}(\mathbf{S})\times\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}H^{k_{\gamma}-j}(\mathbf{S}),&\hbox{$j=0$}\\
H^{k_{\gamma}+1-j}(\mathbf{S}),&\hbox{$j>0$}\end{array}\right.\times H^{k_{\gamma}-2j}(\mathbf{S})\times H^{k_{\gamma}-2j-1}(\mathbf{S})\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\times H^{k_{\gamma}-1}(\mathbf{S})\times H^{k_{\gamma}-2}(\mathbf{S})\,,

(4.66)

\displaystyle H^{k_{\gamma}}_{\zeta}({\mathbf{S}}):

\displaystyle=\prod_{j\in[0,k+1]}H^{k_{\gamma}-2j+1}(\mathbf{S})\times H^{k_{\gamma}-2j+2}(\mathbf{S})\,.

(4.67)

We note that the gauge transformation map
z^{*}
of (
3.26
),

\displaystyle\tilde{x}=z^{*}(x)\,,

(4.68)

is a linear map preserving the regularity of the given data:

\mbox{if}\ (x,z)\in H^{k_{\gamma}}_{\mathrm{Bo}}({\mathbf{S}})\times H^{k_{\gamma}}_{\zeta}({\mathbf{S}})\ \mbox{then}\ z^{*}(x)\in H^{k_{\gamma}}_{\mathrm{Bo}}({\mathbf{S}})\,.

(4.69)

5
Gluing up to gauge

We now present a scheme for gluing, up to residual gauge, the linearised fields

\{h_{\mu\nu},\partial_{u}h_{\mu\nu}\,,\ldots\,,\partial_{u}^{k}h_{\mu\nu}\}

(5.1)

in Bondi gauge, with
2\leq k<\infty
.
We will assume for simplicity that each of the fields
\partial_{u}^{i}h_{\mu\nu}\big|_{\{u=0\}}
,
0\leq i\leq k
, is smooth. The collection of fields of this differentiability class will be denoted by
C^{k}_{u}\,C^{\infty}_{(r,x^{A})}
. The case of finite Sobolev regularity will be discussed in the next section.

The formulation of the problem follows that of
(
ChCong1
, Section 4)
, we repeat it here for
the convenience of the reader: Let
0\leq r_{0}<r_{1}<r_{2}<r_{3}\in\mathbb{R}
.
Consider two sets of vacuum linearised gravitational fields in Bondi gauge
of, say for simplicity,
C^{k}_{u}\,C^{\infty}_{(r,x^{A})}
-differentiability class,
defined in spacetime neighborhoods of
{\mycal N}_{(r_{0},r_{1}]}
and
{\mycal N}_{[r_{2},r_{3})}
. Denote by
\mathbf{S}_{1}
the section of
{\mycal N}_{(r_{0},r_{1}]}
at
r=r_{1}
. The linearised gravitational field near
{\mycal N}_{(r_{0},r_{1}]}

induces an element, say
x_{1}
, of the set
\delta\Psi_{\mathrm{Bo}}[\mathbf{S}_{1},k]
of Bondi cross-section data (cf. (
3.12
)).
Similarly, we denote by
\mathbf{S}_{2}
the section of
{\mycal N}_{[r_{2},r_{3})}
at
r=r_{2}
and the induced data by
x_{2}\in\delta\Psi_{\mathrm{Bo}}[\mathbf{S}_{2},k]
.
To take into account gauge transformation,
\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{1}
(resp.
\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{2}
) will denote the cross-section obtained by gauge-transforming
\mathbf{S}_{1}
(resp.
\mathbf{S}_{2}
).
The associated Bondi data is denoted by
\tilde{x}_{1}\in\delta\Psi_{\mathrm{Bo}}[\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{1},k]

(resp.
\tilde{x}_{2}\in\delta\Psi_{\mathrm{Bo}}[\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{2},k]
), and the outgoing null hypersurface on which it lies by
{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{(r_{0},r_{1}]}
(resp.

{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{2},r_{3})}
).

The goal
is to glue
\tilde{x}_{1}
and
\tilde{x}_{2}

along
{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{1},r_{2}]}
so that the resulting linearised field on
{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{(r_{0},r_{3})}
provide smooth characteristic data for Einstein equations
together. Indeed, we will show that
a
C^{k}_{u}\,C^{\infty}_{(r,x^{A})}
-linearised vacuum data set on
{\mycal N}_{(r_{0},r_{1}]}
can be smoothly glued to another such set on
{\mycal N}_{[r_{2},r_{3})}
up to gauge if and only if the obstructions listed in Tables
1.1
-
1.2
in the Introduction are satisfied.

Let
v_{AB}
be any symmetric traceless tensor field defined on a neighbourhood of
{\mycal N}_{[r_{1},r_{2}]}
which interpolates between the original fields
h_{AB}|_{{\mycal N}_{(r_{0},r_{1}]}}
and
h_{AB}|_{{\mycal N}_{[r_{2},r_{3})}}
,
so that the resulting field on
{\mycal N}_{(r_{0},r_{3})}
is as differentiable as the original fields.
When attempting a
C^{k}_{u}\,C^{\infty}_{(r,x^{A})}
-gluing, we can add to
v_{AB}
a field
w_{AB}|_{[r_{1},r_{2}]}

which vanishes smoothly
(i.e. together with
r
-derivatives of all orders)
at the end cross-sections
\{r_{1}\}\times\mathbf{S}
and
\{r_{2}\}\times\mathbf{S}
without affecting the smoothness of
h_{AB}
.
To take into account the gauge freedom, let
\phi(r)\geq 0
be a function which equals
1
near
r=r_{1}
and equals
0
near
r=r_{2}
. Let
\overset{(1)}{\xi}{}{}^{u}
and
\overset{(1)}{\xi}{}^{A}
be gauge fields which will be used to gauge the metric around
{\mycal N}_{(r_{0},r_{1}]}
, and let
\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}{}^{u}
and
\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}{}^{A}
be gauge fields which will be used to gauge the metric around
{\mycal N}_{[r_{2},r_{3})}
. For
r_{1}\leq r\leq r_{2}
we set

\tilde{h}_{AB}=v_{AB}+w_{AB}+\phi r^{2}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[\mathring{\mathcal{L}}_{\overset{(1)}{\zeta}}{\mathring{\gamma}}_{AB}]+(1-\phi)r^{2}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[\mathring{\mathcal{L}}_{\overset{(2)}{\zeta}}{\mathring{\gamma}}_{AB}]\,.

(5.2)

(Recall that
\zeta^{A}=\xi^{A}-{\mathring{D}}^{A}\xi^{u}/r
, cf. (
3.19
).)

In the gluing problem, the gauge fields evaluated on
\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{a}\,,a=1,2
and the field
w_{AB}
on
{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{(r_{1},r_{2})}
are
free fields
which can be chosen arbitrarily. Our aim in what follows is to show how to choose these fields to solve the transport equations of Section
3.3
and Section
4
to achieve gluing-up-to-gauge.

For the
C^{k}_{u}\,C^{\infty}_{(r,x^{A})}
-gluing we will need smooth functions

\displaystyle\kappa_{i}:(r_{1},r_{2})\rightarrow\mathbb{R}\,,\quad i\in\iota_{\alpha,m}:=\{k_{[\alpha]},k_{[\alpha]}+\frac{1}{2},k_{[\alpha]}+1,\ldots,k_{[m]}+4\}\subset\frac{1}{2}\mathbbm{Z}\,,

(5.3)

where

\displaystyle k_{[\alpha]}:=\begin{cases}4-n\,,&\alpha=0\\
\min(4-n,\tfrac{7-n-2k}{2})\,,&\alpha\neq 0\end{cases}\,,\qquad k_{[m]}:=\begin{cases}k\,,&m=0\\
k(n-1)\,,&m\neq 0\end{cases}\,,

(5.4)

and with
\kappa_{i}
’s satisfying

\displaystyle\displaystyle\langle\kappa_{i},\hat{\kappa}_{j}\rangle\equiv\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\kappa_{i}(s)\hat{\kappa}_{j}(s)\,ds=\delta_{ij}\,,\ \mbox{where}\ \hat{\kappa}_{i}(s):=s^{-i}\,,

(5.5)

and vanishing near the end points
r\in\{r_{1},r_{2}\}
, which is possible since the
\hat{\kappa}_{i}
’s are linearly independent.
The existence of such functions is standard, as we will work in a space where only a finite number of the
\kappa_{i}
’s is needed: Indeed, let
\phi\geq 0
,
\phi\not\equiv 0
, be any smooth function compactly supported in
(r_{1},r_{2})
. For
i,j\in\iota_{\alpha,m}
define

A_{ij}=\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\phi(s)\hat{\kappa}_{i}(s)\hat{\kappa}_{j}(s)\,ds\,.

(5.6)

The matrix
A_{ij}
is symmetric, and positive definite since the
\hat{\kappa}_{i}
’s are linearly independent:

A_{ij}x^{i}x^{j}=\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\phi(s)(\hat{\kappa}_{i}(s)x^{i})^{2}\,ds>0\,.

(5.7)

Hence its inverse, say
A^{ij}
, exists. Using the summation convention, set

\kappa_{i}(s)=A^{ik}\phi(s)\hat{\kappa}_{k}(s)\,.

(5.8)

Then

\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\kappa_{i}(s)\hat{\kappa}_{j}(s)\,ds=A^{ik}\underbrace{\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\phi(s)\hat{\kappa}_{k}(s)\hat{\kappa}_{j}(s)\,ds}_{A_{kj}}=\delta_{ij}\,,

(5.9)

as desired.

The fields
w_{AB}
of (
5.2
) will be taken of the following form: for
s\in[r_{1},r_{2}]
,

\displaystyle w_{AB}(r)=\sum_{i\in\iota_{\alpha,m}}\kappa_{i}(r)\overset{[i]}{\varphi}_{AB}\,.

(5.10)

We also define

\overset{[i]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}_{AB}\equiv\langle\hat{\kappa}_{i},w_{AB}\rangle\,.

(5.11)

In the following, we will show how to determine the fields
\overset{[i]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}_{AB}
, which we will sometimes refer to as the “interpolating fields”. The un-hatted fields
\overset{[i]}{\varphi}_{AB}
can be obtained from
\overset{[i]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}_{AB}
by solving a linear system of equations, see
(
ChCong1
, Equation (4.7))
.

We will use the York decomposition of the fields
\overset{[i]}{\varphi}_{AB}
:

\overset{[i]}{\varphi}_{AB}=\overset{[i]}{\varphi}{}{}^{[{\mathrm{TT}^{\perp}}]}_{AB}+\overset{[i]}{\varphi}{}^{[{\mathrm{TT}}]}_{AB}\equiv C(\overset{[i]}{w})_{AB}+\overset{[i]}{\varphi}{}^{[{\mathrm{TT}}]}_{AB}\,,

(5.12)

where
\overset{[i]}{\varphi}{}^{[{\mathrm{TT}}]}_{AB}
is transverse and traceless,

{\mathring{D}}^{A}\overset{[i]}{\varphi}{}^{[{\mathrm{TT}}]}_{AB}=0=\mathring{\gamma}^{AB}\overset{[i]}{\varphi}{}^{[{\mathrm{TT}}]}_{AB}\,,

(5.13)

and
\overset{[i]}{w}
a uniquely defined vector field which is
L^{2}
-orthogonal to the space of conformal Killing vector fields of
\mathring{\gamma}
;
similarly for
\overset{[i]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}{}_{AB}
, etc.

To achieve linearised characteristic
C^{k}_{u}\,C^{\infty}_{(r,x^{A})}
-gluing in the
\delta\beta=0
gauge,
it is sufficient to find a
smooth interpolation on
{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}
of the field
\tilde{h}_{AB}
and a continuous interpolation of
the fields

\displaystyle\{\tilde{\chi},\overset{(*)}{\tilde{H}}_{uA},\overset{(0)}{\tilde{H}}_{uA},\overset{(\ell)}{\tilde{H}}_{uA},\overset{(\ell)}{\tilde{q}}_{AB}\}_{\ell=1}^{k}\,,

(5.14)

where we remind the reader that
\tilde{h}
denotes the gauge-transformed counterpart of a field
h
. We will justify this shortly.

We consider first the case where the pair
(n,k)
is convenient with
m=0=\alpha
, because it contains most of the main ideas without
the technical complications which come with the remaining cases.
Then the transport equation for a field
\chi
,
H_{uA}
or
q

appearing in (
5.14
) generally takes the form

\displaystyle\partial_{r}H=r^{q}\hat{D}(h_{AB})\,,

(5.15)

for some
q\in\mathbbm{Z}/2
and for a linear differential operator
\hat{D}
in the
x^{A}
-variables acting on
h_{AB}
;
we list the exponents
q
and the operators
\hat{D}
for the various fields in Table
5.1
.

), p.

,

(

((

), p.

(

(

,

), p.

(

), p.

(

), p.

(
4.55
), p.
4.55

Table 5.1:
The operators appearing in (
5.15
) and (
5.19
).
The exponent
q
of the third column refers to the exponent of
r
in (
5.15
). The operator
P
is defined in (
3.91
), p.
3.91
.
Radially conserved charges arise from the kernel of the gluing operator
\hat{D}
in the case
m=\alpha=0
, whenever non-trivial.

Integrating (
5.15
) from
r_{1}
to
r_{2}
gives

\displaystyle H|_{\mathbf{S}_{2}}-H|_{\mathbf{S}_{1}}=\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}s^{q}\hat{D}(h_{AB}(s))\,ds\,;

(5.16)

this integrated transport equation tells us how to choose
h_{AB}
along
{\mycal N}_{[r_{1},r_{2}]}
to achieve continuous gluing of
H[x_{1}]
with
H[x_{2}]
.

However, it follows from (
5.15
) that
H{}^{[\ker\hat{D}^{\dagger}]}
is a radially conserved charge, since for any

\mu\in\ker\hat{D}^{\dagger}\,,

we have

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\int_{\mathbf{S}_{r}}\mu Hd\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}=r^{q}\int_{\mathbf{S}_{r}}\mu\hat{D}(h_{AB})d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}=r^{q}\int_{\mathbf{S}_{r}}\big(\hat{D}^{\dagger}\mu\big){}^{AB}h_{AB}d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}=0\,.

(5.17)

This shows that no choice of
h_{AB}
along
{\mycal N}_{[r_{1},r_{2}]}
can achieve the gluing of
H{}^{[\ker\hat{D}^{\dagger}]}
. Therefore, a necessary condition to achieve characteristic gluing-up-to-gauge is the existence of a gauge transformation so that

\widetilde{H}{}^{[\ker\hat{D}^{\dagger}]}|_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}-\widetilde{H}{}^{[\ker\hat{D}^{\dagger}]}|_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}=0\,,

(5.18)

where
\widetilde{H{}}
denote the gauge-transformed counterpart of
H{}
.

Let us denote by
\operatorname{\widehat{L}}
the linear differential operator occurring in the formula for the gauge-transformation of the charge associated with
H
:

\int_{\mathbf{S}_{2}}\mu Hd\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}\rightarrow\int_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}\mu(H+\operatorname{\widehat{L}}(\hat{\xi}))d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}\,,

(5.19)

where
\hat{\xi}
stands for a gauge field from the collection
\{\partial^{i}_{u}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}{}^{A},\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}{}^{u}\}_{i=0}^{k+1}
.

We will check that for all the operators
\operatorname{\widehat{L}}
and
\hat{D}
occurring in Table
5.1
we have (see Appendix
B
)

\framebox{$(\ker\operatorname{\widehat{L}}^{\dagger})^{\perp}=\text{im}\operatorname{\widehat{L}}$, similarly $(\ker\hat{D}^{\dagger})^{\perp}=\text{im}\hat{D}$.
}

(5.20)

Then for all
\mu\in(\ker\hat{D}^{\dagger})\cap(\ker\operatorname{\widehat{L}}^{\dagger})
it holds that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbf{S}_{2}}\mu\tilde{H}d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}=\int_{\mathbf{S}_{2}}\mu(H+\operatorname{\widehat{L}}(\hat{\xi}))d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}=\int_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{2}}\mu Hd\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}\,.

(5.21)

In other words, the
L^{2}
-orthogonal projection
H{}^{[(\ker\hat{D}^{\dagger})\cap(\ker\operatorname{\widehat{L}}^{\dagger})]}
of
H

is gauge-invariant and the fields
H|_{\mathbf{S}_{1}}
and
H|_{\mathbf{S}_{2}}
cannot be continuously glued-up-to-gauge unless

\framebox{$H{}^{[(\ker\hat{D}^{\dagger})\cap(\ker\operatorname{\widehat{L}}^{\dagger})]}|_{\mathbf{S}_{1}}=H{}^{[(\ker\hat{D}^{\dagger})\cap(\ker\operatorname{\widehat{L}}^{\dagger})]}|_{\mathbf{S}_{2}}\,.$
}

(5.22)

We shall refer to such projections as
gauge-invariant obstructions
to gluing.

Thus:

(i)

The
L^{2}
-orthogonal projection to

\ker(\hat{D}^{\dagger})\cap\text{im}(\operatorname{\widehat{L}})=\ker(\hat{D}^{\dagger})\cap\ker(\operatorname{\widehat{L}}^{\dagger})^{\perp}

of the gauge-transformed version of the condition (
5.18
) can be by solving the equation

\displaystyle\operatorname{\widehat{L}}(\hat{\xi}^{[(\ker\operatorname{\widehat{L}})^{\perp}]})=H{}^{[(\ker\hat{D}^{\dagger})\cap(\ker\operatorname{\widehat{L}}^{\dagger})^{\perp}]}|_{\mathbf{S}_{1}}-H{}^{[(\ker\hat{D}^{\dagger})\cap(\ker\operatorname{\widehat{L}}^{\dagger})^{\perp}]}|_{\mathbf{S}_{2}}\,,

(5.23)

for a unique field
\hat{\xi}^{[(\ker\operatorname{\widehat{L}})^{\perp}]}
.

(ii)

The projection onto
\text{im}\hat{D}=(\ker\hat{D}^{\dagger})^{\perp}
of the gauge-transformed version of the condition (
5.16
) results in the equation

\displaystyle\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}s^{q}\hat{D}(\tilde{h}_{AB}(s))ds=\widetilde{H}{}^{[(\ker\hat{D}^{\dagger})^{\perp}]}|_{\mathbf{S}_{2}}-\widetilde{H}{}^{[(\ker\hat{D}^{\dagger})^{\perp}]}|_{\mathbf{S}_{1}}\,,

(5.24)

which can be solved for a unique field
\overset{[-q]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}{}^{[(\ker\hat{D})^{\perp}]}_{AB}
.

To summarise: we solve the integrated transport equation (
5.16
) up to gauge by projecting it onto three parts. Then the part

1.

\ker\hat{D}^{\dagger}\cap\ker\operatorname{\widehat{L}}^{\dagger}
gives a gauge-invariant obstruction;

2.

\ker\hat{D}^{\dagger}\cap(\ker\operatorname{\widehat{L}}^{\dagger})^{\perp}
is solved by the gauge field
\hat{\xi}^{[(\ker\operatorname{\widehat{L}})^{\perp}]}
; and

3.

(\ker\hat{D}^{\dagger})^{\perp}
is solved by the interpolating field
\overset{[-q]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}{}^{[(\ker\hat{D})^{\perp}]}_{AB}
.

To make this work one needs to isolate the operators involved, establish their mapping properties, and make sure that the powers
r^{q}
arising in the integrals (
5.24
) are distinct for distinct fields
H
from the collection (
5.14
).
As an example, we provide an analysis of all operators involved in the case when
\mathbf{S}\approx S^{n-1}
in Appendix
E
, with the results summarised in Table
5.2
.

The strategy of the case with non-vanishing
\alpha
and/or
m
, and of the inconvenient case, is more involved due to the coupling between various equations, but comes with less obstructions to gluing. These cases will be treated in detail in Section
6
below.

We sketch now the linearised characteristic
C^{k}_{u}\,C^{\infty}_{(r,x^{A})}
-gluing. This can be achieved in three steps:

1.

Rather similarly to the four-dimensional case addressed in
ChCong1
, we solve the integrated transport equations for each field appearing in the set (
5.14
). These will determine the gauge fields
\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}{}_{A}
and
\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}{}^{u}
and the interpolating fields
\overset{[p]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}_{AB}
, as well as the conditions on
x_{1}\in\delta\Psi_{\mathrm{Bo}}[\mathbf{S}_{1},k]
and
x_{2}\in\delta\Psi_{\mathrm{Bo}}[\mathbf{S}_{2},k]
associated with the gauge-invariant-obstructions.
We list the fields involved in Tables
1.1
-
1.2
, and present more details in the next section. All remaining free fields, such as
\partial_{u}^{p}\overset{(1)}{\xi}{}
, not appearing in these tables are set to zero. The last columns (denoted as “obstructions”) in these tables list the gauge-invariant radial charges associated to the indicated fields appearing in the respective rows. See Table
5.2
for an explicit list of the spaces involved in the case
\mathbf{S}\approx S^{n-1}
,
n\geq 4
, and
\alpha=0=m
.

\hat{D}

\operatorname{\widehat{L}}

\text{im}(\operatorname{\widehat{L}})^{\perp}

\overset{(i)}{q}_{AB}
,
n

,

\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)

,

2.

Once the gauge fields and the fields
\overset{[p]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}_{AB}
with
k_{[\alpha]}\leq p\leq k_{[m]}+4

have been determined, we let
\tilde{h}_{AB}
be as in (
5.2
) and use this to construct the fields

\displaystyle\{\chi,\overset{(*)}{H}_{uA},\overset{(0)}{H}_{uA},\overset{(\ell)}{H}_{uA},\overset{(\ell)}{q}_{AB}\}_{\ell=1}^{k}

(5.25)

on
{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{1},r_{2})}
using the explicit formulae of Sections

3.3
and
4
:

i.

\partial_{u}^{p}\tilde{h}_{ur}
for
0\leq p\leq k
:
We set
\partial_{u}^{p}\tilde{h}_{ur}|_{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}\equiv 0
, which guarantees both smoothness of
\tilde{h}_{ur}
and the validity of the equations, for all
i
,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\mycal E_{rr}|_{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}\equiv-\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\mycal E^{u}{}_{r}|_{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}\equiv\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\mycal E^{uu}|_{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}\,.

(5.26)

ii.

\overset{(*)}{\tilde{H}}_{uA}
and
\overset{(p)}{\tilde{H}}_{uA}
for
0\leq p\leq k
: We determine these fields algebraically using the integrated transport equation (
3.51
) and the integrated version of (
4.3
). Here and in what follows, all
h_{\mu\nu}
fields in the representative formulae are understood to be replaced by
\tilde{h}_{\mu\nu}
’s.
Now, satisfying the transport equation (
4.3
) for
i=p

guarantees that on
{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}
we have

\partial_{u}^{p}\delta\mycal E_{rA}|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}\equiv-\partial_{u}^{p}\delta\mycal E^{u}{}_{A}|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}=0\,.

(5.27)

It follows that

\partial^{p}_{u}\delta\mycal E^{A}{}_{B}|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}=g^{AC}\partial^{p}_{u}\delta\mycal E_{CB}|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}\,.

(5.28)

The divergence identity for the Einstein tensor with a lower index
A
,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mu}\delta\mycal E^{\mu}{}_{A}

(5.29)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle r^{-(n-1)}\partial_{r}(r^{n-1}\delta\mycal E^{r}{}_{A})+\partial_{u}\delta\mycal E^{u}{}_{A}+{\mathring{D}}_{B}\delta\mycal E^{B}{}_{A}\,,

together with its
u
-derivatives, shows that we also have

\forall\ 0\leq i\leq k-1\quad\Big(r^{-(n-1)}\partial_{r}(r^{n-1}\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\mycal E^{r}{}_{A})+{\mathring{D}}_{B}\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\mycal E^{B}{}_{A}\Big)\Big|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}=0\,.

(5.30)

iii.

\tilde{\chi}
for
0\leq p\leq k
: We use the integrated (
3.83
) to set the field
\tilde{\chi}
on
{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}
.

iv.

\overset{(p)}{\tilde{q}}_{AB}
for
1\leq p\leq k
:
We use the representation formulae obtained by integrating (
4.17
) in
r
to
determine the field
\overset{(p)}{\tilde{q}}_{AB}
on
{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}
.
This ensures that

\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}\big(\delta\partial^{p-1}_{u}\mycal E_{AB}\big)\big|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}=0\,.

(5.31)

We have thus obtained all fields in the set (
5.25
). We emphasis that the continuity of these fields at
r_{2}

is guaranteed by the construction from Step 1. We continue now to determine the other metric components from them.

v.

\partial_{u}^{p}\tilde{\chi}
for
1\leq p\leq k
:
For
p=1
, the fields
\overset{(*)}{\tilde{H}}_{uA}
,
\overset{(0)}{\tilde{H}}_{uA}
,
\tilde{\chi}
and
\overset{(1)}{\tilde{q}}_{AB}
, as determined from items ii., iii., and iv. above, together determine the fields
\tilde{h}_{uA}
,
\tilde{h}_{uu}
and
\partial_{u}\tilde{h}_{AB}
algebraically.
Using the field
\partial_{u}\tilde{h}_{AB}
thus obtained, we set
\partial_{u}\tilde{\chi}
on
{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}
according to

\partial_{r}\partial_{u}^{p}\tilde{\chi}=\frac{(n-3)r^{n-5}}{n-1}{\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\partial_{u}^{p}\tilde{h}_{AB}\,,

(5.32)

for
p=1
, with the initial conditions (compare (
3.3.3
))

\displaystyle\partial_{u}^{p}\tilde{\chi}|_{r_{1}}

\displaystyle=\partial_{u}^{p}\chi|_{r_{1}}-\frac{2r_{1}^{n-3}(n-2)}{(n-1)^{2}}\mathring{\Delta}(\mathring{\Delta}+(n-1){\varepsilon})\partial_{u}^{p}\overset{(1)}{\xi}{}^{u}\ \,.

(5.33)

Note that
a priori
, the value of
\partial_{u}\tilde{\chi}|_{r_{2}}
thus obtained may not agree with the gauged data
\delta\Psi_{\mathrm{Bo}}[\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{2},k]
. However, we will justify this in item 3. below.

Next, the field
\overset{(1)}{\tilde{H}}_{uA}
and the equation
\delta\mycal E_{uA}=0
(cf. (
3.104
), with all
h_{\mu\nu}
’s replaced by
\tilde{h}_{\mu\nu}
’s) can be used to determine the field
\partial_{u}\tilde{h}_{uA}
. Using this, the field
\partial_{u}^{2}\tilde{h}_{AB}
can then be obtained from the field
\overset{(2)}{\tilde{q}}_{AB}
. The procedure then iterates: one determines
\partial^{2}_{u}\tilde{\chi}
from (
5.32
)-(
5.33
) with
p=2
and then the field
\partial_{u}^{2}\tilde{h}_{uA}
from
\overset{(2)}{\tilde{H}}_{uA}
and
\partial_{u}\delta\mycal E_{uA}=0
so on, until
p=k
.

The following analysis will be useful for step 3. below: Equations (
5.32
)-(
5.33
) ensure

\partial^{p}_{u}\delta\mycal E_{ru}|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}-\frac{1}{(n-2)r}{\mathring{D}}^{A}\partial^{p}_{u}\delta\mycal E_{rA}|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}=0\,.

(5.34)

Together with (
5.27
), Equation (
5.34
) guarantees that

\partial_{u}^{p}\delta{\mycal E}_{ru}|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}\equiv-\partial_{u}^{p}\delta\mycal E^{u}{}_{u}|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}=0\,.

(5.35)

The
u
-differentiated divergence identity with lower index
r
reads

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\equiv\nabla_{\mu}\partial_{u}^{p}\delta\mycal E^{\mu}{}_{r}

\displaystyle=\partial^{p}_{u}\delta\mycal E^{u}{}_{r}+\frac{1}{r^{n-1}}\partial_{r}(r^{n-1}\delta\partial^{p-1}_{u}\mycal E^{r}{}_{r})

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{\sqrt{|\det\mathring{\gamma}|}}\partial_{A}(\sqrt{|\det\mathring{\gamma}|}\delta\partial^{p-1}_{u}\mycal E^{A}{}_{r})-\frac{1}{r}g^{AB}\delta\partial^{p-1}_{u}\mycal E_{AB}\,,

(5.36)

so that, in view of (
5.26
), (
5.27
) and (
5.35
), we have now

\forall\ 0\leq i\leq k\qquad 0=\frac{1}{r}g^{AB}\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\mycal E_{AB}\big|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}\,.

(5.37)

Together with (
5.31
), it follows that

\forall\ 0\leq i\leq k-1\qquad\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\mycal E_{AB}\big|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}=0\,.

(5.38)

Equation (
5.30
) then gives

\displaystyle\forall\ 0\leq i\leq k-1\qquad 0

\displaystyle=r^{-(n-1)}\partial_{r}(r^{n-1}\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\mycal E^{r}{}_{A})|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}

\displaystyle=-r^{-(n-1)}\partial_{r}(r^{n-1}\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\mycal E_{uA})|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}\,,

(5.39)

where we have used

\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\mycal E^{r}{}_{A}|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}=-g_{uu}\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\mycal E_{rA}|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}-\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\mycal E_{uA}|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}=-\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\mycal E_{uA}|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}\,;

note that the last equality is justified by (
5.27
). Continuity at
r_{1}
, where all the fields
\partial_{u}^{i}\mycal E_{\mu\nu}
,
i\in\mathbb{N}
, vanish when the data there arise from a smooth solution of linearised Einstein equations, together with (
5.39
) implies that

\forall\ 0\leq i\leq k-1\qquad\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\mycal E^{r}{}_{A}|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}=0=\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\mycal E_{uA}|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}\,.

(5.40)

Meanwhile, the divergence identity for the Einstein tensor with a lower index
u
now reduces to

\forall\ 0\leq i\leq k-1\qquad 0\equiv\partial_{u}^{i}\nabla_{\mu}\delta\mycal E^{\mu}{}_{u}\big|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}=r^{-(n-1)}\partial_{r}(r^{n-1}\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\mycal E^{r}{}_{u})\big|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}\,.

(5.41)

Continuity and vanishing at
r_{1}

together with (
5.26
)
and (
5.35
)
implies that

\forall\ 0\leq i\leq k-1\qquad 0=\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\mycal E_{uu}\big|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}=-\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\mycal E^{r}{}_{u}\big|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}=\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\mycal E^{rr}\big|_{{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{[r_{0},r_{2})}}\,.

(5.42)

3.

The construction above guarantees
the continuous gluing at
r_{2}
of
\tilde{h}_{uu}
,
\partial_{u}\tilde{h}_{AB}
,
\overset{(p)}{\tilde{H}}_{uA}
with
0\leq p\leq k
, and
\overset{(p)}{\tilde{q}}_{AB}
with
2\leq p\leq k
. Continuity of the fields
\partial^{p}_{u}\tilde{h}_{uA}

and
\partial_{u}^{p}\tilde{h}_{uu}
for
1\leq p\leq k
and

\partial^{i}_{u}\tilde{h}_{AB}
for

at
r_{2}
follows now
by induction: The integrated transport equation for
\overset{(1)}{\widetilde{H}}_{uA}
and the explicit form (
3.104
) of the equation
\delta\mycal E_{uA}=0
can be solved simultaneously for
\partial_{u}\tilde{h}_{uA}|_{[r_{1},r_{2})}
and
\partial_{r}\partial_{u}\tilde{h}_{uA}|_{[r_{1},r_{2})}
. The solutions will be given in terms of the previously obtained fields
\{\tilde{h}_{uu},\tilde{h}_{uA},\tilde{h}_{AB},\partial_{u}\tilde{h}_{AB}\}
, which are continuous at
r_{2}
, hence ensuring continuity of
\partial_{u}\tilde{h}_{uA}
and
\partial_{r}\partial_{u}\tilde{h}_{uA}
at
r_{2}
. This in turn guarantees the continuity of
\partial_{u}^{2}\tilde{h}_{AB}
at
r_{2}
.

Meanwhile the explicit form (
3.107
) of
\delta\mycal E_{uu}=0
together with continuity at

of
\tilde{h}_{uu}
,
\tilde{h}_{uA}
and
\partial_{u}\tilde{h}_{AB}
, ensures the continuity of
\partial_{u}\tilde{h}_{uu}
at
r_{2}
.

Now, suppose that the continuity of the fields
\partial_{u}^{p}\tilde{h}_{uu}
,
\partial_{r}\partial_{u}^{p}\tilde{h}_{uA}
and
\partial_{u}^{p+1}\tilde{h}_{AB}
has been achieved up to

.
By differentiation of (
3.105
) we obtain the explicit form of (
5.40
) with

:

\displaystyle-r^{n+1}\partial_{r}\partial^{k}_{u}\left(\frac{h_{uA}}{r^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=r^{n-3}{\mathring{D}}^{B}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\partial^{k-1}_{u}{h}_{uB}-r^{n-3}{\mathring{D}}^{B}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\partial^{k-1}_{u}{h}_{uA}+r^{n-3}\partial^{k}_{u}{\mathring{D}}^{B}{h}_{AB}

\displaystyle\quad-2(n-2)r^{n-1}(\alpha^{2}+{2mr^{-n}})\partial^{k-1}_{u}h_{uA}-r^{2}\partial_{r}(r^{n-3}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\partial^{k-1}_{u}{h}_{uu})

\displaystyle\quad-r^{(n-2)}({\varepsilon}-r^{2}(\alpha^{2}+{2mr^{-n}}))((n-3)\partial_{r}\partial^{k-1}_{u}h_{uA}+r\partial^{2}_{r}\partial^{k-1}_{u}h_{uA})\,.

(5.43)

Equation (
5.43
) and the integrated transport equation (
4.3
) with
i=k
, together with the continuity of

\partial^{k-1}_{u}\tilde{h}_{uu}
,

\partial_{u}^{k-1}\tilde{h}_{uA}
and
\partial^{k}_{u}\tilde{h}_{AB}
,
ensures the continuity of
\partial_{u}^{k}\tilde{h}_{uA}
and
\partial_{r}\partial_{u}^{k}\tilde{h}_{uA}
at
r_{2}
. (This in turn guarantees the continuity of
\partial_{u}^{k+1}\tilde{h}_{AB}
at
r_{2}
, but this is irrelevant for
C^{k}_{u}\,C^{\infty}_{(r,x^{A})}
-gluing.)

Finally, the explicit form of (
5.42
) with
i=k-1
,
i.e.

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{u}^{k-1}\delta\mycal E_{uu}\big|_{{\mycal N}_{[r_{1},r_{2})}}

(5.44)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}\bigg[2\partial^{k}_{u}{\mathring{D}}^{A}{h}_{uA}+\partial_{r}\left(\frac{V}{r}\right){\mathring{D}}^{A}\partial_{u}^{k-1}{h}_{uA}-\frac{V}{r^{n-1}}\partial_{r}\bigg(r^{n-2}{\mathring{D}}^{A}\partial_{u}^{k-1}{h}_{uA}\bigg)

\displaystyle+\frac{V}{r^{3}}\bigg({\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}-\mathring{R}^{AB}\bigg)\partial_{u}^{k-1}h_{AB}-\left(r(n-1)(\partial_{u}-\partial_{r}\left(\frac{V}{r}\right))+R[\gamma]+{\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\right)\partial_{u}^{k-1}{h}_{uu}

\displaystyle+\frac{(n-1)V}{r^{2n-4}}\partial_{r}(r^{2n-4}\partial_{u}^{k-1}{h}_{uu})\bigg]+2\Lambda\partial_{u}^{k-1}h_{uu}\,,\phantom{xxxxx}

together with smoothness at
r_{2}
of
\partial_{u}^{k-1}\tilde{h}_{uu}
,
\partial_{u}^{k-1}\tilde{h}_{uA}
,
\partial_{u}^{k}\tilde{h}_{uA}
and
\partial^{k-1}_{u}\tilde{h}_{AB}
, ensures the continuity of
\partial^{k}_{u}\tilde{h}_{uu}
at
r_{2}
.

6
Solving the integrated transport equations on
{\mycal N}

We now provide more details to step 1. of the above.
In particular, the results in Section
5
and the current section will establish our main theorem of this paper, Theorem
6.1
below:

First we recall the definition of the set of Bondi cross-section data

\displaystyle\delta\Psi_{\mathrm{Bo}}[\mathbf{S},k]:=\{x|x=(\partial_{u}^{j}h_{AB}|_{\mathbf{S}},\,\partial_{r}^{j}h_{AB}|_{\mathbf{S}},\,\partial_{u}^{j}\delta\beta|_{\mathbf{S}},\,\partial_{u}^{j}\delta U^{A}|_{\mathbf{S}},\,\partial_{r}\delta U^{A}|_{\mathbf{S}},\,\delta V|_{\mathbf{S}})_{0\leq j\leq k}\}\,.

(6.1)

We will use the following function spaces:

\displaystyle H^{k_{\gamma}}_{\mathrm{Bo}}({\mathbf{S}})

\displaystyle:=\prod_{j\in[0,k]}\bigg(H^{k_{\gamma}-2j}(\mathbf{S})\times\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}H^{k_{\gamma}-j}(\mathbf{S}),&\hbox{$j=0$}\\
H^{k_{\gamma}+1-j}(\mathbf{S}),&\hbox{$j>0$}\end{array}\right.\times H^{k_{\gamma}-2j}(\mathbf{S})\times H^{k_{\gamma}-2j-1}(\mathbf{S})\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\times H^{k_{\gamma}-1}(\mathbf{S})\times H^{k_{\gamma}-2}(\mathbf{S})\,,

(6.4)

\displaystyle H^{k_{\gamma}}_{\zeta}({\mathbf{S}}):

\displaystyle=\prod_{j\in[0,k+1]}H^{k_{\gamma}-2j+1}(\mathbf{S})\times H^{k_{\gamma}-2j+2}(\mathbf{S})\,.

(6.5)

We have:

Theorem
6.1
.

Let
k_{\gamma},k\in\mathbb{N}
with
k_{\gamma}\geq 2k+2
. To any two sets of linearised codimension-two Bondi data

\displaystyle x_{r_{1}}\in\delta\Psi_{\mathrm{Bo}}[\mathbf{S}_{r_{1}},k]\,,\ x_{r_{2}}\in\delta\Psi_{\mathrm{Bo}}[\mathbf{S}_{r_{2}},k]\,,\ \mbox{with}\ x_{r_{a}}\in H^{k_{\gamma}}_{\mathrm{Bo}}({\mathbf{S}})\,,

(6.6)

one can smoothly assign a collection of gauge fields

\displaystyle(\partial^{i}_{u}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}_{A}\,,\partial^{i}_{u}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{u})_{i\in[0,k+1]\cap\mathbbm{Z}}\in H^{k_{\gamma}}_{\zeta}({\mathbf{S}})

(6.7)

on
\mathbf{S}_{r_{2}}
,
as well as a symmetric tensor field

\displaystyle h_{AB}\in H^{k_{\gamma};+}_{{{\mycal N}}}\,,

(6.8)

of the form (
5.2
)-(
5.10
) on
{\mycal N}_{[r_{1},r_{2}]}
,
to achieve
C^{k}
-gluing-up-to-gauge, up to a finite-dimensional space of obstructions.

Remarks
6.2
.

1.

The field
h_{AB}
is given by (
5.2
), with (cf. also (
5.3
) and (
5.10
))

\displaystyle\overset{[j]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}_{AB}\in H^{k_{\gamma}}(\mathbf{S})\,,\quad j\in\iota_{\alpha,m}\,.

(6.9)

Note to be compatible with (
6.8
), the field
v(r,\cdot)

in (
5.2
), as well as the remaining fields in (
5.2
),
belong to
H^{k_{\gamma}}(\mathbf{S})
. It will be part of the work that follows to show the regularity as indicated in (
6.7
)-(
6.9
) using Einstein equations.

2.

By achieving “
C^{k}
-gluing-up-to-gauge”, we mean the construction of the following linearised fields on
{\mycal N}_{[r_{1},r_{2}]}
:

\displaystyle y:=(\partial^{\ell}_{u}\delta V,\partial^{\ell}_{u}\delta\beta,\partial^{\ell}_{u}\delta U^{A},\partial^{\ell}_{u}h_{AB})_{0\leq\ell\leq k}\,,\ \mbox{with}\

(6.10)

\displaystyle y\in H^{k_{\gamma}-2\ell-2;+}_{\mathcal{N}}\times H^{k_{\gamma}-2\ell;+}_{\mathcal{N}}\times H^{k_{\gamma}-2\ell-1;+}_{\mathcal{N}}\times H^{k_{\gamma}-2\ell;+}_{\mathcal{N}}\,,

(6.11)

for
0\leq\ell\leq k
, such that:

(i)
y
agrees with the given data
x_{r_{1}}
at
\mathbf{S}_{1}
,

(ii)
y
agrees with the gauge-transformed data
x_{r_{2}}
at
\mathbf{S}_{2}
; and

(iii)
y
satisfies the linearised null constraint equations.

3.

By “up to a finite-dimensional space of obstructions” we mean that
there exists a finite-dimensional subspace
\mathcal{Q}
of
H^{k_{\gamma}}_{\mathrm{Bo}}({\mathbf{S}})
, such that point 2.(ii)
is satisfied only on the
L^{2}
-orthogonal complement of this space, i.e.,

(y|_{r_{2}}-x_{r_{2}})^{[\mathcal{Q}^{\perp}]}=0\,,

where we write
y|_{r_{2}}
to denote the Bondi cross-section data induced by
y
at
r=r_{2}
.

4.

When
m\neq 0
, the space of obstructions (for linearised data in any gauge) is provided by
\overset{[1]}{Q}(\pi^{A})
, where
\pi^{A}
is a Killing vector of the metric
\mathring{\gamma}
, and
\overset{[2]}{Q}(\lambda)
with
\lambda=1
; recall that these are defined as:

\displaystyle\overset{[1]}{Q}(\pi^{A})[x]

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbf{S}}\pi^{A}\left[r^{n+1}\partial_{r}(r^{-2}h_{uA})-2{r^{n-1}}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\delta\beta\right]\,d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}

(6.12)

\displaystyle\overset{[2]}{Q}(\lambda)[x]

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbf{S}}\lambda\Big[r^{n-3}\delta V-\frac{r^{n-2}}{n-1}\partial_{r}\bigg(r^{2}{\mathring{D}}^{A}\delta U_{A}\bigg)-\tfrac{2r^{n-2}}{n-1}\mathring{\Delta}\delta\beta

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-2r^{n-3}V\delta\beta\Big]d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}\,.

(6.13)

Under the assumption
m\neq 0
the space
\mathcal{Q}
of point 3. is a
(c_{{\mathring{\gamma}}}+1)
-dimensional subspace of the space

\{h_{uA}^{[{\mathrm{KV}}]},\partial_{r}h_{uA}^{[{\mathrm{KV}}]},\delta V^{[0]}\}\big|_{\mathbf{S}}\,,

where
c_{\mathring{\gamma}}
is the dimension of the space of Killing vectors of
(\mathbf{S},{\mathring{\gamma}})
.

5.

The finite-dimensional spaces of obstructions
in the gauge
\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\beta=0
are listed in Tables
1.1
-
1.2
. The formulae for obstructions in a gauge where the
\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\beta
’s do not vanish can be obtained from the formulae derived as follows:
Let us denote by

\mathring{Q}
the functional for one of the radial charges, where the gauge
\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\beta=0
has been used.
By construction,
\mathring{Q}
is gauge invariant under gauge transformations (
3.22
)-(
3.25
) satisfying

\partial_{u}^{i+1}\xi^{u}-\frac{{\mathring{D}}_{B}\partial_{u}^{i}\xi^{B}}{n-1}=0\,.

Thus, under a gauge transformation we have

\mathring{Q}\mapsto\mathring{Q}+\sum_{i=0}^{q}F_{i}\Big[\partial_{u}^{i+1}\xi^{u}-\frac{{\mathring{D}}_{B}\partial_{u}^{i}\xi^{B}}{n-1}\Big]\,,

(6.14)

for some
q\in\mathbbm{Z}
,
q\geq 0
, and some linear differential operators
F_{i}

(possibly depending upon
r
).
We set

Q=\mathring{Q}-\sum_{i=0}^{q}2F_{i}\Big[\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\beta\Big]\,.

(6.15)

It is straightforward to check that
Q
is gauge-invariant, keeping in mind the gauge-transformation law of
\delta\beta
:

2\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\beta\mapsto 2\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\beta+\partial_{u}^{i+1}\xi^{u}-\frac{{\mathring{D}}_{B}\partial_{u}^{i}\xi^{B}}{n-1}\,.

(6.16)

It remains to show that the new expression
Q
will also be radially conserved.
For this, we first take the
r
-derivative of (
6.14
), which gives

\partial_{r}\mathring{Q}\mapsto\partial_{r}\mathring{Q}+\sum_{i=0}^{q}\partial_{r}F_{i}\Big[\partial_{u}^{i+1}\xi^{u}-\frac{{\mathring{D}}_{B}\partial_{u}^{i}\xi^{B}}{n-1}\Big]\,.

(6.17)

Next,
the
r
-independence of
\mathring{Q}
in the gauge where all the fields
\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\beta
vanish implies that
in a general gauge where
\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\beta\neq 0
we must have

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\mathring{Q}=\sum_{i=0}^{p}\hat{H}_{i}\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\beta\,,

(6.18)

for some
p\in\mathbbm{Z}
,
p\geq 0
, and some linear differential operators
\hat{H}_{i}
.
Taking a gauge transformation of (
6.18
)
gives

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\mathring{Q}+\sum_{i=0}^{q}\partial_{r}F_{i}\Big[\partial_{u}^{i+1}\xi^{u}-\frac{{\mathring{D}}_{B}\partial_{u}^{i}\xi^{B}}{n-1}\Big]=\sum_{i=0}^{p}\bigg(\hat{H}_{i}\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\beta+\frac{1}{2}\hat{H}_{i}\Big[\partial_{u}^{i+1}\xi^{u}-\frac{{\mathring{D}}_{B}\partial_{u}^{i}\xi^{B}}{n-1}\Big]\bigg)\,.

(6.19)

Since the above holds for any values of
\partial_{u}^{i}\xi^{u}
and of
\partial_{u}^{i}\xi^{B}
we must have

\displaystyle p=q\,,\quad\partial_{r}F_{i}=\frac{1}{2}\hat{H}_{i}\,.

(6.20)

Thus,

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\Big(\mathring{Q}-\sum_{i=0}^{q}F_{i}\Big[2\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\beta\Big]\Big)=\sum_{i=0}^{q}\hat{H}_{i}\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\beta-\sum_{i=0}^{q}\hat{H}_{i}\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\beta=0\,,

(6.21)

as desired.

6.

As already pointed out, the differentiability classes in the theorem are more sophisticated than necessary for the linearised problem addressed here, and several consistent simpler
choices of functional spaces are possible. The current setup is dictated by consistency with the nonlinear setting of
ChCongGray2
.

To prove Theorem
6.1
, the following will be useful:
we will say that two sets of linearised Bondi data
x_{1}
and
x_{2}
are
gauge-equivalent
, and we will write

x_{1}\sim_{\mathrm{gauge}}x_{2}\,,

if there exist gauge fields

z:=(\partial^{i}_{u}\xi_{A}\,,\partial^{i}_{u}\xi^{u})_{i\in[1,k+1]}\in H^{k_{\gamma}}_{\zeta}({\mathbf{S}})

such that

x_{2}=z^{*}(x_{1})\,,

where the map
z^{*}
was defined in (
3.26
); see also (
4.69
).

Lemma
6.3
.

Let

x_{r_{1};a}\,,\ x_{r_{2};a}\in H^{k_{\gamma}}_{\mathrm{Bo}}({\mathbf{S}})

(6.22)

and

x_{r_{1};b}\,,\ x_{r_{2};b}\in H^{k_{\gamma}}_{\mathrm{Bo}}({\mathbf{S}})

(6.23)

be two sets of gauge-equivalent gluing data, i.e.,

x_{r_{j};a}\sim_{\mathrm{gauge}}x_{r_{j};b}\quad j=1,2\,.

(6.24)

Then
\{x_{r_{1};a}\,,x_{r_{2};a}\}
can be
C^{k}
-glued-up-to-gauge, up to a finite-dimensional space of obstructions, iff
\{x_{r_{1};b}\,,x_{r_{2};b}\}
can.

Proof.

Since data “
a
” and “
b
” are gauge-equivalent, there exist gauge fields

(\partial^{i}_{u}(\overset{(j)}{\xi}{}_{a\rightarrow b})_{A}\,,\partial^{i}_{u}\overset{(j)}{\xi}{}^{u}_{a\rightarrow b})_{i\in[1,k+1]}\in H^{k_{\gamma}}_{\zeta}({\mathbf{S}})\,,\quad j=1,2

such that, if we write

x_{r_{j};a}\equiv\big((\not{h}_{a,j})_{\mu\nu}\big)
,
we have

(\not{h}_{a,j})_{\mu\nu}=(\not{h}_{b,j})_{\mu\nu}+z^{*}_{\mu\nu}[(\partial^{i}_{u}(\overset{(j)}{\xi}{}_{a\rightarrow b})_{A}\,,\partial^{i}_{u}\overset{(j)}{\xi}{}^{u}_{a\rightarrow b})]\,,

(6.25)

where the linear map
z^{*}_{\mu\nu}
denotes the gauge transformation map for the relevant field as given by (
3.22
)-(
3.25
) together with the

u
- and
r
-derivatives of these equations.

Suppose now that data “
a
” can be
C^{k}
-glued up-to-gauge. Thus, there exist gauge fields
(\partial^{i}_{u}(\overset{(j)}{\xi}{}_{a})_{A}\,,\partial^{i}_{u}\overset{(j)}{\xi}{}^{u}_{a})_{i\in[1,k+1]}\in H^{k_{\gamma}}_{\zeta}({\mathbf{S}})
, and a fields
y\equiv(\not{h}_{\mu\nu})

as in (
6.11
) on
{\mycal N}_{[r_{1},r_{2}]}
such that

\displaystyle\not{h}_{\mu\nu}|_{r_{j}}=(\not{h}_{a,j})_{\mu\nu}+z^{*}_{\mu\nu}[(\partial^{i}_{u}\overset{(j)}{\xi}{}_{a,A}\,,\partial^{i}_{u}\overset{(j)}{\xi}{}^{u}_{a})]\,,\quad j=1,2\,.

(6.26)

Set

(\partial^{i}_{u}(\overset{(j)}{\xi}{}_{b})_{A}\,,\partial^{i}_{u}\overset{(j)}{\xi}{}^{u}_{b})=(\partial^{i}_{u}(\overset{(j)}{\xi}{}_{a\rightarrow b})_{A}\,,\partial^{i}_{u}\overset{(j)}{\xi}{}^{u}_{a\rightarrow b})+(\partial^{i}_{u}(\overset{(j)}{\xi}{}_{a})_{A}\,,\partial^{i}_{u}\overset{(j)}{\xi}{}^{u}_{a})\,.

(6.27)

We then have

\displaystyle(\not{h}_{b,j})_{\mu\nu}+z^{*}_{\mu\nu}[(\partial^{i}_{u}(\overset{(j)}{\xi}{}_{b})_{A}\,,\partial^{i}_{u}\overset{(j)}{\xi}{}^{u}_{b})]=(\not{h}_{a,j})_{\mu\nu}+z^{*}_{\mu\nu}[(\partial^{i}_{u}(\overset{(j)}{\xi}{}_{a})_{A}\,,\partial^{i}_{u}\overset{(j)}{\xi}{}^{u}_{a})]\,,

(6.28)

where we have used (
6.25
)-(
6.28
) and the linearity of
z^{*}_{\mu\nu}
.
Equations (
6.28
) and (
6.26
) then give

\displaystyle\not{h}_{\mu\nu}|_{r_{j}}=(\not{h}_{b,j})_{\mu\nu}+z^{*}_{\mu\nu}[(\partial^{i}_{u}\overset{(j)}{\xi}{}_{b,A}\,,\partial^{i}_{u}\overset{(j)}{\xi}{}^{u}_{b})]\,,\quad j=1,2\,.

(6.29)

In other words, data “
b
” is
C^{k}
-glued-up-to-gauge using the same interpolating fields
\not{h}_{\mu\nu}
as “
a
”, but using the gauge fields
(\partial^{i}_{u}(\overset{(j)}{\xi}{}_{b})_{A}\,,\partial^{i}_{u}\overset{(j)}{\xi}{}^{u}_{b})_{i\in[1,k+1]}\in H^{k_{\gamma}}_{\zeta}({\mathbf{S}})
,
j=1,2
.

\Box

We now proceed with the explicit construction of the solution which achieves
C^{k}
-gluing-up-to-gauge of some given data

\displaystyle x_{r_{1}}\,,\ x_{r_{2}}\in H^{k_{\gamma}}_{\mathrm{Bo}}({\mathbf{S}})\,.

(6.30)

6.1
\delta\beta=0
gauge

The gauge functions

\partial_{u}^{i+1}\overset{(j)}{\xi}{}^{u}|_{{\mathbf{S}}_{j}},\partial_{u}^{i}\overset{(j)}{\xi}{}_{A}|_{{\mathbf{S}}_{j}}\in H^{k_{\gamma}-2i}(\mathbf{S})
for
0\leq i\leq k
and
j=1,2
allow
us to transform
\partial_{u}^{j}\delta{\beta}\in H^{k_{\gamma}-2j}(\mathbf{S})

for
j\leq k
to zero on
\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{1}
and
\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{2}
,
by using e.g. the gauge transformation

\displaystyle 2\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\beta|_{\mathbf{S}_{r_{j}}}=-\partial^{i+1}_{u}\overset{(j)}{\xi}{}^{u}\in H^{k_{\gamma}-2i}\,,\quad\partial_{u}^{i}\overset{(j)}{\xi}{}_{A}=0.

(6.31)

These gauge fields transform the given data
x_{r_{1}}\,,x_{r_{2}}
to new data

\tilde{x}_{r_{1}}\,,\ \tilde{x}_{r_{2}}
(according to the transformation rules (
3.22
)-(
3.25
)) which continue to lie in
H^{k_{\gamma}}_{\mathrm{Bo}}({\mathbf{S}})
.

By Lemma
6.3
, it suffices to solve the gluing problem for these new data. In what follows, we proceed to do this. To ease notation, we will continue using the untilded
x_{r_{1}}\,,\ x_{r_{2}}
to denote the data. Furthermore, any gauge fields appearing will be understood to be associated to gauge transformations of the new data.

The
\partial_{u}^{i}\delta G_{rr}
constraint equation (
3.45
) requires

\displaystyle\forall\ 0\leq i\leq k\quad\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\beta|_{r_{1}}=\partial_{u}^{i}\delta\beta|_{r_{2}}\,,

(6.32)

which is automatically satisfied by the (new) data.
It will be convenient to work only with gauge fields at
r_{2}
from now on, that is, we will not perform further gauge transformations at
r_{1}
. Thus, to preserve (
6.32
), we are left with the residual gauge freedom given by (
3.28
)-(
3.31
), together with their
u
- and
r
- derivatives.

To simplify notation we omit the “
|_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{j}}
” on gauge fields, with the understanding that all
all
\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}
fields, and their
u
-derivatives, are evaluated on
\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{2}
.

6.2
Gauge invariant obstructions and gauge freezing

In this section we analyse the gauge-invariant obstructions arising from the transport equations of the Bondi data, and we determine some of the gauge fields needed to match gauge-dependent radial charges (cf. the last two columns of Tables
1.1
-
1.2
). More specifically, we provide a prescription how to determine all the gauge fields for the following two cases:
(1)
convenient pairs
(n,k)
for any
m
and
\alpha
, and
(2)
inconvenient pairs
(n,k)
but with
m=0
and any
\alpha
. For the remaining case, that is
(n,k)
inconvenient and
m\neq 0
, we only determine here the
{\mathrm{CKV}}
part of the gauge fields; the
{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}
part will be taken care of
in Section
6.4.3
.

We start with the field
h_{uu}
.
When
m=0
it follows from the conservation of the gauge-independent charge
\overset{[2]}{Q}(\lambda)
that the gluing of
h_{uu}
requires the data
x_{r_{1}}\in\delta\Psi_{\mathrm{Bo}}[\mathbf{S}_{1},k]
and
x_{r_{2}}\in\delta\Psi_{\mathrm{Bo}}[\mathbf{S}_{2},k]
to satisfy

\overset{[2]}{Q}[x_{r_{1}}]=\overset{[2]}{Q}[x_{r_{2}}]\,.

(6.33)

When
m\neq 0
and on
S^{n-1}
, part of the charge,
\overset{[2]}{Q}{(\lambda^{[=1]})}{}
, can be matched using the gauge field
({\mathring{D}}_{B}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{B})^{[=1]}
by setting:

\displaystyle(\chi[x_{r_{2}}])^{[=1]}-(\chi[x_{r_{1}}])^{[=1]}=\frac{2nm}{n-1}({\mathring{D}}_{B}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{B})^{[=1]}\,.

(6.34)

Let us pass now to the field
\partial_{r}h_{uA}
. It follows from the conservation of the radial charge

\overset{[1]}{Q}(\pi^{A})

that the gluing of
\partial_{r}h_{uA}
requires, for
\pi^{A}\in{\mathrm{CKV}}
,

\displaystyle\overset{[1]}{Q}[x_{r_{1}}](\pi^{A})-\overset{[1]}{Q}[x_{r_{2}}](\pi^{A})=2mn\int_{\mathbf{S}_{2}}\pi^{A}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{u}d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}=-2mn\int_{\mathbf{S}_{2}}({\mathring{D}}_{A}\ \pi^{A})\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{u}d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}\,.

(6.35)

When
m\neq 0

and when
(\mathbf{S},{\mathring{\gamma}})
admits proper conformal
Killing vectors (recall that this occurs only on the round
S^{n-1}
in our setting), we can use the freedom
in the choice of
(\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{u})^{[=1]}
to guarantee that (
6.35
) holds for
\pi^{A}
’s which are proper
{\mathrm{CKV}}
’s. We are thus left with the following gauge-invariant condition:

\overset{[1]}{Q}[x_{r_{1}}]=\overset{[1]}{Q}[x_{r_{2}}]\ \mbox{for}\ \pi^{A}\in\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}{\mathrm{KV}},&\hbox{$m\neq 0$;}\\
{\mathrm{CKV}},&\hbox{$m=0$.}\end{array}\right.

(6.36)

We note that since projections onto
{\mathrm{CKV}}
is smooth, so are the solutions to (
6.34
)-(
6.35
).
At this point, the
(n,k)
convenient and inconvenient cases need to be considered separately:

The case of convenient pairs
(n,k)
.

We start with the field
\overset{(i)}{H}_{uA}
.
When
m=0
, we match the gauge-dependent radial charges using the gauge fields (see (
4.58
))
(\partial_{u}^{i+1}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{A})^{[\ker(\overset{(i)}{\chi}\circ\,C)]}
according to

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{H}{}_{uA}^{[\ker(\overset{(i)}{\chi}\circ\,C)]}\big|_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}=\begin{cases}-n(\partial_{u}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{A})^{[{\mathrm{CKV}}]}-\alpha^{2}n({\mathring{D}}_{A}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{u})^{[{\mathrm{CKV}}]}\,,&i=0\,;\\
-n(\partial_{u}^{i+1}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{A})^{[\ker(\overset{(i)}{\chi}\circ\,C)]}+O(\alpha^{2})\,,&1\leq i\leq k\,.\end{cases}

(6.37)

(See Appendix
E
for an explicit
description
of the space
\ker(\overset{(i)}{\chi}\circ\,C)
when
\mathbf{S}\approx S^{n-1}
.)
The
O(\alpha^{2})
terms in the second line of (
6.37
) contain gauge fields of lower
u
-derivatives, of the form
(\partial_{u}^{j}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{A})^{[\ker(\overset{(i)}{\chi}\circ\,C)]}
with
0\leq j\leq i-1
, and the gauge field
\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{u}
(cf. comments below (
4.58
)).
Equation (
6.37
) can thus be solved recursively starting from
i=0
using the value of
(\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{u})^{[=1]}
determined from (
6.35
).

When
m\neq 0
,
we use the same scheme, but with
\ker(\overset{(i)}{\chi}\circ\,C)
replaced with
\ker C={\mathrm{CKV}}
wherever it appears in this paragraph.
Since all projections involved in (
6.37
) are onto smooth spaces, the solutions obtained are also smooth.
There are no gauge-invariant obstructions associated to the field
\overset{(i)}{H}_{uA}
in the
(n,k)
convenient case.

Finally, the gauge-invariant radial charges associated to the field
\partial_{u}^{p}h_{AB}
when
m=0=\alpha
impose
the following conditions on
x_{r_{1}}
and
x_{r_{2}}
:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbf{S}_{2}}(\overset{(p)}{\mu})^{AB}\overset{(p)}{q}_{AB}d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}-\int_{\mathbf{S}_{1}}(\overset{(p)}{\mu})^{AB}\overset{(p)}{q}_{AB}d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}=0\,,

(6.38)

for
1\leq p\leq k
and where
\overset{(p)}{\mu}\in\ker\overset{(p)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)
, as defined in (
4.39
). (In the
(n,k)
convenient case, when
m\neq 0
or
\alpha\neq 0
, there are no radially conserved charges associated to
\overset{(p)}{q}_{AB}
.)

The case of inconvenient pairs
(n,k)
.

In this case, we start with the field
\overset{(p)}{q}_{AB}
.
When
m=0=\alpha
, the gauge-invariant radial charges associated to
\overset{(p)}{q}_{AB}
for
1\leq p\leq\frac{n-5}{2}
impose the following conditions on
x_{r_{1}}
and
x_{r_{2}}
:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbf{S}_{2}}\overset{(p)}{\mu}{}^{AB}\overset{(p)}{q}_{AB}d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}-\int_{\mathbf{S}_{1}}\overset{(p)}{\mu}{}^{AB}\overset{(p)}{q}_{AB}d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}=0\,,\qquad 1\leq p\leq\frac{n-5}{2}\,,

(6.39)

where
\overset{(p)}{\mu}\in\ker\overset{(p)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)
, as defined in (
4.39
).

When
m=0
but
\alpha\neq 0
, there are no obstructions to gluing the fields
\overset{(p)}{q}_{AB}\,,1\leq p\leq\frac{n-5}{2}
(cf. (
6.141
)).

Next, for the field

\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{q}_{AB}\in H^{k_{\gamma}-(n-3)}(\mathbf{S})

in
the case
m=0=\alpha
, we match the associated gauge-dependent radial charge using the field
(\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{u})^{[(\ker\operatorname{\!\!\check{\,\,L}}_{n})^{\perp}]}

according to

\displaystyle\forall\ \overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{\mu}{}^{AB}\in\ker\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\cap(\ker\operatorname{\!\!\check{\,\,L}}_{n}^{\dagger})^{\perp}

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbf{S}_{1}}\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{\mu}{}^{AB}\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{q}_{AB}d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}-\int_{\mathbf{S}_{2}}\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{\mu}{}^{AB}\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{q}_{AB}d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}=\int_{\mathbf{S}_{2}}\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{\mu}{}^{AB}\operatorname{\!\!\check{\,\,L}}_{n}(\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{u})_{AB}d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}\,,

(6.40)

where the operator
\operatorname{\!\!\check{\,\,L}}_{n}
, which is of order
n-1
, has been defined in (
4.43
)-(
4.44
). Thus, the solution
(\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{u})^{[(\ker\operatorname{\!\!\check{\,\,L}}_{n})^{\perp}]}
lies in
H^{k_{\gamma}+2}(\mathbf{S})
, consistently with (
6.7
),
by ellipticity of
\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(1)}}\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\operatorname{\!\!\check{\,\,L}}_{n}
.

The gauge-invariant radial charge imposes further the condition,

\displaystyle\forall\ \overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{\mu}{}^{AB}\in

\displaystyle\ker\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\cap(\ker\operatorname{\!\!\check{\,\,L}}_{n}^{\dagger})

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbf{S}_{1}}\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{\mu}{}^{AB}\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{q}_{AB}d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}-\int_{\mathbf{S}_{2}}\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{\mu}{}^{AB}\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{q}_{AB}d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}=0\,,

(6.41)

on
x_{r_{1}}
and
x_{r_{2}}
.

In
the case
m=0
but
\alpha\neq 0
, Equation (
6.40
) for
\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{u}
is replaced by (cf. (
4.45
))

\displaystyle(\overset{[3]}{Q}[x_{r_{2}}]-\overset{[3]}{Q}[x_{r_{1}}])^{[\text{im}\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}}\circ\operatorname{\!\!\check{\,\,L}}_{n}]}=\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}}\circ\operatorname{\!\!\check{\,\,L}}_{n}(\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{u})\,,

(6.42)

with
\overset{[3]}{Q}\in H^{k_{\gamma}-(n-3)-2}
(cf. (
4.35
)), which determines
(\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{u})^{[(\ker(\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}}\circ\operatorname{\!\!\check{\,\,L}}_{n}))^{\perp}]}\in H^{k_{\gamma}+2}
uniquely.
In addition, the radial obstruction (
6.41
), is replaced by

\displaystyle(\overset{[3]}{Q}[x_{r_{2}}]-\overset{[3]}{Q}[x_{r_{1}}])^{[(\text{im}(\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}}\circ\operatorname{\!\!\check{\,\,L}}_{n})^{\perp}]}=0\,.

(6.43)

A similar analysis applies to the fields
\overset{(p)}{q}_{AB}
with index
p\geq\frac{n-1}{2}
. For this we first note that

{\mathrm{TT}}^{\perp}\subseteq\ker\overset{(p)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,,

(6.44)

as follows from
{\mathrm{TT}^{\perp}}=\text{im}C
and (
4.30
), p.
4.30
.
Meanwhile, the expression (
4.49
) for
\operatorname{L_{n}}
indicates that

\text{im}\,\operatorname{L_{n}}=(\ker\operatorname{L_{n}^{\dagger}})^{\perp}\subseteq{\mathrm{TT}}^{\perp}\,,

(6.45)

and thus

\text{im}\,\operatorname{L_{n}}\cap\ker\overset{(p)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)=\text{im}\,\operatorname{L_{n}}\,.

(6.46)

We consider now the fields

\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2}+j)}{q}_{AB}\in H^{k_{\gamma}-(n-1)-2j}(\mathbf{S})
.
In the case
m=0=\alpha
,
j\geq 0
, we match the associated gauge-dependent radial charges using the gauge fields

(\partial^{j}_{u}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}_{A})^{[(\ker\operatorname{L_{n}})^{\perp}]}
, which are in
H^{k_{\gamma}+1-2j}(\mathbf{S})
by ellipticity of
\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\operatorname{L_{n}}
(which follows from, e.g., (
C.61
)), according to

\displaystyle\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2}+j)}{q}{}_{AB}^{[\text{im}\operatorname{L_{n}}]}|_{\mathbf{S}_{1}}-\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2}+j)}{q}{}_{AB}^{[\text{im}\operatorname{L_{n}}]}|_{\mathbf{S}_{2}}=\operatorname{L_{n}}[(\partial^{j}_{u}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}_{A})^{[(\ker\operatorname{L_{n}})^{\perp}]}]_{AB}\,.

(6.47)

The gauge-invariant radial charge imposes the condition

\displaystyle\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2}+j)}{q}{}_{AB}^{[(\text{im}\operatorname{L_{n}})^{\perp}\cap\ker\overset{(p)}{\psi}]}|_{\mathbf{S}_{1}}-\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2}+j)}{q}{}_{AB}^{[(\text{im}\operatorname{L_{n}})^{\perp}\cap\ker\overset{(p)}{\psi}]}|_{\mathbf{S}_{2}}=0

(6.48)

on the data. It follows from (
6.46
) that (
6.47
)-(
6.48
) take care of the projection
\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2}+j)}{q}{}_{AB}^{[\ker\overset{(p)}{\psi}]}
.

When
m=0
but
\alpha\neq 0
, we continue using (
6.47
) and (
6.48
) for
j\geq 1
. However, for
j=0

Equation (
6.47
) for
\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}_{A}
is replaced by

\displaystyle(\overset{[4]}{Q}[x_{r_{2}}]-\overset{[4]}{Q}[x_{r_{1}}])^{[\text{im}(\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\operatorname{L_{n}})]}=\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\operatorname{L_{n}}(\overset{(2)}{\xi}{})\,,

(6.49)

which determines
\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}{}_{A}^{[(\ker(\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\operatorname{L_{n}}))^{\perp}]}
uniquely.
Since
\overset{[4]}{Q}\in H^{k_{\gamma}-(n-1)-1}(\mathbf{S})
and the elliptic operator
\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\operatorname{L_{n}}
is of order
n+1
, the solution
\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}{}_{A}^{[(\ker(\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\operatorname{L_{n}}))^{\perp}]}
lies in
H^{k_{\gamma}+1}(\mathbf{S})
.
In addition, the radial obstruction (
6.48
) is replaced by

\displaystyle(\overset{[4]}{Q}[x_{r_{2}}]-\overset{[4]}{Q}[x_{r_{1}}])^{[(\text{im}(\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\operatorname{L_{n}}))^{\perp}]}=0\,.

(6.50)

When
m\neq 0
, there are no radially conserved charges and hence no conditions on the data are required for the gluing of
\overset{(p)}{q}_{AB}
for
1\leq p\leq k
. The gauge fields in this case will be determined below (see Section
6.4.3
).

Finally, for the gauge-dependent radial charges
\overset{(i)}{H}{}^{[\ker(\overset{(i)}{\chi}\circ\,C)]}_{uA}
, in the case
when
m=0
, we
can match the
{\mathrm{CKV}}\in\ker(\overset{(i)}{\chi}\circ\,C)
-part of the charges using the gauge fields
(\partial_{u}^{i+1}\overset{(u)}{\xi}{}^{A})^{[{\mathrm{CKV}}]}
according to, for integers
0\leq i\leq k
(cf. (
4.58
)),

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{H}{}_{uA}^{[{\mathrm{CKV}}]}\big|_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}=\begin{cases}-n(\partial_{u}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{A})^{[{\mathrm{CKV}}]}-\alpha^{2}n({\mathring{D}}_{A}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{u})^{[{\mathrm{CKV}}]}\,,&i=0\,,\\
-n(\partial_{u}^{i+1}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{A})^{[{\mathrm{CKV}}]}+O(\alpha^{2})\,,&1\leq i\leq k\,,\end{cases}

(6.51)

where we note that the fields
(\partial_{u}^{i+1}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{A})^{[{\mathrm{CKV}}]}
remain free at this point since
{\mathrm{CKV}}\in\ker\operatorname{L_{n}}
and
\ker\operatorname{\!\!\check{\,\,L}}_{n}
(cf. below (
4.47
) and (
4.52
)).
As in the convenient case, the
O(\alpha^{2})
terms in (
6.51
) depend only on gauge fields with lower

u
-derivatives, i.e.,
\partial_{u}^{j}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{A}
, with
0\leq j\leq i-1
, and the gauge field
\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{u}
.
Thus Equation (
6.51
) can be solved recursively starting from
i=0
by using the value of
\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{u}
determined from (
6.35
) and (
6.40
).
Since the fields resulting from the projections involved in (
6.51
) are smooth, so are the solutions obtained.

Next, we move on to the
{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}
-part of the radial charges.
We show in Appendix
D.1

that there are no obstructions to gluing
\overset{(i)}{H}{}_{uA}^{[{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}]}
when
{{\varepsilon}}\leq 0
.
On the other hand, when
{{\varepsilon}}>0
, the obstructions associated to
\overset{(i)}{H}{}_{uA}
for
1\leq i\leq k-\frac{n+1}{2}
(there are no obstructions if
k<\frac{n+3}{2}
) impose the following conditions on
x_{r_{1}}
and
x_{r_{2}}
:

\displaystyle\overset{[5,i]}{Q}{}_{B}(x_{r_{1}})=\overset{[5,i]}{Q}{}_{B}(x_{r_{2}})\,.

(6.52)

The obstructions
\overset{[5,i]}{Q}{}_{B}
are of the form

\displaystyle\overset{[5,i]}{Q}{}

\displaystyle:=\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\operatorname{L_{n}}\Big[\overset{(i)}{H}{}^{[{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}\cap\ker(\overset{(i)}{\chi}\circ C)]}_{uA}\Big]+(...)\,,

(6.53)

with
(...)
depending on the radial charges
\overset{(j)}{q}{}^{[\ker\overset{(i)}{\psi}]}_{AB}
,
\overset{[3]}{Q}
and
\overset{[4]}{Q}
(see (
D.11
)-(
D.12
) for full expressions).
Now, since
\ker(\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\operatorname{L_{n}})=\{0\}

when
{{\varepsilon}}>0
(cf. (
D.16
)), imposing (
6.52
) ensures the gluing of the projection

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{H}{}_{uA}^{[{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}\cap\ker(\overset{(i)}{\chi}\circ\,C)]}

(6.54)

after the radial charges
\overset{(j)}{q}{}^{[\ker\overset{(i)}{\psi}]}_{AB}
,
\overset{[3]}{Q}
and
\overset{[4]}{Q}

have been matched.

For
\max(k-\frac{n-1}{2},1)\leq i\leq k
, the gauge-dependent radial charge
\overset{(i)}{H}{}_{uA}^{[{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}\cap\ker(\overset{(i)}{\chi}\circ\,C)]}
is matched using the smooth gauge fields
\partial_{u}^{i+1}\xi_{A}^{[{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}\cap\ker(\overset{(i)}{\chi}\circ\,C)]}
according to

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{H}{}_{uA}^{[{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}\cap\ker(\overset{(i)}{\chi}\circ\,C)]}=\partial_{u}^{i+1}\xi_{A}^{[{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}\cap\ker(\overset{(i)}{\chi}\circ\,C)]}+O(\alpha^{2})\,.

(6.55)

As before, the
O(\alpha^{2})
term in (
6.55
) depends only on gauge fields of lower
u
derivatives, i.e.,
\partial_{u}^{j}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{A}
, with
0\leq j\leq i-1
, and the field
\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{u}
.
Thus Equation (
6.55
) can be solved recursively starting from
i=k-\frac{n-1}{2}
by using the values of
\partial_{u}^{j}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{A}
, with
0\leq j\leq k-\frac{n-1}{2}
, and that of the field
\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{u}
as determined from (
6.40
)-(
6.49
).

When
m\neq 0
and for all
\alpha
, we continue using (
6.51
); no conditions on
x_{r_{1}}
and
x_{r_{2}}
are needed in this case. The gauge fields needed for the gluing of
\overset{(i)}{H}{}^{[{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}]}_{uA}
will be derived in Section
6.4.3
below.

The above gauge invariant conditions on
x_{r_{1}}
and
x_{r_{2}}
, together with the equations for the gauge fields, solve the
L^{2}
-orthogonal projections of the transport equations for the fields in the collection (
5.14
) onto their
radially conserved components
. In what follows, we analyse the conditions which arise from the
radially non-conserved components
of the fields. These will become equations for the interpolating fields
\overset{[p]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}_{AB}
. Their solutions will depend on the values of the gauge fields as determined above.

6.3
Undifferentiated equations

We begin with an analysis of the remaining characteristic equations which do not involve
u
-differentiated fields.
In what follows, the given data at
\mathbf{S}_{1}
and
\mathbf{S}_{2}
will be assumed to be in
H^{k_{\gamma}}_{\mathrm{Bo}}({\mathbf{S}})
, as defined in (
4.67
).

6.3.1
Continuity of
\partial_{r}\tilde{h}_{uA}

Taking into account the allowed gauge perturbations to Bondi data (
3.57
) and the transport equation (
3.50
), the gluing of
\partial_{r}\tilde{h}_{uA}
requires
\tilde{h}_{AB}
to satisfy on
{\,\,\,\,\,\widetilde{\!\!\!\!\!{\mycal N}}}_{(r_{1},r_{2})}
,

\displaystyle\overset{(*)}{H}_{uA}|_{\mathbf{S}_{2}}-\overset{(*)}{\tilde{H}}_{uA}|_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}

\displaystyle=2r_{2}^{n-2}{\operatorname{L_{1}}}(\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{u})_{A}+2r_{2}^{n-1}{\mathring{D}}^{B}C(\overset{(2)}{\zeta})_{AB}-2mn{\mathring{D}}_{A}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{u}

\displaystyle\qquad-(n-1)\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\hat{\kappa}_{4-n}(s){\mathring{D}}^{B}\tilde{h}_{AB}\,.

(6.56)

In this transport equation, and the ones that follow, the gauge fields made explicit come from the gauge transformation
at
r=r_{2}
of the field that is transported;

\overset{(*)}{H}_{uA}
in this case. Those gauge fields coming from the gauge transformations at
r=r_{1}
, and
from the gauge-corrections (
5.2
) both at
r=r_{1}
and
r_{2}
of the field
\tilde{h}_{AB}
,
are implicit in the notation.

For all cases except the
(n,k)
inconvenient,
m\alpha\neq 0
case, we solve the projection of (
6.56
) onto
\big[\ker(\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}^{\dagger})\big]^{\perp}
using the field

\overset{[4-n]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}_{AB}^{[{\mathrm{TT}}^{\perp}]}
.

We emphasise that a unique solution
\overset{[4-n]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}_{AB}^{[{\mathrm{TT}}^{\perp}]}
to the projection of (
6.56
) onto

\big[\ker(\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}^{\dagger})\big]^{\perp}={\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}=\text{im}(\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}})

exists. The solution will depend on the data
\delta\Psi_{\mathrm{Bo}}[\mathbf{S}_{1},k]
and
\delta\Psi_{\mathrm{Bo}}[\mathbf{S}_{2},k]
as well as the value of the gauge fields
which has been determined in Sections
6.1
and
6.2
. A straightfoward comparison with what has been said so far about the regularity of the other fields appearing in (
6.56
) shows that the solution
\overset{[4-n]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}_{AB}^{[{\mathrm{TT}}^{\perp}]}\in H^{k_{\gamma}}(\mathbf{S})
.

The case of inconvenient pairs
(n,k)
with
m\alpha\neq 0
is analysed in Appendix
G
.

6.3.2
Continuity of
\tilde{h}_{uA}

Taking into account the allowed gauge perturbations to Bondi data, it follows from (
3.61
)
and (
3.62
) that the gluing of
\tilde{h}_{uA}
requires

\displaystyle\overset{(0)}{H}_{uA}|_{\mathbf{S}_{2}}-\overset{(0)}{\tilde{H}}_{uA}|_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}-\frac{1}{r_{2}}\left((\mathring{\Delta}+(n-2){\varepsilon})+\frac{n-3}{n-1}{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\right)\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}_{B}

\displaystyle\quad+n\partial_{u}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}_{A}+\frac{n-4}{r^{2}}{\operatorname{L_{1}}}(\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{u})_{A}+\alpha^{2}n{\mathring{D}}_{A}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{u}=\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\hat{\kappa}_{4}(s){\mathring{D}}^{B}\tilde{h}_{AB}ds\,.

(6.57)

For all cases except the
(n,k)
inconvenient,
m\alpha\neq 0
case, we solve the projection of this equation onto
\big[\ker(\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}^{\dagger})\big]^{\perp}={\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}=\text{im}(\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}})
using the field
\overset{[4]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}^{[{\mathrm{TT}}^{\perp}]}_{AB}
in terms of the given data and the predetermined gauge fields.

For the case of inconvenient pairs
(n,k)
with
m\alpha\neq 0
, see Appendix
G
.

6.3.3
Continuity of
\tilde{h}_{uu}

The transport equation (
3.83
) together with the gauge transformation (
3.3.3
) of
\chi
results in the following condition for the continuity of
h_{uu}
at
r_{2}
:

\displaystyle\chi|_{\mathbf{S}_{2}}-\tilde{\chi}|_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}+\frac{2r_{2}^{n-2}(n-3)}{n-1}\bigg(\frac{1}{n-1}\mathring{\Delta}+{\varepsilon}\bigg){\mathring{D}}_{B}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{B}-\frac{2nm}{n-1}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{B}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{2r_{2}^{n-3}}{(n-1)^{2}}\mathring{\Delta}(\mathring{\Delta}+(n-1){\varepsilon})\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{u}=\frac{n-3}{n-1}\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\hat{\kappa}_{5-n}(s)\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}}(\tilde{h})ds\,.

(6.58)

For all cases except the
(n,k)
inconvenient,
m\alpha\neq 0
case, the continuity at
r_{2}
of the part of
\chi
which lies in the image

\text{im}\,\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}}
of the operator

\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}}:=\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(1)}}\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}
, can be achieved by solving
the projection onto
\text{im}\,\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}}
of (
6.58
) for a unique field

\overset{[5-n]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}_{AB}^{[(\ker\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}})^{\perp}]}\in S\cap H^{k_{\gamma}}(\mathbf{S})

(6.59)

(compare (
C.12
))
in terms of the predetermined gauge fields.
We note for future reference that
\overset{[5-n]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}{}{}^{[\ker\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}}]}_{AB}
remains free up to this point.
The reader is referred to Appendix
G
for the case of inconvenient pairs
(n,k)
with
m\alpha\neq 0
.

6.4
Higher derivatives

6.4.1
Continuity of
\partial_{u}^{p}\tilde{h}_{uA}

Taking into account the allowed gauge perturbations of Bondi data, it follows from (
4.3
) and (
4.58
) that the gluing of
\partial_{u}^{p}\tilde{h}_{uA}
,
1\leq p\leq k
requires

\displaystyle\overset{(p)}{H}_{uA}\big|_{\mathbf{S}_{2}}-\overset{(p)}{\tilde{H}}_{uA}\big|_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}

\displaystyle={\mathring{D}}^{B}\overset{(p)}{\chi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\hat{\kappa}_{p+4}(s)\tilde{h}_{AB}\ ds+m^{p}\overset{(p)}{\chi}_{[m]}\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\hat{\kappa}_{p(n-1)+4}(s){\mathring{D}}^{B}\tilde{h}_{AB}\ ds

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j,\ell}^{p_{*}}m^{j}\alpha^{2\ell}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\overset{(p)}{\chi}_{j,\ell}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\hat{\kappa}_{(p+4)+j(n-2)-2\ell}(s)\tilde{h}_{AB}\ ds

\displaystyle\quad-n\partial_{u}^{p+1}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}_{A}+\text{ gauge fields with lower $u$-derivatives}\,;

(6.60)

recall that the sum
\sum^{p_{*}}_{j,\ell}
has been defined in (
4.4
).

When
m=0
, the projection of (
6.60
) onto

\text{im}\,[\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\overset{(p)}{\chi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)]=(\ker[\overset{(p)}{\chi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\circ\,C])^{\perp}

can be solved uniquely for a field

\overset{[p+4]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}_{AB}^{[(\ker(\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\overset{(p)}{\chi}(\mathring{\Delta},P))^{\perp}]}\in(S\oplus V)\cap H^{k_{\gamma}}(\mathbf{S})

(6.61)

(compare (
C.12
))
in terms of the given data and predetermined gauge fields for
1\leq p\leq k
. We leave out the explicit formula of the gauge fields;
these can be determined order-by-order in the index
p
using the recursion formula (
4.11
) and the gauge transformations (
3.29
)-(
3.31
).

We move on now to the case
m\neq 0
.

The case of convenient pairs
(n,k)
.

First, recall that the projection of (
6.60
) onto
{\mathrm{CKV}}
has been solved in the paragraph below (
6.37
). We thus restrict our attention now to the projection of (
6.60
) onto
{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}
. Since
\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}
is surjective onto
{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}
,
and

\ker\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}={\mathrm{TT}}
,
it makes sense to rewrite this projection as

\displaystyle\underbrace{\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}^{-1}(\overset{(p)}{H}_{uA}\big|_{\mathbf{S}_{2}}-\overset{(p)}{\tilde{H}}_{uA}\big|_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{1}})^{[{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}]}+\text{known fields}}_{=:\overset{(p)}{S}_{AB}}

\displaystyle=\overset{(p)}{\chi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\overset{[p+4]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}_{AB}^{[{\mathrm{TT}^{\perp}}]}+m^{p}\overset{(p)}{\chi}_{[m]}\overset{[p(n-1)+4]}{{\hat{\varphi}}_{AB}}{}^{[{\mathrm{TT}^{\perp}}]}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j,\ell,\\
(p+4)+j(n-2)-2\ell>4\end{subarray}}^{p_{*}}m^{j}\alpha^{2\ell}\overset{(p)}{\chi}_{j,\ell}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\overset{[(p+4)+j(n-2)-2\ell]}{{\hat{\varphi}}_{AB}}{}^{[{\mathrm{TT}^{\perp}}]}\,,

(6.62)

with
\overset{(p)}{S}_{AB}\in H^{k_{\gamma}-2p}(\mathbf{S})
.
In (
6.62
), the term “known fields” denotes fields which have already been determined up to this point. These include (the
{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}
projections of) all the gauge fields and the fields
v_{AB}^{[{\mathrm{TT}^{\perp}}]}
and
\overset{[4]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}_{AB}^{[{\mathrm{TT}^{\perp}}]}
; this last field is the reason for the exclusion of
(p+4)+j(n-2)-2\ell=4
from the summation.

The last term in the right-hand side of (
6.62
) takes the form (cf. (
4.3
)-(
4.4
) with
i
there replaced by
p
)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j,\ell,\\
(p+4)+j(n-2)-2\ell>4\end{subarray}}^{p_{*}}m^{j}\alpha^{2\ell}\overset{(p)}{\chi}_{j,\ell}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\overset{[(p+4)+j(n-2)-2\ell]}{{\hat{\varphi}}_{AB}}{}^{[{\mathrm{TT}^{\perp}}]}

(6.63)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j\leq p-1\,,\ 0\leq\ell\leq p-j\\
