i=(p+4)+j(n-2)-2\ell>4\end{subarray}}m^{j}\alpha^{2\ell}\overset{(p)}{\chi}_{j,\ell}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\overset{[i]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}{}_{AB}^{[{\mathrm{TT}^{\perp}}]}\,.

We have

i=(p+4)+j(n-2)-2\ell\leq(p+4)+j(n-2)<(p+4)+p(n-2)=p(n-1)+4\,,

and to simplify notation it is convenient to rewrite (
6.62
) as

\displaystyle\overset{(p)}{S}_{AB}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=5}^{p(n-1)+4}\overset{(p)}{\chi}_{i}\overset{[i]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}_{AB}^{[{\mathrm{TT}^{\perp}}]}\,,

(6.64)

with some operators
\overset{(p)}{\chi}_{i}
(possibly zero).
For example,
one finds

\displaystyle\overset{(1)}{\chi}_{5}

\displaystyle=\overset{(1)}{\chi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\,,\quad\overset{(1)}{\chi}_{(n-1)+4}=m\overset{(1)}{\chi}_{[m]}\,,

(6.65)

\displaystyle\overset{(2n)}{\chi}_{2n+4}

\displaystyle=\overset{(2n)}{\chi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)+\sum_{j=1}^{2n-1}m^{j}\alpha^{\frac{j(n-2)}{2}}\overset{(2n)}{\chi}_{j,\frac{j(n-2)}{2}}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\,,\quad\text{if $n$ is even.}

(6.66)

The following facts can be easily verified and will be useful for the analysis below:

(i)

each of the coefficients
\overset{(p)}{\chi}_{j}
are either numbers, or products of commuting operators of the form

\operatorname{L}_{a,c}:=aP+\mathring{\Delta}+2\mathring{\mathscr{R}}+c\,,\quad a,c\in\mathbb{R}\,,

(6.67)

or sums thereof;

(ii)

for any
1\leq p\leq k
, the operators
\overset{(p)}{\chi}_{j}
are partial differential operators on
\mathbf{S}
of order less than or equal
2p
, with equality achieved only by
\overset{(p)}{\chi}_{4+p}
. In addition, we have

\overset{(p)}{\chi}_{4+p}=\overset{(p)}{\chi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)+\ell.o.\,,

(6.68)

where “
\ell.o.
” refers to operators of lower order (i.e.,
<2p
in this case). It follows from the ellipticity of
\overset{(p)}{\chi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)
(see Proposition
C.7
below) that
\overset{(p)}{\chi}_{4+p}
is elliptic;

(iii)

for each
1\leq p\leq k
, the coefficients
\overset{(p)}{\chi}_{p(n-1)+4}
are non-vanishing numbers (cf. (
4.7
) and (
4.9
)), and are given by
m^{p}\overset{(p)}{\chi}_{[m]}
, while
\overset{(p)}{\chi}_{j}=0
for
j>p(n-1)+4
.

Now, for
k\geq 1
we let

\Phi_{k}:=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\overset{[5]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}{}^{[{\mathrm{TT}^{\perp}}]}_{AB}\\
\overset{[6]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}{}^{[{\mathrm{TT}^{\perp}}]}_{AB}\\
\vdots\\
\overset{[4+k]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}{}^{[{\mathrm{TT}^{\perp}}]}_{AB}\\
\end{array}\right)\,,\qquad\Psi_{k}:=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\overset{[5+k]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}{}^{[{\mathrm{TT}^{\perp}}]}_{AB}\\
\overset{[6+k]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}{}^{[{\mathrm{TT}^{\perp}}]}_{AB}\\
\vdots\\
\overset{[4+k(n-1)]}{{\hat{\varphi}}_{AB}}{}{}^{[{\mathrm{TT}^{\perp}}]}\\
\end{array}\right)\,,\qquad S_{k}:=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\overset{(1)}{S}_{AB}\\
\overset{(2)}{S}_{AB}\\
\vdots\\
\overset{(k)}{S}_{AB}\\
\end{array}\right)\,.

The system (
6.62
) for
1\leq p\leq k
takes the form

S_{k}=\chi_{k}\Phi_{k}+M_{k}\Psi_{k}\,,

(6.69)

where

\chi_{k}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\overset{(1)}{\chi}_{5}&\ldots&\overset{(1)}{\chi}_{4+k}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\overset{(k)}{\chi}_{5}&\ldots&\overset{(k)}{\chi}_{4+k}\\
\end{array}\right)\,,\quad M_{k}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\overset{(1)}{\chi}_{5+k}&\ldots&\overset{(1)}{\chi}_{4+k(n-1)}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\overset{(k)}{\chi}_{5}&\ldots&\overset{(k)}{\chi}_{4+k(n-1)}\\
\end{array}\right)\,.

(6.70)

We continue by noting that the operator
\chi_{k}
is elliptic in the sense of Agmon, Douglis and Nirenberg (cf. Appendix
F.1
or e.g.,
MorreyNirenberg
). Indeed, it follows from item (ii) above that in the

p
’th row
of
\chi_{k}
the operators
\overset{(p)}{\chi}_{j}
are of order less than
2p
, except for the operator
\overset{(p)}{\chi}_{4+p}
lying on the diagonal, which is elliptic and precisely of order
2p
. This shows that the Agmon-Douglis-Nirenberg condition on the order
s_{p}+t_{j}
of
\overset{(p)}{\chi}_{j}
holds by setting
s_{p}=2p
and
t_{j}=0
.
The Agmon-Douglis-Nirenberg symbol is a block diagonal matrix with blocks on the diagonal having non-zero determinants,
and ellipticity readily follows.
This results in the estimate
(
MorreyNirenberg
, Theorem C)
:

Proposition
6.4
.

For all
k_{\gamma}\geq 2k
we have

\sum_{p=5}^{4+k}\|\overset{[p]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}{}\|_{k_{\gamma}}\leq C(k,k_{\gamma})\sum_{p=5}^{4+k}\big(\|(S_{k}-M_{k}\Psi_{k})_{p}\|_{k_{\gamma}-2p}+\|\overset{[p]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}{}\|_{0}\big)\,,

(6.71)

where
\|\cdot\|_{k}
is the
H^{k}(\mathbf{S})
-norm, and where
(S_{k}-M_{k}\Psi_{k})_{p}
denotes the
p
-th entry of the vector
S_{k}-M_{k}\Psi_{k}
.

Note that the operators
\operatorname{L}_{a,c}
of (
6.67
) are elliptic (see Lemma
C.6
), self-adjoint and pairwise commuting (see paragraph above (
I.8
)). These properties imply the existence of a complete set of smooth,
pairwise
L^{2}
-orthogonal, joint eigenfunctions
\phi_{\ell}
of all the
\operatorname{L}_{a,c}
’s appearing in
\chi_{k}
and
M_{k}
, with a corresponding discrete set of eigenvalues
\lambda_{a,c,\ell}
satisfying

|\lambda_{a,c,\ell}|\to_{\ell\to\infty}\infty\,.

We can therefore write

\Phi_{k}=\sum_{\ell}\Phi_{k,\ell}\phi_{\ell}\,,\quad\Psi_{k}=\sum_{\ell}\Psi_{k,\ell}\phi_{\ell}\,,\quad S_{k}=\sum_{\ell}S_{k,\ell}\phi_{\ell}\,.

(6.72)

The rest of the argument is essentially a repetition of that of
ChCong1
, we reproduce it here for the convenience of the reader:
It follows from item (i) above that (
6.69
) can be solved mode-by-mode:

\displaystyle\chi_{k}\Phi_{k,\ell}+M_{k}\Psi_{k,\ell}

\displaystyle=S_{k,\ell}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\chi_{k}|_{\operatorname{L}_{a,c}\mapsto\lambda_{a,c,\ell}}\Phi_{k,\ell}+M_{k}|_{\operatorname{L}_{a,c}\mapsto\lambda_{a,c,\ell}}\Psi_{k,\ell}

\displaystyle=S_{k,\ell}\,,

(6.73)

where
\operatorname{L}_{a,c}\mapsto\lambda_{a,c,\ell}
means that every occurrence of
\operatorname{L}_{a,c}
should be replaced by
\lambda_{a,c,\ell}
.

Now,
\det\chi_{k}|_{\operatorname{L}_{a,c}\mapsto\lambda_{a,c,\ell}}
is a polynomial in
\lambda_{a,c,\ell}
.
Keeping in mind that in each line of the matrix
\chi_{k}
the highest order operator is on the diagonal, we see that
\det\chi_{k}|_{\operatorname{L}_{a,c}\mapsto\lambda_{a,c,\ell}}
is non-zero for
\ell
large enough, and therefore there exists
N(k)
such that we can find a unique solution of (
6.73
) with
\Psi_{k,\ell}=0
for all
\ell>N(k)
.
Thus the
\Psi_{k}
’s are finite combinations of eigenfunctions of the
\operatorname{L}_{a,c}
’s, hence smooth, which renders useful the estimate (
6.71
).

It remains to show that (
6.73
) can be solved in the finite dimensional space of
\Phi_{k}
’s and
\Psi_{k}
’s of the form

\Phi_{k}=\sum_{\ell\leq N(k)}\Phi_{k,\ell}\phi_{\ell}\,,\quad\Psi_{k}=\sum_{\ell\leq N(k)}\Psi_{k,\ell}\phi_{\ell}\,,\quad S_{k}=\sum_{\ell\leq N(k)}S_{k,\ell}\phi_{\ell}\,.

(6.74)

This is equivalent to the requirement that all the linear maps obtained by juxtaposing
\chi_{k}|_{\operatorname{L}_{a,c}\mapsto\lambda_{a,c,\ell}}
and
M_{k}|_{\operatorname{L}_{a,c}\mapsto\lambda_{a,c,\ell}}
with
\ell<N(k)
are surjective. (Note, by the way, that we have already established surjectivity for
\ell\geq N(k)
.) This, in turn, is equivalent to the fact that the adjoints of these linear maps have no kernel.

Let us denote by
(\chi_{k}\,M_{k})
the relevant matrices. For simplicity in what follows we will write
\overset{(p)}{\chi}_{j}
for
\overset{(p)}{\chi}_{j}|_{\operatorname{L}_{a,c}\mapsto\lambda_{a,c,\ell}}
.
It follows from item (iii) above that
(\chi_{k}\,M_{k})
is of the form

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{ccccc}\begin{array}[]{|cc|}\hline\cr\overset{(1)}{\chi}_{5}&\dots\overset{(1)}{\chi}_{(n-1)+4}\\
\overset{(2)}{\chi}_{5}&\dots\overset{(2)}{\chi}_{(n-1)+4}\\
\vdots&\vdots\\
\vdots&\vdots\\
\vdots&\vdots\\
\overset{(k)}{\chi}_{5}&\dots\overset{(k)}{\chi}_{(n-1)+4}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}&\ldots&\begin{array}[]{|ccc|}\hline\cr 0&\ldots&0\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
0&\ldots&0\\
\overset{(k-j)}{\chi}_{(k-j-1)(n-1)+5}&\ldots&\overset{(k-j)}{\chi}_{(k-j)(n-1)+4}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\overset{(k)}{\chi}_{(k-j-1)(n-1)+5}&\ldots&\overset{(k)}{\chi}_{(k-j)(n-1)+4}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}&\ldots&\begin{array}[]{|ccc|}\hline\cr 0&\ldots&0\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\cdot&\cdot&\cdot\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
0&\ldots&0\\
\overset{(k)}{\chi}_{(k-1)(n-1)+5}&\ldots&\overset{(k)}{\chi}_{k(n-1)+4}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\end{array}\right)\,,

where the
(i,j)
-th entry is
\overset{(i)}{\chi}_{4+j}
. Note that each of the
k\times(n-1)
blocks, as grouped above, has a specific vanishing-block structure. This gives the following adjoint matrix:

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{l}\begin{array}[]{|cccccc|}\hline\cr\overset{(1)}{\chi}_{5}&\overset{(2)}{\chi}_{5}&\dots\dots&\ldots\dots&\dots\quad\dots\quad\dots\quad\dots\quad\ldots&\,\overset{(k)}{\chi}_{5}\\
\vdots&\vdots&&&&\,\vdots\\
\framebox{ $\overset{(1)}{\chi}_{(n-1)+4}$ }&\overset{(2)}{\chi}_{(n-1)+4}&\dots\dots&\ldots\dots&\dots\quad\dots\quad\dots\quad\dots\quad\ldots&\,\overset{(k)}{\chi}_{(n-1)+4}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\\
\begin{array}[]{cccccc}&&&\quad\vdots&&\end{array}\\
\begin{array}[]{|cccccc|}\hline\cr\phantom{\overset{(k)}{\chi}_{,k}}\,0&\phantom{\overset{(k)}{\chi}_{,k}}\quad\qquad 0\dots&0&\overset{(k-j)}{\chi}_{(k-j-1)(n-1)+5}&\ldots\quad\dots\quad\dots\quad\ldots&\overset{(k)}{\chi}_{(k-j-1)(n-1)+5}\\
\phantom{\overset{(k)}{\chi}_{,k}}\,\vdots&\phantom{\overset{(k)}{\chi}_{,k}}\quad\qquad\vdots\,\ddots&\vdots&\vdots&&\vdots\\
\phantom{\overset{(k)}{\chi}_{,k}}\,0&\phantom{\overset{(k)}{\chi}_{,k}}\quad\qquad 0\dots&0&\framebox{$\overset{(k-j)}{\chi}_{(k-j)(n-1)+4}$}&\ldots\quad\dots\quad\dots\quad\ldots&\overset{(k)}{\chi}_{(k-j)(n-1)+4}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\\
\begin{array}[]{cccccc}&&&\quad\vdots&&\end{array}\\
\begin{array}[]{|cccccc|}\hline\cr\phantom{\overset{(k)}{\chi}_{,k}}\,0&\phantom{\overset{(k)}{\chi}_{,k}}\quad\qquad 0\dots&\dots\dots&\ldots\quad\dots 0&\,\overset{(k-1)}{\chi}_{(k-2)(n-1)+5}&\overset{(k)}{\chi}_{(k-2)(n-1)+5}\\
\phantom{\overset{(k)}{\chi}_{,k}}\,\vdots&\phantom{\overset{(k)}{\chi}_{,k}}\quad\qquad\vdots\dots&\dots\dots&\ldots\quad\quad\vdots&\,\vdots&\vdots\\
\phantom{\overset{(k)}{\chi}_{,k}}\,0&\phantom{\overset{(k)}{\chi}_{,k}}\quad\qquad 0\dots&\dots\dots&\ldots\quad\dots 0&\,\framebox{$\overset{(k-1)}{\chi}_{(k-1)(n-1)+4}$}&\overset{(k)}{\chi}_{(k-1)(n-1)+4}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\\
\\
\begin{array}[]{|cccccc|}\hline\cr\phantom{\overset{(k)}{\chi}_{,k}}\,0&\phantom{\overset{(k)}{\chi}_{,k}}\quad\qquad 0\dots&\dots\dots&\ldots\quad\dots 0&\quad 0&\phantom{\overset{(k)}{\chi}_{,kk.}\quad}\overset{(k)}{\chi}_{(k-1)(n-1)+5}\\
\phantom{\overset{(k)}{\chi}_{,k}}\,\vdots&\phantom{\overset{(k)}{\chi}_{,k}}\quad\qquad\vdots\dots&\dots\dots&\ldots\quad\quad\vdots&\quad\vdots&\phantom{\overset{(k)}{\chi}_{,kk.}\quad}\vdots\\
\phantom{\overset{(k)}{\chi}_{,k}}\,0&\phantom{\overset{(k)}{\chi}_{,k}}\quad\qquad 0\dots&\dots\dots&\ldots\quad\dots 0&\quad 0&\phantom{\overset{(k)}{\chi}_{,kk.}\quad}\framebox{$\overset{(k)}{\chi}_{k(n-1)+4}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\end{array}\right)\,.

It follows from item (iii)
that the entries
\overset{(p)}{\chi}_{p(n-1)+4}
, boxed in the above, are non-zero when
m\neq 0
.
This easily implies that that the matrix in (
6.4.1
) has maximal rank, and thus trivial kernel.

We have therefore proved:

Theorem
6.5
.

Suppose, for simplicity, that
k_{\gamma}\geq 2k-1
.
The system (
6.62
) with
p\in\{1,\ldots,k\}
can be solved by a choice of interpolating fields

\overset{[j]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}_{AB}\in H^{k_{\gamma}}(\mathbf{S})\,,\quad 5\leq j\leq k(n-1)+4\,,

for any finite
k
. Its solutions are determined by an elliptic system, uniquely up to a finite number of eigenfunctions of the operators
\operatorname{L}_{a,c}
, and satisfy the estimate (
6.71
).

The case of inconvenient pairs
(n,k)
will be addressed in Section
6.4.3
below.

6.4.2
Continuity of
\partial_{u}^{p}\tilde{h}_{AB}

It follows from the transport equation (
4.17
) that the gluing of
\partial_{u}^{p}\tilde{h}_{AB}
with
p\geq 1
requires

\displaystyle\overset{(p)}{q}_{AB}\big|_{\mathbf{S}_{2}}-\overset{(p)}{\tilde{q}}_{AB}\big|_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}

\displaystyle=\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}[\ \overset{(p)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\hat{\kappa}_{(7-n+2p)/2}(s)\tilde{h}_{AB}+\alpha^{2p}\overset{(p)}{\psi}_{[\alpha]}\hat{\kappa}_{(7-n-2p)/2}(s)\tilde{h}_{AB}

\displaystyle\phantom{\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}[\ \quad}+m^{p}\overset{(p)}{\psi}_{[m]}\hat{\kappa}_{(7-n+2p(n-1))/2}(s)\tilde{h}_{AB}\ ]\ ds

\displaystyle\quad+m(\ldots)+\text{ gauge fields}

(6.108)

where, as already stated, the term “gauge fields” come from the gauge transformation
at
r=r_{2}
of
\overset{(p)}{q}_{AB}
, the exact form of which is unimportant for the argument here;
we will address the
m(...)
-terms in (
6.109
) below.

When
m=0
, the projection of (
6.108
) onto

{\text{im}[\overset{(p)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)]=}(\ker[\overset{(p)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)])^{\perp}

can be solved uniquely for the field

\overset{[(7-n+2p)/2]}{{\hat{\varphi}}_{AB}}{}^{[(\ker\overset{(p)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P))^{\perp}]}
.
If in addition we have
\alpha=0
, then the projection of (
6.108
) onto
\ker[\overset{(p)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)]
is a gauge-invariant obstruction (see (
6.38
)).

In the
(n,k)
convenient case, the operators
\overset{(p)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)
are elliptic and the solution
\overset{[(7-n+2p)/2]}{{\hat{\varphi}}_{AB}}{}^{[(\ker\overset{(p)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P))^{\perp}]}\in H^{k_{\gamma}}(\mathbf{S})
. The remaining projection of (
6.108
) onto
\ker[\overset{(p)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)]
has been solved using gauge fields in (
6.38
).

In the
(n,k)
inconvenient case, with
p\geq\frac{n-1}{2}
, we have
(\ker[\overset{(p)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)])^{\perp}={\mathrm{TT}}
. Since the operators
\overset{(p)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)
are elliptic when restricted to
{\mathrm{TT}}
, the regularity of the solution follows again from ellipticity. The projection onto
\ker[\overset{(p)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)]
has been solved in (
6.47
)-(
6.48
).

Finally, when
p=\frac{n-3}{2}
, we have
(\ker[\overset{(p)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)])^{\perp}={\mathrm{TT}}\oplus V
, where the space
V
is as defined in (
2.6
). The restriction of
\overset{(p)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)
to this space is once again elliptic (cf. Proposition
C.12
) and regularity follows from ellipticity. The projection onto
\ker[\overset{(p)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)]
has been solved using gauge fields in (
6.40
)-(
6.43
), after taking obstructions into account.

To proceed further, we need to consider the convenient and inconvenient cases separately.

The case of convenient pairs
(n,k)
.

First, we note that for such pairs
(n,k)
we have

\frac{n-7-2p}{2},\frac{n-7+2p}{2},\frac{n-7+2p(n-1)}{2}\neq 4+j,n-5,n-4

for any
p\geq 1
,
j\in\mathbb{Z}\,.

Hence the fields
\overset{[(7-n-2p)/2]}{{\hat{\varphi}}_{AB}}{},\overset{[(7-n+2p)/2]}{{\hat{\varphi}}_{AB}}{},\overset{[(7-n+2p(n-1))/2]}{{\hat{\varphi}}_{AB}}{}
are free at this point.

Thus
when
m=0
but
\alpha\neq 0
, the projection of (
6.108
) onto
\ker[\overset{(p)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)]
can be solved uniquely for the interpolating field

\overset{[(7-n-2p)/2]}{{\hat{\varphi}}_{AB}}{}^{[\ker\overset{(p)}{\psi}]}
for all
p\geq 1
.
Since the operators
\overset{(p)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)
are elliptic (cf. Proposition
C.10
),
\overset{[(7-n-2p)/2]}{{\hat{\varphi}}_{AB}}{}^{[\ker\overset{(p)}{\psi}]}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbf{S})
.
Equation (
6.108
) with
m=0
is a system of uncoupled equations for the fields
\overset{[(7-n+2p)/2]}{{\hat{\varphi}}_{AB}}{}
and
\overset{[(7-n-2p)/2]}{{\hat{\varphi}}_{AB}}{}
for
p\leq k
. However when
m\neq 0
, coupling between the equations with different
p
’s calls for a more involved scheme as follows.

When
m\neq 0
, we rewrite (
6.108
) as

\displaystyle\underbrace{\overset{(p)}{q}_{AB}\big|_{\mathbf{S}_{2}}-\overset{(p)}{q}_{AB}\big|_{\mathbf{S}_{1}}+\text{known fields}}_{=:\overset{(p)}{r}_{AB}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\in H^{k_{\gamma}-2p}(\mathbf{S})}}

\displaystyle=\overset{(p)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\overset{[(7-n+2p)/2]}{{\hat{\varphi}}_{AB}}{}+\alpha^{2p}\overset{(p)}{\psi}_{[\alpha]}\overset{[(7-n-2p)/2]}{{\hat{\varphi}}_{AB}}{}+m^{p}\overset{(p)}{\psi}_{[m]}\overset{[(7-n+2p(n-1))/2]}{{\hat{\varphi}}_{AB}}{}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j,\ell}^{p_{**}}m^{j}\alpha^{2\ell}\overset{(p)}{\psi}_{j,\ell}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\overset{[p-\frac{n-7}{2}+j(n-2)-2\ell]}{{\hat{\varphi}}_{AB}}{}\,,\quad 1\leq p\leq k\,,

(6.109)

where we used (
4.17
), and where the “known fields” refer to the predetermined gauge fields and the field
v

of (
5.2
).

Theorem
6.6
.

The system (
6.109
) can be solved by a choice of interpolating fields

\overset{[j]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}_{AB}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\in H^{k_{\gamma}}(\mathbf{S})}\,,\quad j\in\left[\frac{7-n-2k}{2},\frac{7-n+2k(n-1)}{2}\right]

for any finite
k
with
(n,k)
convenient. Its solutions are determined by an elliptic system, uniquely up to a finite number of joint eigenfunctions of the operators
\operatorname{L}_{a,c}
of (
6.67
).

Proof.

In the same spirit as (
6.64
), we write (
6.109
) as

\displaystyle\overset{(p)}{r}_{AB}=\sum_{j=\frac{7-n-2p}{2}}^{\frac{7-n+2p(n-1)}{2}}\overset{(p)}{\psi}_{j}\overset{[j]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}_{AB}\,,\quad 1\leq p\leq k\,.

(6.110)

The following facts, which will be useful for the analysis below, can be easily verified:

(i)

each of the coefficients
\overset{(p)}{\psi}_{j}
are either products of operators of the form
\operatorname{L}_{a,c}
in (
6.67
)
or sums thereof;

(ii)

for any
1\leq p\leq k
, the coefficients
\overset{(p)}{\psi}_{j}
are differential operators on
\mathbf{S}
of order less than or equal
2p
, with equality achieved only by
\overset{(p)}{\psi}_{\frac{7-n+2p}{2}}
. In addition, we have

\overset{(p)}{\psi}_{\frac{7-n+2p}{2}}=\overset{(p)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)+\ell.o.\,,

(6.111)

where “
\ell.o.
” refers to operators of lower order (i.e.,
<2p
). It follows from the ellipticity of
\overset{(p)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)
(see Proposition
C.10
) that
\overset{(p)}{\psi}_{\frac{7-n+2p}{2}}
is elliptic;

(iii)

for each
1\leq p\leq k
, the coefficients
\overset{(p)}{\psi}_{\frac{7-n+2p(n-1)}{2}}
are non-vanishing numbers and are given by
m^{p}\overset{(p)}{\psi}_{[m]}
, while
\overset{(p)}{\psi}_{j}=0
for
j>\frac{7-n+2p(n-1)}{2}
. In addition, when
\alpha\neq 0
,
the coefficients
\overset{(p)}{\psi}_{\frac{7-n-2p}{2}}=\alpha^{2p}\overset{(p)}{\psi}_{[\alpha]}
are non-vanishing numbers while the coefficients
\overset{(p)}{\psi}_{j}=0
for
j<\frac{7-n-2p}{2}
.

For
k\geq 1
we let

\displaystyle\Theta_{k}

\displaystyle:=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\overset{[\frac{7-n-2k}{2}]}{\hat{\varphi}}\\
\vdots\\
\overset{[\frac{7-n-2}{2}]}{\hat{\varphi}}\\
\end{array}\right)\,,\qquad\Xi_{k}:=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\overset{[\frac{9-n}{2}]}{\hat{\varphi}}\\
\vdots\\
\overset{[\frac{7-n+2k}{2}]}{\hat{\varphi}}\\
\end{array}\right)\,,\qquad\Omega_{k}:=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\overset{[\frac{9-n+2k}{2}]}{\hat{\varphi}}\\
\vdots\\
\overset{[\frac{7-n+2k(n-1)}{2}]}{\hat{\varphi}}\end{array}\right)\,,

\displaystyle r_{k}

\displaystyle:=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\overset{(1)}{r}_{AB}\\
\vdots\\
\overset{(k)}{r}_{AB}\\
\end{array}\right)\,.

The system (
6.110
) then takes the form

A_{k}\Theta_{k}+\psi_{k}\Xi_{k}+N_{k}\Omega_{k}=r_{k}\,,

(6.124)

where
A_{k}
and
\psi_{k}
are
k\times k
matrices of operators, and
N_{k}
is a
k\times k(n-2)
matrix of operators,

\displaystyle A_{k}

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\overset{(1)}{\psi}_{\frac{7-n-2k}{2}}&\ldots&\overset{(1)}{\psi}_{\frac{7-n-2}{2}}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\overset{(k)}{\psi}_{\frac{7-n-2k}{2}}&\ldots&\overset{(k)}{\psi}_{\frac{7-n-2}{2}}\end{array}\right)\,,\quad\psi_{k}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\overset{(1)}{\psi}_{\frac{9-n}{2}}&\ldots&\overset{(1)}{\psi}_{\frac{7-n+2k}{2}}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\overset{(k)}{\psi}_{\frac{9-n}{2}}&\ldots&\overset{(k)}{\psi}_{\frac{7-n+2k}{2}}\\
\end{array}\right)\,,

\displaystyle N_{k}

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\overset{(1)}{\psi}_{\frac{9-n+2k}{2}}&\ldots&\overset{(1)}{\psi}_{\frac{7-n+2k(n-1)}{2}}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\overset{(k)}{\psi}_{\frac{9-n+2k}{2}}&\ldots&\overset{(k)}{\psi}_{\frac{7-n+2k(n-1)}{2}}\end{array}\right)\,,

with
A_{k}
vanishing when
\alpha=0
.
Next, it holds by a similar argument to that below (
6.70
) that the operator
\psi_{k}
is elliptic in the sense of Agmon, Douglis and Nirenberg after setting
s_{i}=2i
,
t_{j}=0
.
In addition, there exists a complete set of smooth,
pairwise
L^{2}
-orthogonal, joint eigenfunctions
\eta_{\ell}
of all the
\operatorname{L}_{a,c}
’s appearing in
\psi_{k}
and
N_{k}
, with a corresponding discrete set of eigenvalues
\lambda_{a,c,\ell}
with
|\lambda_{a,c,\ell}|\to_{\ell\to\infty}\infty
.
We can therefore write

\Theta_{k}=\sum_{\ell}\Theta_{k,\ell}\eta_{\ell}\,,\quad\Xi_{k}=\sum_{\ell}\Xi_{k,\ell}\eta_{\ell}\,,\quad\Omega_{k}=\sum_{\ell}\Omega_{k,\ell}\eta_{\ell}\,,\quad r_{k}=\sum_{\ell}r_{k,\ell}\eta_{\ell}\,.

(6.134)

It then follows from item (i) above that (
6.124
) can be solved mode-by-mode:

\displaystyle A_{k}\Theta_{k,\ell}+\psi_{k}\Xi_{k,\ell}+N_{k}\Omega_{k,\ell}

\displaystyle=r_{k,\ell}

(6.135)

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\quad A_{k}|_{\operatorname{L}_{a,c}\mapsto\lambda_{a,c,\ell}}\Theta_{k,\ell}+\psi_{k}|_{\operatorname{L}_{a,c}\mapsto\lambda_{a,c,\ell}}\Xi_{k,\ell}+N_{k}|_{\operatorname{L}_{a,c}\mapsto\lambda_{a,c,\ell}}\Omega_{k,\ell}

\displaystyle=r_{k,\ell}\,.

(6.136)

Now, by an argument similar to that below (
6.73
) (with
\chi_{k}
there replaced by
\psi_{k}
), there exists
N(k)
such that we can find a unique solution of (
6.136
) with
\Xi_{k,\ell}=0=\Omega_{k,\ell}
for all
\ell>N(k)
.
It remains to show that (
6.135
) can be solved in the finite dimensional space of
\Theta_{k,\ell}
’s,
\Xi_{k,\ell}
’s and
\Omega_{k,\ell}
’s of the form

\Theta_{k}=\sum_{\ell\leq N(k)}\Theta_{k,\ell}\eta_{\ell}\,,\quad\Xi_{k}=\sum_{\ell\leq N(k)}\Xi_{k,\ell}\eta_{\ell}\,,\quad\Omega_{k}=\sum_{\ell\leq N(k)}\Omega_{k,\ell}\eta_{\ell}\,.

(6.137)

This is equivalent to the requirement that the linear maps obtained by juxtaposing
A_{k}|_{\operatorname{L}_{a,c}\mapsto\lambda_{a,c,\ell}}
,
\psi_{k}|_{\operatorname{L}_{a,c}\mapsto\lambda_{a,c,\ell}}
and
N_{k}|_{\operatorname{L}_{a,c}\mapsto\lambda_{a,c,\ell}}
with
\ell<N(k)
are surjective. This, in turn, is equivalent to the fact that the adjoints of these linear maps have no kernel.

We shall denote by
(A_{k}\,\psi_{k}\,N_{k})
the relevant matrices. For simplicity, in what follows we shall write
\overset{(p)}{\psi}_{j}
for
\overset{(p)}{\psi}_{j}|_{\operatorname{L}_{a,c}\mapsto\lambda_{a,c,\ell}}
. It follows from item (iii) above that the matrix
A_{k}|_{\operatorname{L}_{a,c}\mapsto\lambda_{a,c,\ell}}
is of the form

\displaystyle A_{k}=\begin{pmatrix}0&\ldots&\overset{(1)}{\psi}_{\frac{7-n-2}{2}}\\
\vdots&\iddots&\vdots\\
\overset{(k)}{\psi}_{\frac{7-n-2k}{2}}&\ldots&\overset{(k)}{\psi}_{\frac{7-n-2}{2}}\end{pmatrix}

(6.138)

with all entries on the reverse diagonal non-vanishing when
\alpha\neq 0
, and all entries above the reverse diagonal vanishing.
Clearly,
A_{k}^{\dagger}
has trivial kernel in this case. Meanwhile it follows from (iii) above that the matrix
(\psi_{k}\,N_{k})
is of the same form as
(\chi_{k}\,M_{k})
in (
6.4.1
), but with all
\overset{(p)}{\chi}_{j}
’s there replaced by
\overset{(p)}{\psi}_{j-\frac{1+n}{2}}
. It follows by the argument below (
6.4.1
) that
(\psi_{k}\,N_{k})^{\dagger}
has trivial kernel.

We thus conclude that the matrix
(A_{k}\,\psi_{k}\,N_{k})^{\dagger}
has trivial kernel regardless of the value of
\alpha
,
since a block matrix of the form
\begin{pmatrix}A\\
B\end{pmatrix}

(here “
B=\psi N
”) has trivial kernel when either
A
or
B
does.

Note that we can always set
\Theta_{k}=0
and solve only for
\Omega_{k}
; but if
\alpha\neq 0
, we can set
\Omega_{k}=0=\Xi_{k}
and solve for
\Theta_{k}
.

Finally, since from above, the
\Xi_{k}
’s and
\Omega_{k}
’s are finite combinations of eigenfunctions of the
\operatorname{L}_{a,c}
’s, they are smooth. Furthermore, we have the following estimate analogous to that in Proposition
6.4
:
For all
k_{\gamma}\geq 2k
we have the Agmon-Douglis-Nirenberg estimate
(choosing
\ell=k_{\gamma}-2k
in Theorem
F.1
)

\sum_{p=\frac{9-n}{2}}^{\frac{7-n+2k}{2}}\|\overset{[p]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}{}\|_{k_{\gamma}}\leq C(k,k_{\gamma})\sum_{p=\frac{9-n}{2}}^{\frac{7-n+2k}{2}}\big(\|(r_{k}-A_{k}\Theta_{k}-N_{k}\Omega_{k})_{p}\|_{k_{\gamma}-2p}+\|\overset{[p]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}{}\|_{0}\big)\,,

(6.139)

where
\|\cdot\|_{k}
is the
H^{k}(\mathbf{S})
-norm, and where
(r_{k}-A_{k}\Theta_{k}-N_{k}\Omega_{k})_{p}
denotes the
p
-th entry of the vector
r_{k}-A_{k}\Theta_{k}-N_{k}\Omega_{k}
.

\Box

6.4.3
The case of inconvenient pairs
(n,k)

The case
m=0=\alpha
has been covered below (
6.108
).

The case
m=0
,
\alpha\neq 0
.

For this case
the projection of (
6.108
) onto
\ker[\overset{(p)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)]
, for
1\leq p\leq\frac{n-5}{2}
, can be solved uniquely for the smooth interpolating field

\overset{[(7-n-2p)/2]}{{\hat{\varphi}}_{AB}}{}^{[(\ker\overset{(p)}{\psi})]}
, which gets rid of the obstructions in (
6.39
) in this range of
p
’s. Explicitly, we set, for
1\leq p\leq\frac{n-5}{2}
,

\displaystyle\big(\overset{(p)}{q}_{AB}\big|_{\mathbf{S}_{2}}

\displaystyle-\overset{(p)}{q}_{AB}\big|_{\mathbf{S}_{1}}-\text{(known fields})\big)^{[\ker\overset{(p)}{\psi}]}=\alpha^{2p}\overset{(p)}{\psi}_{[\alpha]}\overset{[(7-n-2p)/2]}{{\hat{\varphi}}_{AB}}{}^{[\ker\overset{(p)}{\psi}]}\,,

(6.140)

\displaystyle(\overset{(p)}{q}_{AB}\big|_{\mathbf{S}_{2}}

\displaystyle-\overset{(p)}{q}_{AB}\big|_{\mathbf{S}_{1}})^{[(\ker\overset{(p)}{\psi})^{\perp}]}-\text{(known fields})^{[(\ker\overset{(p)}{\psi})^{\perp}]}

\displaystyle=\overset{(p)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\overset{[(7-n+2p)/2]}{{\hat{\varphi}}_{AB}}{}{}^{[(\ker\overset{(p)}{\psi})^{\perp}]}+\alpha^{2p}\overset{(p)}{\psi}_{[\alpha]}\underbrace{\overset{[(7-n-2p)/2]}{{\hat{\varphi}}_{AB}}{}^{[(\ker\overset{(p)}{\psi})^{\perp}]}}_{\text{set }\equiv 0}\,,

(6.141)

where the “known fields” on the left-hand sides refer to the field
v
and those gauge fields which have been predetermined in Section
6.2
for the case
m=0
and
\alpha\neq 0
.
We note that (
6.141
) determines the field

\overset{[(7-n+2p)/2]}{{\hat{\varphi}}_{AB}}{}^{[(\ker\overset{(p)}{\psi})^{\perp}]}\in H^{k_{\gamma}}(\mathbf{S})
uniquely.

We continue with
p=\frac{n-3}{2}
, in which case we take care of the projection of (
6.108
) onto
(\ker[\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)])^{\perp}
by solving the equation

\displaystyle\big(\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{q}_{AB}\big|_{\mathbf{S}_{2}}

\displaystyle-\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{q}_{AB}\big|_{\mathbf{S}_{1}}-\text{(known fields})\big)^{[\ker(\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{\psi})^{\perp}]}

\displaystyle=\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\overset{[2]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}_{AB}^{[\ker(\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{\psi})^{\perp}]}+\alpha^{n-3}\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{\psi}_{[\alpha]}\underbrace{\overset{[5-n]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}_{AB}^{[\ker(\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{\psi})^{\perp}]}}_{\text{set }\equiv 0}

(6.142)

for the field

\overset{[2]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}_{AB}^{[\ker(\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{\psi})^{\perp}]}\in V\cap H^{k_{\gamma}}(\mathbf{S})
(see Proposition
C.12
, p.
C.12
). We note that setting
\overset{[5-n]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}_{AB}^{[\ker(\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{\psi})^{\perp}]}
to zero does not conflict with (
6.58
) since
\ker(\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{\psi})^{\perp}\subset\ker\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}}
(cf. (
4.36
)).

Next, consider the projection of (
6.108
) onto
\ker(\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{\psi})
.
Assume that the obstruction (
6.43
) is satisfied by the data and that (
6.42
) holds.
This,
together with the solution of the transport equation for the field
\chi
solves the projection of (
6.108
) onto
\ker(\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{\psi})\cap(\ker\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}})^{\perp}
. The remaining projection onto
\ker(\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{\psi})\cap\ker\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}}
can be achieved using the
smooth
fields
\overset{[5-n]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}{}_{AB}^{[\ker\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{\psi}\cap\ker\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}}]}
according to

\displaystyle\big(\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{q}_{AB}\big|_{\mathbf{S}_{2}}-\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{q}_{AB}\big|_{\mathbf{S}_{1}}-\text{(known fields})\big)^{[\ker\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}}\cap\ker\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{\psi}]}=\alpha^{n-3}\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{\psi}_{[\alpha]}\,\overset{[5-n]}{{\hat{\varphi}}_{AB}}{}^{[\ker\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}}\cap\ker\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{\psi}]}\,.

(6.143)

We continue with the case
p=\frac{n-1}{2}
, where we solve the projection of (
6.108
) onto
{\mathrm{TT}}
by solving the projections

\displaystyle\big(\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2})}{q}_{AB}\big|_{\mathbf{S}_{2}}-\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2})}{q}_{AB}\big|_{\mathbf{S}_{1}}-\text{(known fields})\big)^{[(\ker\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2})}{\psi})^{\perp}\cap{\mathrm{TT}}]}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad=\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2})}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\overset{[4]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}_{AB}^{[\ker(\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2})}{\psi})^{\perp}\cap{\mathrm{TT}}]}+\alpha^{n-1}\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2})}{\psi}_{[\alpha]}\underbrace{\overset{[4-n]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}_{AB}^{[\ker(\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2})}{\psi})^{\perp}\cap{\mathrm{TT}}]}}_{\text{set }=0}\,,

(6.144)

\displaystyle\big(\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2})}{q}_{AB}\big|_{\mathbf{S}_{2}}-\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2})}{q}_{AB}\big|_{\mathbf{S}_{1}}-\text{(known fields})\big)^{[\ker\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2})}{\psi}\cap{\mathrm{TT}}]}=\alpha^{n-1}\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2})}{\psi}_{[\alpha]}\overset{[4-n]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}_{AB}^{[\ker\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2})}{\psi}\cap{\mathrm{TT}}]}

(6.145)

for the fields

\overset{[4-n]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}_{AB}^{[\ker(\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2})}{\psi})^{\perp}\cap{\mathrm{TT}}]}\in H^{k_{\gamma}}(\mathbf{S})
and
\overset{[4-n]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}_{AB}^{[{\mathrm{TT}}]}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbf{S})

respectively. We note that the latter field remains free after solving (
6.56
). Next, (
6.49
)-(
6.50
) together with the solution of the transport equation for the field
\overset{(*)}{H}_{uA}
ensures that the projection of (
6.108
) with
p=\frac{n-1}{2}
onto
{\mathrm{TT}^{\perp}}
holds.

For each
p
with
p\geq\frac{n+1}{2}
, we solve the projection of (
6.108
) onto
\ker(\overset{(p)}{\psi})^{\perp}
for the interpolating field

\overset{[(7-n+2p)/2]}{{\hat{\varphi}}_{AB}}{}^{[\ker(\overset{(p)}{\psi})^{\perp}]}\in(V\oplus S)\cap H^{k_{\gamma}}(\mathbf{S})
.
Explicitly, we set, for each
p\geq\frac{n+1}{2}
,

\displaystyle(\overset{(p)}{q}_{AB}\big|_{\mathbf{S}_{2}}

\displaystyle-\overset{(p)}{q}_{AB}\big|_{\mathbf{S}_{1}})^{[(\ker\overset{(p)}{\psi})^{\perp}]}-\text{(known fields})^{[(\ker\overset{(p)}{\psi})^{\perp}]}=\overset{(p)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\overset{[(7-n+2p)/2]}{{\hat{\varphi}}_{AB}}{}{}^{[(\ker\overset{(p)}{\psi})^{\perp}]}\,.

(6.146)

(The projection of (
6.108
) onto
\ker(\overset{(p)}{\psi})
has already been addressed in (
6.47
)-(
6.48
).)

The case
m\neq 0
,
\alpha=0
.

In this case Equation (
6.60
) for
\overset{(p)}{H}{}^{[{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}]}_{uA}
and (
6.108
) for
\overset{(p)}{q}{}^{[{\mathrm{TT}^{\perp}}]}_{AB}
may involve
\overset{[i]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}_{AB}^{[{\mathrm{TT}^{\perp}}]}
’s with the same index
i
. This forces us to consider the
\overset{(p)}{H}{}^{[{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}]}_{uA}
- and
\overset{(p)}{q}{}_{AB}^{[{\mathrm{TT}^{\perp}}]}
-equations as a system of coupled equations.

First,
note that the transport equations for
\overset{(p)}{q}_{AB}
with
\alpha=0
take the form (cf. (
4.17
))

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\overset{(p)}{q}_{AB}

\displaystyle=\overset{(p)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,r^{(n-7-2p)/2}h_{AB}+m^{p}\overset{(p)}{\psi}_{[m]}r^{\frac{n-7-2p(n-1)}{2}}h_{AB}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j=1}^{p-1}m^{j}\overset{(p)}{\psi}_{j,0}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,r^{\frac{n-7}{2}-p-j(n-2)}h_{AB}\,,\quad 1\leq p\leq k\,,

(6.147)

with

\displaystyle\overset{(p)}{q}_{AB}

\displaystyle=\partial_{u}\overset{(p-1)}{q}_{AB}-\overset{(p-1)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\hat{q}_{AB}^{(n-5-2p)/2}-m^{p-1}\overset{(p-1)}{\psi}_{[m]}\hat{q}^{(\frac{n-7-2(p-1)(n-1)}{2})}_{AB}

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{j=1}^{p-2}m^{j}\overset{(p-1)}{\psi}_{j,0}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\hat{q}^{(\frac{1}{2}(n-7-2(p-1)-2j(n-2)))}_{AB}\,,\quad 1\leq p\leq k\,.

(6.148)

The associated integrated transport equation then reads

\displaystyle\underbrace{\overset{(p)}{q}_{AB}\big|_{\mathbf{S}_{2}}-\overset{(p)}{q}_{AB}\big|_{\mathbf{S}_{1}}+\text{known fields}}_{=:\overset{(p)}{r}_{AB}}

\displaystyle=\text{gauge fields }+\overset{(p)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\overset{[(7-n+2p)/2]}{{\hat{\varphi}}_{AB}}{}+m^{p}\overset{(p)}{\psi}_{[m]}\overset{[(7-n+2p(n-1))/2]}{{\hat{\varphi}}_{AB}}{}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j=1}^{p-1}m^{j}\overset{(p)}{\psi}_{j,0}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\overset{[p-\frac{n-7}{2}+j(n-2)]}{{\hat{\varphi}}_{AB}}{}\,,\quad 1\leq p\leq k\,,

(6.149)

where the “known fields” on the left-hand side refer to
the contribution to this equation from the field
v
; an analysis of the “gauge fields”
(coming from the gauge transformation of
\overset{(p)}{q}_{AB}\big|_{\mathbf{S}_{2}}
and the gauge corrections (
5.2
) at
r_{2}
of the field
\tilde{h}_{AB}
)
appearing on the right-hand side are provided in Appendix
F.1
.

The projection of (
6.149
) onto
{\mathrm{TT}}
can be solved using the fields
\overset{[(7-n+2p)/2]}{{\hat{\varphi}}_{AB}}{}{}^{[{\mathrm{TT}}]}
and
\overset{[(7-n+2p(n-1))/2]}{{\hat{\varphi}}_{AB}}{}{}^{[{\mathrm{TT}}]}
for each
1\leq p\leq k
according to:

\displaystyle\overset{(p)}{r}{}^{[{\mathrm{TT}}\cap(\ker\overset{(p)}{\psi})^{\perp}]}_{AB}

\displaystyle=\overset{(p)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\overset{[(7-n+2p)/2]}{{\hat{\varphi}}_{AB}}{}{}^{[{\mathrm{TT}}\cap(\ker\overset{(p)}{\psi})^{\perp}]}+m^{p}\underbrace{\overset{(p)}{\psi}_{[m]}\overset{[(7-n+2p(n-1))/2]}{{\hat{\varphi}}_{AB}}{}{}^{[{\mathrm{TT}}\cap(\ker\overset{(p)}{\psi})^{\perp}]}}_{\text{set }=0}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j=1}^{p-1}\overset{(p)}{\psi}_{j,0}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\underbrace{\overset{[p-\frac{n-7}{2}+j(n-2)]}{{\hat{\varphi}}_{AB}}{}{}^{[{\mathrm{TT}}\cap(\ker\overset{(p)}{\psi})^{\perp}]}}_{\text{set }=0}\,,

(6.150)

\displaystyle\overset{(p)}{r}{}^{[{\mathrm{TT}}\cap\ker\overset{(p)}{\psi}]}_{AB}

\displaystyle=m^{p}\overset{(p)}{\psi}_{[m]}\overset{[(7-n+2p(n-1))/2]}{{\hat{\varphi}}_{AB}}{}{}^{[{\mathrm{TT}}\cap\ker\overset{(p)}{\psi}]}+\sum_{j=1}^{p-1}\overset{(p)}{\psi}_{j,0}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\underbrace{\overset{[p-\frac{n-7}{2}+j(n-2)]}{{\hat{\varphi}}_{AB}}{}{}^{[{\mathrm{TT}}\cap\ker\overset{(p)}{\psi}]}}_{\text{set }=0}\,,

(6.151)

where we note that the projection of the “gauge fields” in the right-hand side of (
6.149
) onto
{\mathrm{TT}}
vanishes.

For consideration of the coupled system for
\overset{(p)}{H}{}^{[{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}]}_{uA}
and
\overset{(p)}{q}{}^{[{\mathrm{TT}^{\perp}}]}_{AB}
, it turns out to be convenient to write the interpolating fields
\overset{[p]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}{}^{[{\mathrm{TT}^{\perp}}]}
as

\displaystyle\overset{[p]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}{}^{[{\mathrm{TT}^{\perp}}]}=C(\overset{[p]}{w})

(6.152)

where
\overset{[p]}{w}\in{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}
is the unique vector solution to (
6.152
) (cf. Appendix
C.2
).

The following commutation relation will be useful:

\displaystyle\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}(\mathring{\Delta}+2\mathring{\mathscr{R}})\hat{\varphi}=(\mathring{\Delta}+(n-2){{\varepsilon}})\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\hat{\varphi}\,.

(6.153)

It implies

\displaystyle\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ(aP+\mathring{\Delta}+2\mathring{\mathscr{R}}+c)\hat{\varphi}=(a\,\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C+\mathring{\Delta}+(n-2){{\varepsilon}}+c)\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\hat{\varphi}\,.

(6.154)

We note the following implication of (
6.154
):

Proposition
6.7
.

\overset{(i)}{\psi}({\mathrm{TT}})\subset{\mathrm{TT}}\,,\qquad\overset{(i)}{\psi}({\mathrm{TT}^{\perp}})\subset{\mathrm{TT}^{\perp}}\,.

Proof.

Since the
\overset{(i)}{\psi}
’s are of the form

aP+\mathring{\Delta}+2\mathring{\mathscr{R}}+c
, the inclusion
\overset{(i)}{\psi}({\mathrm{TT}})\subset{\mathrm{TT}}
follows immediately from (
6.154
). The second inclusion follows from the fact that
aP+\mathring{\Delta}+2\mathring{\mathscr{R}}+c
is
formally self-adjoint.

Equation (
6.154
) and Proposition
6.7
allow us to write the divergence of the
{\mathrm{TT}^{\perp}}
projection of (
6.149
) as

\displaystyle\underbrace{{\mathring{D}}^{A}\overset{(p)}{r}{}^{[{\mathrm{TT}^{\perp}}]}_{AB}}_{=:\overset{(p)}{r}_{B}}

\displaystyle=\text{gauge fields }+\overset{(p)}{\tilde{\psi}}(\mathring{\Delta},\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C)\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C(\overset{[\frac{7-n+2p}{2}]}{w})

\displaystyle\quad+m^{p}\overset{(p)}{\psi}_{[m]}\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C(\overset{[\frac{7-n+2p(n-1)}{2}]}{w})

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j=1}^{p-1}m^{j}\overset{(p)}{\tilde{\psi}}_{j,0}(\mathring{\Delta},\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C)\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C(\overset{[p-\frac{n-7}{2}+j(n-2)]}{w})\,,\quad 1\leq p\leq k\,;

(6.155)

recall that the notation
\tilde{U}(\mathring{\Delta},\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C)
(a tilde over an operator
U
) denotes the replacement of all appearances of the operator
P:=C\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}
, respectively
\mathring{\mathscr{R}}
, in
U
by the operator
\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C
, respectively
1/2(n-2){{\varepsilon}}
(cf. (
6.154
)).
Similarly to the convenient case (cf. (
6.64
)), in order to simplify notation we group terms together and
rewrite (
6.155
) as

\displaystyle\overset{(p)}{r}_{B}=\text{gauge fields}+\sum_{j=\frac{7-n+2p}{2}}^{\frac{7-n+2p(n-1)}{2}}\overset{(p)}{\tilde{\psi}}_{j}\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C(\overset{[j]}{w})\,,\quad 1\leq p\leq k\,,

(6.156)

with some operators
\overset{(p)}{\tilde{\psi}}_{j}
.
Meanwhile, the integrated transport equation for
\overset{(p)}{H}{}^{[{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}]}_{uA}
is as given in (
6.60
), which we rewrite as:

\displaystyle\underbrace{\big(\overset{(p)}{H}_{uA}\big|_{\mathbf{S}_{2}}-\overset{(p)}{\tilde{H}}_{uA}\big|_{\tilde{\mathbf{S}}_{1}}+\text{known fields}\big)^{[{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}]}}_{=:\overset{(p)}{s}_{A}}

\displaystyle=(\text{gauge fields})^{[{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}]}+\overset{(p)}{\tilde{\chi}}(\mathring{\Delta},\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C)\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C(\overset{[p+4]}{w})+m^{p}\overset{(p)}{\tilde{\chi}}_{[m]}\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C(\overset{[p(n-1)+4]}{w})

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j=1}^{p-1}m^{j}\overset{(p)}{\chi}_{j,0}(\mathring{\Delta},\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C)\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C(\overset{[(p+4)+j(n-2)]}{w})\,,

(6.157)

where
1\leq p\leq k
and the “known fields” on the left-hand side refer to the
contribution to this equation from the
field
{\mathring{D}}^{A}v_{AB}^{[{\mathrm{TT}^{\perp}}]}

.
Similarly to the convenient case, we rewrite (
6.157
) as:

\displaystyle\overset{(p)}{s}_{A}

\displaystyle=(\text{gauge fields})^{[{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}]}+\sum_{i=5}^{p(n-1)+4}\overset{(p)}{\tilde{\chi}}_{i}\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C(\overset{[i]}{w})\,.

(6.158)

Recall that the spaces
S,V\subset H^{k}({\mathbf{S}})
,
k\geq 1
are defined as

S=\{\xi_{A}\in H^{k}({\mathbf{S}}):\xi_{A}={\mathring{D}}_{A}\phi\,,\phi\in H^{k+1}(\mathbf{S})\}\,,\quad V=\{\xi_{A}\in H^{k}({\mathbf{S}}):{\mathring{D}}^{A}\xi_{A}=0\}\,.

When
\mathbf{S}
is compact and boundaryless, the spaces
S
and
V
are
L^{2}
-orthogonal, and
any vector field
\xi\in H^{k}({\mathbf{S}})
, can be decomposed into its “scalar” and “vector” parts, denoted as

\displaystyle\xi=\xi^{[S]}+\xi^{[V]}\,.

(6.159)

We show in Appendix
C.3
that, under the current conditions, the scalar-vector decomposition of an element of
{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}
is compatible with this splitting. That is, if
\xi\in{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}
, then both

\xi^{[S]}\,,\xi^{[V]}\in{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}
as well.

We define the vector field
\upsilon_{k,2}
as

\displaystyle\upsilon_{k,2}^{[S]}=({\mathring{D}}_{A}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{u})^{[{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}]}\,,\quad\upsilon_{k,2}^{[V]}=\overset{[2]}{w}{}^{[V]}\,;

(6.160)

recall that the fields
\overset{[p]}{w}
have been defined in (
6.152
).

We can then write the coupled system as follows: for
n>5
we let

\displaystyle u_{k}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}(\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{r}\,,\overset{(1)}{r}\,,\dots\,,\overset{(\frac{n-5}{2})}{r}\,,\overset{(1)}{s}\,,\dots\,,\overset{(k)}{s})^{T},&\\
(\underbrace{\overset{(k)}{r}\,,\overset{(k-1)}{r}\,,\dots\,,\overset{(\frac{n+1}{2})}{r}}_{k_{n}\text{ terms}}\,,\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2})}{r}\,,\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{r}\,,\overset{(1)}{r}\,,\dots\,,\overset{(\frac{n-5}{2})}{r}\,,\overset{(1)}{s}\,,\dots\,,\overset{(k)}{s})^{T},&\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\upsilon_{k}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}(\upsilon_{k,2}\,,\overset{[\frac{9-n}{2}]}{w}\,,\dots\,,\overset{[1]}{w}\,,\overset{[5]}{w}\,,\dots\,\overset{[4+k]}{w})^{T},\\
(\partial^{k_{n}}_{u}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}_{A}\,,\partial^{k_{n}-1}_{u}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}_{A}\,,\dots\,,\partial_{u}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}_{A}\,,\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}_{A}\,,\upsilon_{k,2}\,,\overset{[\frac{9-n}{2}]}{w}\,,\dots\,,\overset{[1]}{w}\,,\overset{[5]}{w}\,,\dots\,\overset{[4+k]}{w})^{T[{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}]},\end{array}\right.

where the upper case holds for
k=\frac{n-3}{2}
and the lower for
k>\frac{n-3}{2}
, and
with

0\leq k_{n}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0,&\hbox{$k=\frac{n-3}{2}$;}\\
k-\frac{n-1}{2},&\hbox{$k\geq\frac{n-1}{2}$\,,}\end{array}\right.

(6.165)

where
\cdot^{T}
stands for transposition.
We will return to the case
n=5
shortly, cf. (
6.196
)-(
6.197
) below.
Note that
u_{k}
contains all the
\overset{(j)}{r}
’s and
\overset{(j)}{s}
’s,
j\in\{1,\ldots,k\}
, that
\overset{(2)}{w}{}^{[S]}
,
\overset{(3)}{w}
,
\overset{(4)}{w}
do not appear in the list, and that
\overset{(2)}{w}{}^{[V]}
enters through
v_{k,2}
;
this particular arrangement of terms in the vectors
u_{k}
and
v_{k}
is convenient for checking the ellipticity of the system.

We
claim, first,
that the system of coupled equations in (
6.156
) and (
6.158
) is equivalent to

\displaystyle u_{k}^{[X]}=\Lambda^{[X]}_{k}\upsilon_{k}^{[X]}+\mathcal{N}^{[X]}_{k}\rho_{k}^{[X]}\,,\quad X\in\{V,S\}\,,

(6.166)

where the
\Lambda^{[X]}_{k}
’s are some
2k\times 2k
matrices of operators,
\mathcal{N}^{[X]}_{k}
’s are
2k\times k(n-2)
matrices of operators and

\displaystyle\rho_{k}:=\begin{pmatrix}\overset{[5+k]}{w}\\
\overset{[6+k]}{w}\\
\vdots\\
\overset{[4+k(n-1)]}{w}\end{pmatrix}\,.

(6.167)

For this
we need to show that fields
\upsilon_{k}^{[S]}
and
\rho_{k}^{[S]}
only produce
u_{k}^{[S]}
, that is,

\displaystyle(\Lambda^{[S]}_{k}\upsilon_{k}^{[S]})^{[V]}=0\,,\qquad(\mathcal{N}^{[S]}_{k}\rho_{k}^{[S]})^{[V]}=0\,;

(6.168)

similarly for
\upsilon_{k}^{[V]}
,
\rho_{k}^{[V]}
, and
u_{k}^{[V]}
.
This can be justified by first noting
that the operators appearing in (
6.156
) and (
6.158
) are sums of products of elliptic, self-adjoint, pairwise commuting operators of the form

\displaystyle\tilde{\operatorname{L}}_{a,c,b}:

\displaystyle X\rightarrow X\,,\quad X\in\{S,V\}\,,

\displaystyle\tilde{\operatorname{L}}_{a,c,b}(\xi)=

\displaystyle a\,\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C(\xi)+(\delta_{1b}\mathring{\Delta}+c)\xi

(6.169)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\big(\frac{a(n-2)}{n-1}(\mathring{\Delta}+{{\varepsilon}})+\delta_{1b}\mathring{\Delta}+c\big)\xi\,,&\hbox{when restricted to $S$;}\\
\big(\frac{a}{2}(\mathring{\Delta}+(n-2){{\varepsilon}})+\delta_{1b}\mathring{\Delta}+c\big)\xi\,,&\hbox{when restricted to $V$,}\end{array}\right.

with
a,c\in\mathbb{R}
and
b\in\{0,1\}
,
where
\delta_{ij}
is the usual Kronecker delta. The second equality follows from the results in Appendices
C.4
-
C.6
.
Equation (
6.168
) and its
[V]\leftrightarrow[S]
equivalent is then established by noting that the inclusion
\tilde{\operatorname{L}}_{a,c,b}V\subset V
follows from (
6.4.3
) and the commutation relation (
I.3
), p.
I.3
, while
\tilde{\operatorname{L}}_{a,c,b}S\subset S
follows from (
6.4.3
) and the commutation relation (
I.1
).

Now, it follows from (
6.4.3
) that
the system (
6.166
) can be decomposed using
eigenvectors of
\mathring{\Delta}
.
Let therefore
\{\eta_{\ell}\}
be a complete set of smooth,
pairwise
L^{2}
-orthogonal,
eigenvectors of
\mathring{\Delta}
, with a corresponding discrete set of eigenvalues
\lambda_{\ell}\to_{\ell\to\infty}-\infty
.
We can then write, for
X\in\{S,V\}
,

\displaystyle u^{[X]}_{k}=\sum_{\ell}u^{[X]}_{k,\ell}\eta_{\ell}\,,\quad\upsilon^{[X]}_{k}=\sum_{\ell}\upsilon^{[X]}_{k,\ell}\eta_{\ell}\,,\quad\rho^{[X]}_{k}=\sum_{\ell}\rho^{[X]}_{k,\ell}\eta_{\ell}\,,

(6.172)

and we will show that equation (
6.166
) can be solved mode-by-mode:

\displaystyle u_{k,\ell}^{[X]}=\Lambda^{[X]}_{k}\upsilon_{k,\ell}^{[X]}+\mathcal{N}^{[X]}_{k}\rho_{k,\ell}^{[X]}\iff u_{k,\ell}^{[X]}=\Lambda^{[X]}_{k}|_{\mathring{\Delta}\mapsto\lambda_{\ell}}\upsilon_{k,\ell}^{[X]}+\mathcal{N}^{[X]}_{k}|_{\mathring{\Delta}\mapsto\lambda_{\ell}}\rho_{k,\ell}^{[X]}\,.

(6.173)

Note that since
{\mathrm{CKV}}=\ker\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C=\ker\tilde{\operatorname{L}}_{1,0,0}
by (
6.169
) and by Proposition
C.1
, p.
C.1
, we see that conformal Killing vectors are eigenvectors of
\mathring{\Delta}
. Since the source terms
u^{[X]}_{k}\in{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}
, we only need to consider the modes for which
\eta_{\ell}\in{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}
.

The remaining argument for the solvability of equation (
6.166
) is similar to the convenient case.
We show in Appendix
F.1
how to assign to
\Lambda^{[X]}_{k}
integers
s_{i}
and
t_{j}
so
that the operators
\Lambda^{[X]}_{k}
in (
6.166
) are elliptic in the sense of Agmon, Douglis and Nirenberg (ADN).
As explained in Appendix
F.2
, p.
F.2
,
this implies that there exists
N(k)
such that we can find a unique solution of (
6.173
) with
\rho^{[X]}_{k,\ell}=0
for all
\ell>N(k)
.
It follows from the ADN estimates that the sum of these terms converges when the source terms are sufficiently regular, in Sobolev spaces made precise by Theorem
F.1
.
It remains to show that (
6.173
) can be solved in the finite dimensional space of smooth fields
u^{[X]}_{k}
,
\upsilon^{[X]}_{k}
and
\rho^{[X]}_{k}
of the form

u^{[X]}_{k}=\sum_{\ell\leq N(k)}u^{[X]}_{k,\ell}\eta_{\ell}\,,\quad\upsilon^{[X]}_{k}=\sum_{\ell\leq N(k)}\upsilon^{[X]}_{k,\ell}\eta_{\ell}\,,\quad\rho^{[X]}_{k}=\sum_{\ell\leq N(k)}\rho^{[X]}_{k,\ell}\eta_{\ell}\,.

(6.174)

This is equivalent to the requirement that all the linear maps obtained by juxtaposing
\Lambda^{[X]}_{k}|_{\mathring{\Delta}\mapsto\lambda_{\ell}}
and
\mathcal{N}^{[X]}_{k}|_{\mathring{\Delta}\mapsto\lambda_{\ell}}
with
\ell<N(k)
and
\eta_{\ell}\in{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}
are surjective.
This, in turn, is equivalent to the fact that the adjoints of these linear maps have no kernel. In what follows, we leave out the “
|_{\mathring{\Delta}\mapsto\lambda_{\ell}}
” to ease notation.

It turns out that the question of surjectivity is easier to analyse if we carry out a permutation of the rows and columns of the system (
6.166
) as follows: let
\sigma_{1}
and
\sigma_{2}
be permutations of
\{1,2,...,2k\}
such that the permuted vectors
(\hat{u}_{k})_{i}:=(u_{k})_{\sigma_{1}(i)}
and
(\hat{\upsilon}_{k})_{i}:=(\upsilon_{k})_{\sigma_{2}(i)}
read

\displaystyle\hat{u}_{k}

\displaystyle=(\overset{(1)}{r}\,,\overset{(1)}{s}\,,\overset{(2)}{r}\,,\overset{(2)}{s}\,,\dots\,,\overset{(k)}{r}\,,\overset{(k)}{s})^{T}\,,

(6.175)

\displaystyle\hat{\upsilon}_{k}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}(\upsilon_{k,2}\,,\overset{[\frac{9-n}{2}]}{w}\,,\dots\,,\overset{[1]}{w}\,,\overset{[5]}{w}\,,\dots\,\overset{[4+k]}{w})^{T},&\hbox{$k=\frac{n-3}{2}$;}\\
(\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}_{A}\,,\upsilon_{k,2}\,,\partial^{k_{n}}_{u}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}_{A}\,,\partial^{k_{n}-1}_{u}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}_{A}\,,\dots\,,\partial_{u}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}_{A}\,,\overset{[\frac{9-n}{2}]}{w}\,,\dots\,,\overset{[1]}{w}\,,\overset{[5]}{w}\,,\dots\,\overset{[4+k]}{w})^{T[{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}]},&\hbox{$k>\frac{n-3}{2}$.}\end{array}\right.

Then
(\Lambda^{[X]}_{k}\,\mathcal{N}^{[X]}_{k})
is surjective iff

(\hat{\Lambda}^{[X]}_{k}\,\hat{\mathcal{N}}^{[X]}_{k})

is, where
(\hat{\Lambda}_{k}^{[X]})^{i}{}_{j}:=({\Lambda}_{k}^{[X]})^{\sigma_{1}(i)}{}_{\sigma_{2}(j)}

and

(\hat{\mathcal{N}}^{[X]}_{k})^{i}{}_{j}:=({\mathcal{N}}^{[X]}_{k})^{\sigma_{1}(i)}{}_{j}
.
The benefit in doing so is that the permuted matrix
(\hat{\Lambda}^{[X]}_{k}\,\hat{\mathcal{N}}^{[X]}_{k})^{\dagger}
then has a similar structure to that in (
6.4.1
). Specifically, it can be verified that

(\hat{\Lambda}^{[X]}_{k}\,\hat{\mathcal{N}}^{[X]}_{k})^{\dagger}=\begin{pmatrix}F^{[X]}\\
G^{[X]}\end{pmatrix}\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C\,,

(6.178)

with
G^{[X]}
given by

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{l}\begin{array}[]{|cccccc|}\hline\cr\overset{(1)}{\tilde{\psi}}_{\frac{9-n}{2}}&\overset{(1)}{\tilde{\chi}}_{\frac{9-n}{2}}&\dots\dots&\ldots\dots&\dots\quad\dots\quad\dots\quad\dots\quad\ldots&\,\overset{(k)}{\tilde{\chi}}_{\frac{9-n}{2}}\\
\vdots&\vdots&&&&\,\vdots\\
\framebox{ $\overset{(1)}{\tilde{\psi}}_{\frac{7-n}{2}+(n-1)}$ }&\overset{(1)}{\tilde{\chi}}_{\frac{7-n}{2}+(n-1)}&\dots\dots&\ldots\dots&\dots\quad\dots\quad\dots\quad\dots\quad\ldots&\,\overset{(k)}{\tilde{\chi}}_{{\frac{7-n}{2}+(n-1)}}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\\
\\
\begin{array}[]{|cccccc|}\hline\cr\phantom{\overset{(k)}{\tilde{\chi}}_{,kkk}}\,0&\hskip 42.67912pt\overset{(1)}{\tilde{\chi}}_{{\frac{9-n}{2}+(n-1)}}&\dots&\dots&\ldots\quad\dots\quad\dots\quad\ldots\quad\dots\quad\ldots&\overset{(k)}{\tilde{\chi}}_{{\frac{9-n}{2}+(n-1)}}\\
\phantom{\overset{(k)}{\tilde{\chi}}_{,kkk}}\,\vdots&\hskip 56.9055pt\vdots&&&&\vdots\\
\phantom{\overset{(k)}{\tilde{\chi}}_{,kkk}}\,0&\hskip 42.67912pt\framebox{$\overset{(1)}{\tilde{\chi}}_{(n-1)+4}$}&\dots&\dots&\ldots\quad\dots\quad\dots\quad\ldots\quad\dots\quad\ldots&\overset{(k)}{\tilde{\chi}}_{(n-1)+4}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\\
\begin{array}[]{cccccc}&&&\quad\vdots&&\end{array}\\
\begin{array}[]{|ccccccc|}\hline\cr\phantom{\overset{(k)}{\tilde{\chi}}_{,kkk}}\,0&&0&\ldots\quad\ldots\quad\ldots\quad\dots\dots&0&\hskip 8.5359pt\overset{(k)}{\tilde{\psi}}_{(k-1)(n-1)+5}&\overset{(k)}{\tilde{\chi}}_{(k-1)(n-1)+5}\\
\phantom{\overset{(k)}{\tilde{\chi}}_{,kkk}}\,\vdots&&\vdots&\ddots\quad\ddots\quad\ddots\quad\ddots\quad\ddots&\vdots&\,\vdots&\vdots\\
\phantom{\overset{(k)}{\tilde{\chi}}_{,kkk}}\,0&&0&\ldots\quad\ldots\quad\ldots\quad\dots\dots&0&\hskip 8.5359pt\framebox{$\overset{(k)}{\tilde{\psi}}_{\frac{7-n}{2}+k(n-1)}$}&\overset{(k)}{\tilde{\chi}}_{\frac{7-n}{2}+k(n-1)}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\\
\\
\begin{array}[]{|ccccccc|}\hline\cr\phantom{\overset{(k)}{\tilde{\chi}}_{,kkk}}\,0&&0&\ldots\quad\ldots\quad\ldots\quad\dots\dots&0&\hskip 39.83368pt0&\hskip 34.14322pt\overset{(k)}{\tilde{\chi}}_{\frac{9-n}{2}+k(n-1)}\\
\phantom{\overset{(k)}{\tilde{\chi}}_{,kkk}}\,\vdots&&\vdots&\ddots\quad\ddots\quad\ddots\quad\ddots\quad\ddots&\vdots&\hskip 39.83368pt\vdots&\hskip 34.14322pt\vdots\\
\phantom{\overset{(k)}{\tilde{\chi}}_{,kkk}}\,0&&0&\ldots\quad\ldots\quad\ldots\quad\dots\dots&0&\hskip 42.67912pt0&\hskip 34.14322pt\framebox{$\overset{(k)}{\tilde{\chi}}_{k(n-1)+4}$}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}\end{array}\right)\,,

where all the operators appearing in (
6.178
) and (
6.4.3
) are understood to be the associated restrictions onto the space
X\in\{S,V\}
.
In addition, the boxed entries in (
6.4.3
) are all non-vanishing numbers (cf. items (iii) of Sections (
6.4
), p.
6.4
and (
6.4.2
), p.
6.4.2
). It follows readily that
G^{[X]}\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C
,
and hence

(\hat{\Lambda}^{[X]}_{k}\,\hat{\mathcal{N}}^{[X]}_{k})^{\dagger}
, has trivial kernel
on modes
\eta_{\ell}\in{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}
.

We show in Appendix
F.3
that the resulting solution lies in the expected spaces, specifically

\displaystyle j\in[1,k_{n}]\cap\mathbb{N}:\ \partial_{u}^{j}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}_{A}\in H^{k_{\gamma}+1-2j}(\mathbf{S})\,,

(6.194)

\displaystyle\xi^{A}\in H^{k_{\gamma}+1}(\mathbf{S})\,,\quad\xi^{u}\in H^{k_{\gamma}+2}(\mathbf{S})\,,\quad\overset{[p]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}{}^{[{\mathrm{TT}^{\perp}}]}\in H^{k_{\gamma}}(\mathbf{S})\,.

(6.195)

The case
n=5
.

In this case, we have
\frac{n-3}{2}=1
. The above analysis can be easily adapted to this case, by removing the rows and columns in the matrix
\Lambda{}^{[X]}_{k}
(cf. Figure
F.1
) associated to the equations for
\overset{(p)}{r}
with
“
1\leq p\leq\frac{n-5}{2}=0
”. Specifically, instead of (
6.4.3
)-(
6.4.3
) we let

\displaystyle u_{k}

\displaystyle:=\begin{cases}(\overset{(2)}{r}\,,\overset{(1)}{r}\,,\overset{(1)}{s}\,,\dots\,,\overset{(k)}{s})^{T},&k=2\,,\\
(\underbrace{\overset{(k)}{r}\,,\overset{(k-1)}{r}\,,\dots\,,\overset{(3)}{r}}_{k_{5}\text{ terms}}\,,\overset{(2)}{r}\,,\overset{(1)}{r}\,,\overset{(1)}{s}\,,\dots\,,\overset{(k)}{s})^{T},&k>2\,,\end{cases}

(6.196)

\displaystyle\upsilon_{k}

\displaystyle:=\begin{cases}(\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}_{A}\,,\upsilon_{k,2}\,,\overset{[5]}{w}\,,\dots\,\overset{[4+k]}{w})^{T[{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}]},&k=2\,,\\
(\partial^{k_{5}}_{u}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}_{A}\,,\partial^{k_{5}-1}_{u}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}_{A}\,,\dots\,,\partial_{u}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}_{A}\,,\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}_{A}\,,\upsilon_{k,2}\,,\overset{[5]}{w}\,,\dots\,\overset{[4+k]}{w})^{T[{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}]},&k>2\,,\end{cases}

(6.197)

with
k_{5}
as given in (
6.165
). The analysis then proceeds analogously to the above.

7
Unobstructed gluing with
m\neq 0

In this section we conclude by briefly discussing how to remove the obstructions to
C^{k}_{u}\,C^{\infty}_{(r,x^{A})}
-gluing imposed by the radially conserved charges (compare with the results summarised in Tables
1.1
and
1.3
). As in previous work
ChCong1
, the simplest way to fill these gaps is to exploit the linear nature of the field equations and add fields satisfying the linearized equations and whose charges are specifically chosen to compensate for the obstructions.

For brevity, we shall focus only on the case
m\neq 0
, as in Tables
1.1
-
1.2
,
ignoring those radial charges which occur in Table
1.3
when
m=0
.
Then we only have two sets of obstructions to gluing (defined in (
3.55
) and (
3.73
)) which we reproduce here:

\overset{[1]}{Q}(\pi^{A}):=\int_{\mathbf{S}}\pi^{A}\overset{(*)}{H}_{uA}=\int_{\mathbf{S}}\pi^{A}\left[r^{n+1}\partial_{r}(r^{-2}h_{uA})-2{r^{n-1}}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\delta\beta\right]\,d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}\,,

and

\overset{[2]}{Q}(\lambda):=\int_{\mathbf{S}}\lambda\Big[r^{n-3}\delta V-\frac{r^{n-2}}{n-1}\partial_{r}\big(r^{2}{\mathring{D}}^{A}\delta U_{A}\big)-\tfrac{2r^{n-2}}{n-1}\mathring{\Delta}\delta\beta-2r^{n-3}V\delta\beta\Big]d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}\,.

Here
\pi^{A}\in{\mathrm{CKV}}
, while

\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\lambda]=0\,.

Note again that, on a compact Einstein manifold, the fields
\lambda
solving this last equation
are only non-constant on the
(n-1)
-sphere, where they are linear combinations of
\ell=0,1
spherical harmonics.

Now, as in
ChCong1
, we can differentiate the Birmingham–Kottler metrics with respect to the mass to obtain one family of such data:

\frac{d}{dm}\Big[\big({\varepsilon}-{\alpha^{2}}r^{2}{-\frac{2m}{r^{n-2}}}\big)du^{2}-2du\,dr+r^{2}{\mathring{\gamma}}_{AB}dx^{A}dx^{B}\Big]=-\frac{2}{r^{n-2}}du^{2}\,.

(7.1)

This family can be then used to match all of the
\overset{[2]}{Q}(\lambda=\text{const.})
charges.

Likewise, a stationary class of data is provided by differentiating the higher dimensional Kerr-(anti) de Sitter metrics with respect to each angular-momentum parameter associated with its respective axis of symmetry. We provide this construction in Bondi coordinates and generalise the linear solution to include Ricci-flat topologies in Appendix
H
.
This leads to the metric perturbation

\delta V=0=\delta\beta\,,\quad\delta U^{A}=-\frac{\mathring{\lambda}^{A}(x^{C})}{r^{n}}\,,

(7.2)

where
\mathring{\lambda}^{A}
is any
u
-independent Killing vector on
\mathbf{S}
. This represents angular momentum about a particular axis of rotation.
One can check that the linearised Einstein equations, discussed in Section
3
, remain satisfied.
When
m\neq 0
, it turns out, we need only the linear combination of these two families:

\mathring{h}={r^{-(n-2)}}\left(\mathring{\mu}\,du^{2}-2\mathring{\lambda}_{A}(x^{C})\,dx^{A}du\right)\,.

(7.3)

To see this for the first charge
\overset{[1]}{Q}(\pi)
note that, when
m\neq 0
, we have used in (
6.35
) the gauge field
(\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{u})^{[=1]}
to match all of charges associated with the proper conformal Killing vector fields (i.e.
{\mathring{D}}_{A}\pi^{A}\neq 0
which only exist on
\mathbf{S}
when it is the round sphere
S^{n-1}
). This leaves only the obstructions arising from the Killing vectors as in (
7.2
). This charge evaluated on the compensating perturbation (
7.3
) is simply

\overset{[1]}{Q}(\pi)={n}\int_{\mathbf{S}}\pi^{A}\lambda_{A}\,d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}\,.

(7.4)

Finally to see this for the second charge
\overset{[2]}{Q}(\lambda)
note that, when
m\neq 0
, on
S^{n-1}
in (
6.34
) we have matched part of the charge,
\overset{[2]}{Q}{(\lambda^{[=1]})}{}
, using the gauge field
({\mathring{D}}_{B}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{B})^{[=1]}
. Thus it remains to match the
\lambda=const.
charges which is achieved with
\mathring{\mu}
. In this case the charge associated with the compensating perturbation (
7.3
) is simply

\overset{[2]}{Q}{(1)}{}=-\int_{\mathbf{S}}\,\mathring{\mu}\,d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}=\mathring{\mu}\,|S^{n-1}|\,,

(7.5)

where
|S^{n-1}|
is the
(n-1)
-dimensional volume of the
S^{n-1}
sphere.

This completes the removal of obstructions associated with linearised
C^{k}_{u}\,C^{\infty}_{(r,x^{A})}
-gluing when
m\neq 0
.

Appendix A
Transport equations involving
\partial_{u}^{i}\check{h}_{uA}
and
\partial^{i+1}_{u}h_{AB}

In this Appendix we derive the transport equations given in Section
4
of the main text.
The guiding principle behind the calculations that follow is, that an induction argument to determine the higher order
u
-derivatives of the fields will require calculating
r^{k}\partial_{u}h_{AB}
as a sum of terms of the form
r^{k^{\prime}}L_{kk^{\prime}}h_{AB}
, where
L_{kk^{\prime}}
is a partial differential operator in the
x^{A}
-variables,
together with a term which integrates-out in
r
to a boundary term determined by the boundary data.

Suppose that

\mycal E_{rA}=0
and
\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[\mycal E_{AB}]=0
.

Assume, first, that

\frac{\mathbbm{Z}}{2}\ni k\not\in\{-3,n-3,(n-7)/2\}\,.

(A.1)

We claim that we can then write

\displaystyle r^{k}\partial_{u}h_{AB}=\partial_{r}\hat{q}_{AB}^{(k)}+\mathcal{K}\left(k,\mathring{\Delta},P\right)r^{k-1}h_{AB}+\alpha^{2}\mathcal{K}_{[\alpha]}(k)r^{k+1}h_{AB}+m\mathcal{K}_{[m]}(k)r^{k+1-n}h_{AB}\,,

(A.2)

for a field
\hat{q}_{AB}^{(k)}
, some functions
\mathcal{K}_{[\alpha]}(k)
,
\mathcal{K}_{[m]}(k)
and operators
\mathcal{K}\left(k,\mathring{\Delta},P\right)
.
(As suggested by the notation,
\alpha^{2}\mathcal{K}_{[\alpha]}(k)
is the term involving
\alpha
and
m\mathcal{K}_{[m]}(k)
is that involving
m
; in the linear approximation considered here no terms mixing
\alpha
and
m
occur).

For this recall (
3.100
), which we reproduce here with a convenient rearrangement of terms:

\displaystyle 0=\

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\Big[r^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\partial_{u}\check{h}_{AB}-\frac{2}{(n+1)r^{(n+1)/2}}\partial_{r}(r^{n-1}TS\big[{\mathring{D}}_{A}h_{uB}])

\displaystyle\qquad\underbrace{-\frac{r^{\frac{n-3}{2}}}{2}V\partial_{r}\check{h}_{AB}-\frac{n-1}{4}r^{\frac{n-5}{2}}V\check{h}_{AB}+\frac{2r^{\frac{n-3}{2}}}{n+1}P\check{h}_{AB}}_{=:\tilde{q}_{AB}}\Big]

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{8}\left(n^{2}-1\right)\alpha^{2}r^{\frac{n-5}{2}}h_{AB}+\frac{(n-1)^{2}}{4r^{(n+5)/2}}mh_{AB}

\displaystyle+\bigg[\frac{4}{n+1}P-\mathring{\mathscr{R}}-\frac{1}{2}\mathring{\Delta}+\frac{(n-3)(n-1){{\varepsilon}}}{8}\bigg]r^{(n-9)/2}h_{AB}\,,

(A.3)

Recall also (
3.60
):

\displaystyle 0=\partial_{r}\bigg(n\check{h}_{uA}+r\partial_{r}\check{h}_{uA}-\frac{1}{r^{3}}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}\bigg)-\frac{1}{r^{4}}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}\,.

(A.4)

It can be verified by a lengthy calculation, similar to that in Appendix E
of
ChCong1
,
that

\displaystyle\quad r^{k}\partial_{u}h_{AB}+B_{k}r^{k-\frac{n-7}{2}}\times\text{RHS of }\eqref{3III23.4}+H_{k}r^{k+3}\times C\big[\text{RHS of }\eqref{3III23.3}\big]

\displaystyle=\partial_{r}\bigg[\ E_{k}r^{k+1}\partial_{u}h_{AB}+G_{k}r^{5-n+k+\frac{n-1}{k+4-n}}\partial_{r}(r^{\frac{n-1}{n-k-4}+n-3}C(h_{uA}))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+B_{k}r^{k-\frac{n-7}{2}}\tilde{q}_{AB}-H_{k}r^{k}P(h)_{AB}+\frac{r^{k}}{2}({\varepsilon}-\alpha^{2}r^{2}-\frac{2m}{r^{n-2}})h_{AB}\ \bigg]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathcal{K}\left(k,\mathring{\Delta},P\right)r^{k-1}h_{AB}+\alpha^{2}\mathcal{K}_{[\alpha]}(k)r^{k+1}h_{AB}+m\,\mathcal{K}_{[m]}(k)r^{k+1-n}h_{AB}\,,

(A.5)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}\left(k,\mathring{\Delta},P\right)

\displaystyle:=-\frac{1}{7-n+2k}\bigg[\frac{2(n-1)P}{(3+k)(3-n+k)}+2\mathring{\mathscr{R}}+\mathring{\Delta}-(n-4-k)(2+k){{\varepsilon}}\bigg]\,,

(A.6)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{[\alpha]}(k)

\displaystyle:=\frac{(k+4)(n-k-4)}{n-7-2k}\,,

(A.7)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{[m]}(k)

\displaystyle:=\frac{2(4-n+k)^{2}}{7-n+2k}\,,

(A.8)

and

\displaystyle B_{k}

\displaystyle:=-\frac{2}{n-7-2k}=:E_{k}\,,

\displaystyle H_{k}

\displaystyle:=\frac{2(n-2-k)}{(n+1)(k+3)(n-3-k)}\,,\quad G_{k}:=\frac{2(n-4-k)}{(k+3)(n-7-2k)(n-3-k)}\,.

(A.9)

We thus obtain the desired form (
A.2
) with

\displaystyle\hat{q}_{AB}^{(k)}

\displaystyle:=\ E_{k}r^{k+1}\partial_{u}h_{AB}+G_{k}r^{5-n+k+\frac{n-1}{k+4-n}}\partial_{r}(r^{\frac{n-1}{n-k-4}+n-3}C(h_{uA}))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+B_{k}r^{k-\frac{n-7}{2}}\tilde{q}_{AB}-H_{k}r^{k}P(h)_{AB}+\frac{r^{k}}{2}({\varepsilon}-\alpha^{2}r^{2}-\frac{2m}{r^{n-2}})h_{AB}\,.

(A.10)

In the special case
k=-3
we define

\displaystyle\mathring{\mathcal{K}}\left(-3,\mathring{\Delta},P\right)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{n-1}\bigg[\frac{(n(1-n)-2)P}{n}+2\mathring{\mathscr{R}}+\mathring{\Delta}+(n-1){{\varepsilon}}\bigg]\,,

(A.11)

\displaystyle H_{-3}

\displaystyle:=\frac{n^{2}-5}{1-n^{2}}\,,\quad G_{-3}:=(n-1)-\frac{2(n-2)}{(n-1)n}\,,

(A.12)

and

\displaystyle\hat{q}^{(-3)}_{AB}

\displaystyle:=E_{-3}r^{-2}\partial_{u}h_{AB}+\partial_{r}(r^{-1}C(h_{uA}))+\frac{2}{n}\partial_{r}\bigg(\log{(\tfrac{r}{r_{2}})}r^{-1}C(h_{uA})\bigg)+G_{-3}r^{-2}C(h_{uA})

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2(n-1)}{n}r^{-2}\log{(\tfrac{r}{r_{2}})}C(h_{uA})+B_{-3}r^{(1-n)/2}\tilde{q}_{AB}

\displaystyle\quad-H_{-3}r^{-3}P(h)_{AB}-\frac{2}{n}\log{(\tfrac{r}{r_{2}})}r^{-3}P(h)_{AB}+\frac{r^{-3}}{2}({\varepsilon}-\alpha^{2}r^{2}-\frac{2m}{r^{n-2}})h_{AB}\,.

(A.13)

The analysis in Appendices
A.1
-
A.2
shows that the remaining cases excluded in (
A.1
) do not occur in our context.

Anticipating, we note that the
\log
-terms appearing in(
A
) will not contribute to the final recurrence, because of some (unexpected) cancellations which will be established shortly.

In any case, it can be verified by another straightforward calculation that

\displaystyle\quad r^{-3}\partial_{u}h_{AB}+B_{-3}r^{-\frac{n-1}{2}}\times\text{RHS of }\eqref{3III23.4}-H_{-3}\times C\big[\text{RHS of }\eqref{3III23.3}\big]

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{n}\log{(\tfrac{r}{r_{2}})}\times C\big[\text{RHS of }\eqref{3III23.3}\big]=\partial_{r}\hat{q}^{(-3)}_{AB}+\mathring{\mathcal{K}}\left(-3,\mathring{\Delta},P\right)r^{-4}h_{AB}-\frac{2}{n}\log{(\tfrac{r}{r_{2}})}r^{-4}P(h)_{AB}

\displaystyle\phantom{+\frac{2}{n}\log{(\tfrac{r}{r_{2}})}\times C\big[\text{RHS of }\eqref{3III23.3}\big]=}+\alpha^{2}\mathcal{K}_{[\alpha]}(-3)r^{-2}h_{AB}+m\,\mathcal{K}_{[m]}(-3)r^{-2-n}h_{AB}\,,

(A.14)

and thus in particular,

\displaystyle r^{-3}\partial_{u}h_{AB}

\displaystyle=\partial_{r}\hat{q}^{(-3)}_{AB}+\bigg[\mathring{\mathcal{K}}\left(-3,\mathring{\Delta},P\right)-\frac{2}{n}\log{(\tfrac{r}{r_{2}})}P\bigg]r^{-4}h_{AB}

\displaystyle\quad+\alpha^{2}\mathcal{K}_{[\alpha]}(-3)r^{-2}h_{AB}+m\,\mathcal{K}_{[m]}(-3)r^{-2-n}h_{AB}\,.

(A.15)

We note that the operators
\mathcal{K}\left(k,\mathring{\Delta},P\right)
and
\mathring{\mathcal{K}}\left(-3,\mathring{\Delta},P\right)
are self-adjoint and commute with each other.
This is shown explicitly in Appendix
I
below, and ultimately arises from the fact that these operators depend on
P
and the special combination
\mathring{\mathscr{R}}+\frac{1}{2}\mathring{\Delta}
such that
[P,\mathring{\mathscr{R}}+\frac{1}{2}\mathring{\Delta}](h)=0
see (
I
).

A.1
Integral formulae involving
\partial_{u}^{i}h_{uA}

First, taking the
u
-derivative of (
A.4
) and making use of (
A.2
), we have,

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\bigg(\partial_{u}\overset{(0)}{H}_{uA}-{\mathring{D}}^{B}\hat{q}^{(-4)}_{AB}\bigg)

\displaystyle={\mathring{D}}^{B}\mathcal{K}\left(-4,\mathring{\Delta},P\right)r^{-5}h_{AB}+m\mathcal{K}_{[m]}(-4)r^{-3-n}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}\,,

(A.16)

where we made use of the fact that
\mathcal{K}_{[\alpha]}(-4)=0
.

Assuming
\mycal E_{rA}=0
and
\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[\mycal E_{AB}]=0
hold, then it follows by induction that
\partial_{u}^{i}\mycal E_{rA}=0
hold if and only if

\displaystyle\forall\ i\geq 1\quad\partial_{r}\overset{(i)}{H}_{uA}

\displaystyle=r^{-(i+4)}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\big(\overset{(i)}{\chi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,h_{AB}\big)+m^{i}\overset{(i)}{\chi}_{[m]}r^{-(4+i(n-1))}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j,\ell}^{i_{*}}m^{j}\alpha^{2\ell}r^{-(i+4)-j(n-2)+2\ell}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\big(\overset{(i)}{\chi}_{j,\ell}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,h_{AB}\big)\,,

(A.17)

where
\sum_{j,\ell}^{i_{*}}
denotes the sum over
j,\ell
, with

1\leq j\leq i-1\,,\ j+\ell\leq i\,,\ \text{and }0\leq 2\ell\leq i+j(n-2)\,.

(A.18)

In the above, the fields
\overset{(i)}{H}_{uA}
depend on
(r,\partial_{u}^{j}h_{uA},\partial_{r}\partial_{u}^{j}h_{uA},\partial^{j}_{u}h_{AB})_{j=0}^{i}
, with
\overset{(i)}{\chi}_{[m]}\in\mathbb{R}
, and with
\overset{(i)}{\chi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)
, respectively
\overset{(i)}{\chi}_{j,\ell}(\mathring{\Delta},P)
, being polynomials in
P
of order
i
, respectively
i-j-\ell\leq i-1
. We have the recursion formulae:

\displaystyle\overset{(0)}{H}_{uA}

\displaystyle:=H_{uA}\,,\quad\overset{(1)}{H}_{uA}:=\partial_{u}H_{uA}-{\mathring{D}}^{B}\hat{q}^{(-4)}_{AB}

(A.19)

\displaystyle\overset{(1)}{\chi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{K}\left(-4,\mathring{\Delta},P\right)\,,\quad\overset{(1)}{\chi}_{[m]}=\mathcal{K}_{[m]}(-4)\,,

(A.20)

\displaystyle\overset{(i+1)}{\chi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)

\displaystyle=\overset{(i)}{\chi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\mathcal{K}\left(-(i+4),\mathring{\Delta},P\right)\,,\quad\overset{(i+1)}{\chi}_{[m]}=\overset{(i)}{\chi}_{[m]}\mathcal{K}_{[m]}(-(4+i(n-1)))

(A.21)

\displaystyle\overset{(i+1)}{H}_{uA}

\displaystyle=\partial_{u}\overset{(i)}{H}_{uA}-{\mathring{D}}^{B}\overset{(i)}{\chi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\hat{q}^{(-i-4)}_{AB}-\overset{(i)}{\chi}_{[m]}m^{i}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\hat{q}^{(-4-i(n-1))}_{AB}

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{j,\ell}^{i_{*}}m^{j}\alpha^{2\ell}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\overset{(i)}{\chi}_{j,\ell}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\hat{q}^{(-(i+4)-j(n-2)+2\ell)}_{AB}

\displaystyle\quad-\alpha^{2}\mathcal{K}_{[\alpha]}(-(i+4))\mathcal{\tilde{K}}\left(-(i+3),\mathring{\Delta},\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C\right)\overset{(i-1)}{H}_{uA}\,,

(A.22)

with the operator
\mathcal{\tilde{K}}\left(k,\mathring{\Delta},\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C\right)
defined in (
A.24
) below.

A comment on the powers of
r
appearing in (
A.17
), and in several similar formulae below, is in order: all the terms appearing in equations such as (
A.17
) should have the same dimension, keeping in mind that the mass coefficient
m
has the same dimension as
r^{n-2}
, while
\alpha
has the same dimension as
r^{-1}
. Hence
m^{j}\alpha^{2\ell}r^{-j(n-2)+2\ell}
is dimensionless, as required.

The last condition in (
A.18
), which is justified below, is the same as
-(i+4)-j(n-2)+2\ell\leq-4
, which expresses the fact that the powers of
r
in the sum do not exceed
-4
. It prevents the appearance of
\log
terms in the current recursion.
Also note that, since
j\leq i-1
,

-(i+4)-j(n-2)+2\ell>-(i+4)-i(n-2)=-\big(4+i(n-1)\big)\,,

(A.23)

so that the most negative power of
r
comes with the term involving
m^{i}
in the first line.

We now prove the recursion by induction. The
i=1
version of (
A.17
) is provided by (
A.16
).

To prove (
A.17
) with
i=2
we make use of the commutation relation (
6.153
), which allows us to write:

\displaystyle{\mathring{D}}^{A}\mathcal{K}\left(k,\mathring{\Delta},P\right)h_{AB}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{\tilde{K}}\left(k,\mathring{\Delta},\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C\right){\mathring{D}}^{A}h_{AB}\,,

(A.24)

where we recall from the main text that the notation
\tilde{U}(\mathring{\Delta},\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C)
(a tilde over an operator
U
) denotes the replacement of all appearances of the operator
P:=C\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}
, respectively
\mathring{\mathscr{R}}
, in
U
by the operator
\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C
, respectively
(n-2){{\varepsilon}}
.

Then, taking a
u
-derivative of (
A.16
) and making use of (
A.2
) and (
A.24
) we find

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\bigg[\partial_{u}\overset{(1)}{H}_{uA}-{\mathring{D}}^{B}\overset{(1)}{\chi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\hat{q}^{(-5)}_{AB}-m{\mathring{D}}^{B}\overset{(1)}{\chi}_{[m]}\hat{q}^{(-3-n)}_{AB}-\alpha^{2}\mathcal{\tilde{K}}\left(-4,\mathring{\Delta},\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}C\right)\mathcal{K}_{[\alpha]}(-5)H_{uA}\bigg]

\displaystyle={\mathring{D}}^{B}\overset{(1)}{\chi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\bigg[\mathcal{K}\left(-5,\mathring{\Delta},P\right)r^{-6}h_{AB}+m\mathcal{K}_{[m]}(-5)r^{-4-n}h_{AB}\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad+m{\mathring{D}}^{B}\overset{(1)}{\chi}_{[m]}\bigg[\mathcal{K}\left(-3-n,\mathring{\Delta},P\right)r^{-4-n}h_{AB}+\alpha^{2}\mathcal{K}_{[\alpha]}(-3-n)r^{-2-n}h_{AB}+m\mathcal{K}_{[m]}(-3-n)r^{-2-2n}h_{AB}\bigg]

\displaystyle={\mathring{D}}^{B}\underbrace{\overset{(1)}{\chi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\mathcal{K}\left(-5,\mathring{\Delta},P\right)}_{\overset{(2)}{\chi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)}r^{-6}h_{AB}+m{\mathring{D}}^{B}\underbrace{\bigg[\overset{(1)}{\chi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\mathcal{K}_{[m]}(-5)+\overset{(1)}{\chi}_{[m]}\mathcal{K}\left(-3-n,\mathring{\Delta},P\right)\bigg]}_{\overset{(2)}{\chi}_{1,0}(\mathring{\Delta},P)}r^{-4-n}h_{AB}

\displaystyle\quad+m\alpha^{2}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\underbrace{\overset{(1)}{\chi}_{[m]}\mathcal{K}_{[\alpha]}(-3-n)}_{\overset{(2)}{\chi}_{1,1}(\mathring{\Delta},P)}r^{-2-n}h_{AB}+m^{2}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\underbrace{\overset{(1)}{\chi}_{[m]}\mathcal{K}_{[m]}(-3-n)}_{\overset{(2)}{\chi}_{[m]}}r^{-2-2n}h_{AB}\,,

(A.25)

where we also made use of (
A.4
).
This ends the proof of (
A.17
) with
i=2
.

Suppose, next, that (
A.17
) holds for some
i\geq 2
. Taking the
u
-derivative of (
A.17
) and making use of (
A.2
) we have

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\bigg(\partial_{u}\overset{(i)}{H}_{uA}-{\mathring{D}}^{B}\overset{(i)}{\chi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\hat{q}^{(-i-4)}_{AB}-\sum_{j,\ell}^{i_{*}}m^{j}\alpha^{2\ell}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\overset{(i)}{\chi}_{j,\ell}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\hat{q}^{(-(i+4)-j(n-2)+2\ell)}_{AB}

\displaystyle\qquad-m^{i}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\overset{(i)}{\chi}_{[m]}\hat{q}^{(-(4+i(n-1)))}_{AB}\bigg)

\displaystyle={\mathring{D}}^{B}\overset{(i)}{\chi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\bigg[\mathcal{K}\left(-(i+4),\mathring{\Delta},P\right)r^{-(i+5)}h_{AB}+\alpha^{2}\mathcal{K}_{[\alpha]}(-(i+4))r^{-(i+3)}h_{AB}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+m\mathcal{K}_{[m]}(-(i+4))r^{-(i+3)-n}h_{AB}\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad+m^{i}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\overset{(i)}{\chi}_{[m]}\bigg[\mathcal{K}\left(-(4+i(n-1)),\mathring{\Delta},P\right)r^{-(5+i(n-1))}h_{AB}+\alpha^{2}\mathcal{K}_{[\alpha]}(-(4+i(n-1)))r^{-(3+i(n-1))}h_{AB}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+m\mathcal{K}_{[m]}(-(4+i(n-1)))r^{-(4+(i+1)(n-1))}h_{AB}\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j,\ell}^{i_{*}}m^{j}\alpha^{2\ell}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\overset{(i)}{\chi}_{j,\ell}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\times\bigg[\mathcal{K}\left(-(i+4)-j(n-2)+2\ell,\mathring{\Delta},P\right)r^{-(i+5)-j(n-2)+2\ell}h_{AB}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\alpha^{2}\mathcal{K}_{[\alpha]}(-(i+4)-j(n-2)+2\ell)r^{-(i+3)-j(n-2)+2\ell}h_{AB}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+m\mathcal{K}_{[m]}(-(i+4)-j(n-2)+2\ell)r^{-(i+4)-j(n-2)+2\ell-(n-1)}h_{AB}\bigg]\,.

(A.26)

Let us first justify our claim above, that the powers of
r
appearing in the final sum do not exceed
-4
.
By our induction hypothesis it holds that
-(i+4)-j(n-2)+2\ell\leq-4
in each term of the sum appearing in (
A.17
). Now, out of the three terms in the square brackets, only the one multiplied by
\alpha^{2}
is associated with an increase (compared with
-(i+4)-j(n-2)+2\ell
) in the power of
r
, and this increase is by
1
. Thus the only terms with a power of
r
which could possibly exceed
-4
are those for which
-(i+3)-j(n-2)+2\ell=-3
. However, all such terms are multiplied by
\mathcal{K}_{[\alpha]}(-4)=0
, which establishes the claim.

Next, all the terms in the last equation are as in (
A.17
) except perhaps for the one multiplied by a single power of
\alpha^{2}
. In order to handle this term we use (
A.24
) and
(
A.17
) (with
i
replaced by
i-1
) to write

\displaystyle{\mathring{D}}^{B}\overset{(i)}{\chi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)

\displaystyle r^{-(i+3)}h_{AB}={\mathring{D}}^{B}\mathcal{K}\left(-(i+3),\mathring{\Delta},P\right)\overset{(i-1)}{\chi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,r^{-(i+3)}h_{AB}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{\tilde{K}}\left(-(i+3),\mathring{\Delta},\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}C\right){\mathring{D}}^{B}\big(\overset{(i-1)}{\chi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,r^{-(i+3)}h_{AB}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{\tilde{K}}\left(-(i+3),\mathring{\Delta},\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}C\right)\bigg[\partial_{r}\overset{(i-1)}{H}_{uA}-m^{i-1}\overset{(i-1)}{\chi}_{[m]}r^{-(4+(i-1)(n-1))}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{j,\ell}^{(i-1)_{*}}m^{j}\alpha^{2\ell}r^{-(i+3)-j(n-2)+2\ell}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\big(\overset{(i-1)}{\chi}_{j,\ell}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,h_{AB}\big)\bigg]

\displaystyle=\partial_{r}\big(\mathcal{\tilde{K}}\left(-(i+3),\mathring{\Delta},\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}C\right)\overset{(i-1)}{H}_{uA}\big)

\displaystyle\quad-{\mathring{D}}^{B}\bigg[m^{i-1}\mathcal{K}\left(-(i+3),\mathring{\Delta},P\right)\overset{(i-1)}{\chi}_{[m]}r^{-(4+(i-1)(n-1))}h_{AB}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j,\ell}^{(i-1)_{*}}m^{j}\alpha^{2\ell}r^{-(i+3)-j(n-2)+2\ell}\mathcal{K}\left(-(i+3),\mathring{\Delta},P\right)\overset{(i-1)}{\chi}_{j,\ell}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,h_{AB}\bigg]\,,

(A.27)

and the induction is completed.

A.2
Integral formulae involving
\partial_{u}^{i}h_{AB}

Assume that

\mycal E_{rA}=0
and that the equations
\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[\mycal E_{AB}]=0
hold.

We wish to show, by induction, that the equations

\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[\partial_{u}^{i-1}\mycal E_{AB}]=0

hold if and only if

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\overset{(i)}{q}_{AB}

\displaystyle=\overset{(i)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,r^{(n-7-2i)/2}h_{AB}+\alpha^{2i}\overset{(i)}{\psi}_{[\alpha]}r^{(n-7+2i)/2}h_{AB}+m^{i}\overset{(i)}{\psi}_{[m]}r^{\frac{n-7-2i(n-1)}{2}}h_{AB}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j,\ell}^{i_{**}}m^{j}\alpha^{2\ell}\overset{(i)}{\psi}_{j,\ell}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,r^{\frac{n-7}{2}-i-j(n-2)+2\ell}h_{AB}\,,

(A.28)

with
i\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}

(recall that
n>3
),
and
\sum_{j,\ell}^{i_{**}}
denotes sum over
j,\ell
with

\begin{cases}1\leq j\leq i-1\,,\ j+\ell\leq i\,,&\mbox{if $n$ is even}\\
1\leq j\leq i-1\,,\ j+\ell\leq i\,,\ \frac{n-7}{2}-i-j(n-2)+2\ell\leq-4,&\mbox{if $n$ is odd.}\end{cases}

(A.29)

The fields
\overset{(i)}{q}_{AB}
appearing in (
A.28
) depend on
(r,h_{AB},\partial^{j}_{u}h_{AB},\partial_{u}^{j-1}h_{uA},\partial_{r}\partial_{u}^{j-1}h_{uA})_{j=1}^{i}
, the operators
\overset{(i)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)
and
\overset{(i)}{\psi}_{j,\ell}(\mathring{\Delta},P)
are polynomials in
P
of orders
i
and
i-j-\ell
respectively,
and
\overset{(i)}{\psi}_{[\alpha]}
and
\overset{(i)}{\psi}_{[m]}
are constants. These are all defined recursively, with

\displaystyle\overset{(0)}{q}_{AB}

\displaystyle=0\,,\quad\overset{(0)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)=\overset{(0)}{\psi}_{[\alpha]}=\overset{(0)}{\psi}_{[m]}=1\,,\quad\overset{(1)}{q}_{AB}=q_{AB}\,,\quad\overset{(1)}{\psi}_{[\alpha]}=\frac{1}{8}(n^{2}-1)\,,

(A.30)

\displaystyle\overset{(1)}{\psi}_{[m]}

\displaystyle=-\frac{(n-1)^{2}}{4}\,,\qquad\overset{(1)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)=-\bigg[\frac{4}{n+1}P-\mathring{\mathscr{R}}-\frac{1}{2}\mathring{\Delta}+\frac{(n-3)(n-1){{\varepsilon}}}{8}\bigg]\,,

(A.31)

and

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\psi}_{[\alpha]}=\mathcal{K}_{[\alpha]}(\tfrac{n-9+2i}{2})\overset{(i-1)}{\psi}_{[\alpha]}\,,\quad\overset{(i)}{\psi}_{[m]}=\mathcal{K}_{[m]}(\tfrac{n-7-2(i-1)(n-1)}{2})\overset{(i-1)}{\psi}_{[m]}\,,\quad i\geq 2\,,

(A.32)

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)=\mathcal{K}\left(\tfrac{n-5-2i}{2},\mathring{\Delta},P\right)\overset{(i-1)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,,\quad 2\leq i\,,\ \mbox{with}\ i\neq\frac{n+1}{2}\ \text{if $n$ is odd}\,,

(A.33)

\displaystyle\overset{(\frac{n+1}{2})}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)=\mathring{\mathcal{K}}\left(-3,\mathring{\Delta},P\right)\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2})}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,,\quad\text{for odd $n$ only}\,,

(A.34)

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{q}_{AB}=\partial_{u}\overset{(i-1)}{q}_{AB}-\overset{(i-1)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\hat{q}_{AB}^{(\frac{n-5-2i}{2})}-\alpha^{2(i-1)}\overset{(i-1)}{\psi}_{[\alpha]}\hat{q}_{AB}^{(\frac{n-9+2i}{2})}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad-m^{i-1}\overset{(i-1)}{\psi}_{[m]}\hat{q}^{(\frac{n-7-2(i-1)(n-1)}{2})}_{AB}-\alpha^{2}\mathcal{\widehat{K}}(2(i-1),P)\overset{(i-2)}{q}_{AB}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad-\sum_{j,\ell}^{(i-1)_{**}}m^{j}\alpha^{2\ell}\overset{(i-1)}{\psi}_{j,\ell}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\hat{q}^{(\frac{1}{2}(n-7-2(i-1)-2j(n-2)+4\ell))}_{AB}\,,

(A.35)

where
\mathcal{\widehat{K}}(2i,P)
is defined in (
A.44
) below. We note that
\mathcal{K}_{[\alpha]}(n-4)=0
and hence in fact

\overset{(i)}{\psi}_{[\alpha]}=0\quad\text{for}\quad i\geq\frac{n+1}{2}

(A.36)

when
n
is odd. Equation (
A.35
) holds for all
i\geq 2
when
n
is even and for
2\leq i\leq\frac{n+1}{2}
when
n>3
is odd. When
n>3
is odd, for
i>\frac{n+1}{2}
, we have instead

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{q}_{AB}

\displaystyle=\partial_{u}\overset{(i-1)}{q}_{AB}-\overset{(i-1)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\hat{q}_{AB}^{(\frac{n-5-2i}{2})}-m^{i-1}\overset{(i-1)}{\psi}_{[m]}\hat{q}^{(\frac{n-7-2(i-1)(n-1)}{2})}_{AB}

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{j,\ell}^{(i-1)_{**}}m^{j}\alpha^{2\ell}\overset{(i-1)}{\psi}_{j,\ell}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\hat{q}^{(\frac{1}{2}(n-7-2(i-1)-2j(n-2)+4\ell))}_{AB}\,.

(A.37)

We will now prove the recursion by induction. First, (
A.28
)
with
i=1
is simply (
3.100
).

Next, taking the
u
-derivative of (
3.100
) gives
