\displaystyle\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(1)}}\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\overset{((n-5)/2)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\circ P\circ\,C\circ{\mathring{D}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad=\underbrace{\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(1)}}\circ\overset{((n-5)/2)}{\tilde{\psi}}(\mathring{\Delta},\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C)}_{=:\overset{((n-5)/2)}{\hat{\psi}}(\mathring{\Delta})\,\circ\,\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(1)}}}\circ\,\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ P\circ\,C\circ{\mathring{D}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad=\overset{((n-5)/2)}{\hat{\psi}}(\mathring{\Delta})\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(1)}}\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ P\circ\,C\circ{\mathring{D}}\,,

(C.58)

where the first equality follows from the commutation relation (
6.154
). The second equality makes use of fact that the operator
\overset{(p)}{\tilde{\psi}}
is a product of operators of the form
\tilde{\operatorname{L}}_{a,c}
of Lemma
C.8
; the operator
\overset{((n-5)/2)}{\hat{\psi}}(\mathring{\Delta})
is obtained by using the commutation relation (
C.55
) to commute
\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(1)}}
and
\overset{((n-5)/2)}{\tilde{\psi}}(\mathring{\Delta},\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C)
. Clearly,
\overset{((n-5)/2)}{\hat{\psi}}(\mathring{\Delta})
is a product of elliptic operators and is hence itself elliptic. We thus conclude that the operator (
C.56
), and hence
\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(1)}}\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\operatorname{\!\!\check{\,\,L}}_{n}
, are elliptic.

Now, we have

h^{[S]}=\text{im}(C\circ{\mathring{D}})
. Next,
by Proposition
C.12
,
\ker(\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P))=h^{[S]}

plus possibly a finite dimensional
space of smooth tensor fields in
h^{[V]}
.
Finally, noting that

\displaystyle\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(1)}}\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\operatorname{\!\!\check{\,\,L}}_{n}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\!\!\check{\,\,L}}_{n}^{\dagger}\circ\,C\circ{\mathring{D}}\,,

(C.59)

we conclude that
\ker\operatorname{\!\!\check{\,\,L}}_{n}^{\dagger}\cap\ker(\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P))
is smooth and finite dimensional.

\Box

Next, we move on to the operator
\operatorname{L_{n}}
(
4.49
), relevant for the gauge transformation of the fields
\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2}+j)}{q}_{AB}
for
j\geq 0
.

Proposition
C.14
.

The kernel
\ker\operatorname{L_{n}^{\dagger}}\cap{\mathrm{TT}^{\perp}}
is smooth and finite dimensional.

Proof.

Recall from (
4.52
) that

\displaystyle\underline{\operatorname{L_{n}}}(\xi)_{AB}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{8}(\mathring{\Delta}+2\mathring{\mathscr{R}}-4{{\varepsilon}})(\mathring{\Delta}+2\mathring{\mathscr{R}}-6{{\varepsilon}})C(\xi)_{AB}&\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad-\frac{1}{6}(\mathring{\Delta}+2\mathring{\mathscr{R}}-5{{\varepsilon}})\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}{\mathring{D}}_{C}\xi^{C}]\,,&n=5\\
\frac{1}{(n-1)(n-5)}\bigg((\mathring{\Delta}+2\mathring{\mathscr{R}}-2(n-2){{\varepsilon}})(\mathring{\Delta}+2\mathring{\mathscr{R}}+(1-n){{\varepsilon}})C(\xi)_{AB}&\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad-\frac{2(n-3)}{n-2}(\mathring{\Delta}+2\mathring{\mathscr{R}}+(5-2n){{\varepsilon}})\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}{\mathring{D}}_{C}\xi^{C}]\bigg)\,,&n\neq 5\,,\end{cases}

(C.60)

and

\operatorname{L_{n}}=\overset{(\frac{n-5}{2})}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\underline{\operatorname{L_{n}}}\,,\quad\operatorname{L_{n}^{\dagger}}=(\underline{\operatorname{L_{n}}})^{\dagger}\overset{(\frac{n-5}{2})}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,.

(C.61)

For the purpose of calculating the space
\ker\big((\underline{\operatorname{L_{n}}})^{\dagger}\big)\cap{\mathrm{TT}^{\perp}}
, we start by analysing the symbol of
(\underline{\operatorname{L_{n}}})^{\dagger}\circ\,C
given by, when
n\neq 5
,

\sigma(k)_{B}=-\frac{1}{(n-5)(n-1)}\bigg(|k|^{4}k^{A}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[k_{A}\xi_{B}]-\frac{2(n-3)}{(n-2)}|k|^{2}k_{B}k^{C}k^{D}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[k_{C}\xi_{D}]\bigg)\,.

(C.62)

To find its kernel for
k\neq 0
, we contract (
C.62
) with
k^{B}
and set it to zero giving

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\frac{n-4}{(n-2)(n-5)(n-1)}|k|^{4}k^{C}k^{D}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[k_{C}\xi_{D}]\implies\frac{n-4}{(n-5)(n-1)^{2}}|k|^{6}k^{A}\xi_{A}=0\,,

(C.63)

and hence
k^{A}\xi_{A}=0
since
|k|^{2},(n-5),(n-1),(n-4)\neq 0
. This can be substituted back into (
C.62
) to give

0=|k|^{4}k^{A}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[k_{A}\xi_{B}]=\frac{1}{2}|k|^{4}|k|^{2}\xi_{B}\implies\xi_{B}=0\,.

(C.64)

Thus
(\underline{\operatorname{L_{n}}})^{\dagger}\circ\,C
is elliptic. This, together with the ellipticity of
\overset{(\frac{n-5}{2})}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)
, and the fact that
\overset{(\frac{n-5}{2})}{\psi}({\mathrm{TT}^{\perp}})\subseteq{\mathrm{TT}^{\perp}}
(cf. Proposition
6.7
, p.
6.7
), allows us to conclude that

\ker\operatorname{L_{n}^{\dagger}}\cap{\mathrm{TT}^{\perp}}
is finite dimensional and its elements are smooth.

The same conclusion holds for
n=5
, with (
C.62
) replaced by

\sigma(k)_{B}=-\frac{1}{8}|k|^{4}k^{A}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[k_{A}\xi_{B}]+\frac{1}{12}|k|^{2}k_{B}k^{C}k^{D}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[k_{C}\xi_{D}]\,.

(C.65)

Appendix D
\partial_{u}^{i}h_{uA}^{[{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}]}
obstructions for
m=0
,
\alpha\neq 0

In this appendix, we derive the expressions for the obstructions associated to the fields
\overset{(i)}{H}{}_{uA}^{[{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}]}
when
m=0
and
\alpha\neq 0
.
Throughout this section we assume that
n
is odd and
m=0
.

This is most conveniently done using the machinery developed in Section
6.4.3
of the main text, making use of the
S
and
V
decomposition of
{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}
.

Indeed, it follows from the arguments there that (cf. around (
6.168
)) the transport equation for
\overset{(i)}{H}{}_{uA}^{[{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}]}
can be solved by considering the
X=S,V
projections separately. In the current case, this reads (cf. (
4.3
))

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\overset{(i)}{H}{}_{uA}^{[{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}\cap X]}

\displaystyle=r^{-(i+4)}\overset{(i)}{\tilde{\chi}}\circ{\mathring{D}}^{B}C(Y^{[X]})_{AB}\,,

(D.1)

where
Y\in{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}
is the unique field such that
h_{AB}^{[{\mathrm{TT}^{\perp}}]}=C(Y^{[X]})_{AB}
. Thus, the radial charges are the projections of
\overset{(i)}{H}{}_{uA}
onto the spaces
{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}\cap X\cap\ker\overset{(i)}{\tilde{\chi}}
.

D.1
The case
{{\varepsilon}}\leq 0

Lemma
D.1
.

For
{{\varepsilon}}\leq 0
, the space
{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}\cap X\cap\ker\overset{(i)}{\tilde{\chi}}=\emptyset
.

Proof.

Recall the definition of the operators
\overset{(i)}{\tilde{\chi}}(\mathring{\Delta},\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C)
:

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\tilde{\chi}}(\mathring{\Delta},\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C)

\displaystyle=\prod_{j=1}^{i}\mathcal{\tilde{K}}\left(-(j+3),\mathring{\Delta},\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C\right)\,,\quad\overset{(0)}{\tilde{\chi}}(\mathring{\Delta},\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C):=1\,,

(D.2)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{\tilde{K}}\left(j,\mathring{\Delta},\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C\right)

\displaystyle:=c_{j}\bigg[\frac{2(n-1)\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ C}{(3+j)(3-n+j)}+(n-2){{\varepsilon}}+\mathring{\Delta}-(n-4-j)(2+j){{\varepsilon}}\bigg]\,,

(D.3)

with
c_{j}:=-\frac{1}{7-n+2j}
.

Let
\xi\in X\cap{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}
. From (
6.4.3
), we have for
n>3
,
j\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{\tilde{K}}\left(-(j+3),\mathring{\Delta},\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C\right)\xi=\begin{cases}\frac{(j+2)(j+n-2)}{j(j+n)(2j+n-1)}\big(\mathring{\Delta}+\underbrace{(j(j+n)+1)}_{\geq 0}{{\varepsilon}}\big)\,,&X=S\\
\frac{(j+1)(j+n-1)}{j(j+n)(2j+n-1)}\big(\mathring{\Delta}+\underbrace{(j(j+n)+n-2)}_{\geq 0}{{\varepsilon}}\big)\,.&X=V\end{cases}

(D.4)

Thus
\mathcal{\tilde{K}}\left(-(j+3),\mathring{\Delta},\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C\right)\xi^{[{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}]}\neq 0
by negativity of
\mathring{\Delta}
when
{{\varepsilon}}\leq 0
.

\Box

It follows from Lemma
D.1
that there are no obstructions associated to
\overset{(i)}{H}{}^{[{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}]}_{uA}
when
{{\varepsilon}}\leq 0
.

D.2
The case
{{\varepsilon}}>0

Next, we move on to the case
{{\varepsilon}}>0
. For this, we first derive a relation between the operators
\operatorname{\!\!\check{\,\,L}}_{n}
and
\operatorname{L_{n}}
. We begin by looking at the operator
\operatorname{\!\!\check{\,\,L}}_{n}
(cf. (
4.46
)-(
4.47
)):
setting

a_{n}=\begin{cases}-\frac{1}{18}\,,&n=5\,,\\
-\frac{n-4}{(n-5)(n-2)^{2}}\,,&n>5\,,\end{cases}

we have

\displaystyle{\mathring{D}}_{C}\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}}\circ\operatorname{\!\!\check{\,\,L}}_{n}(\xi^{u})

\displaystyle={\mathring{D}}_{C}{\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\underline{\operatorname{\!\!\check{\,\,L}}}_{n}({\mathring{D}}\xi^{u})_{AB}

\displaystyle=a_{n}{\mathring{D}}_{C}{\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\overset{(\frac{n-5}{2})}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\big((n-1)P-2(n-2)^{2}{{\varepsilon}}\big)\,C({\mathring{D}}\xi^{u})_{AB}

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{28VIII23.f4})}{=}a_{n}{\mathring{D}}_{C}{\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\overset{(\frac{n-5}{2})}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\big((n-2)(\mathring{\Delta}+2\mathring{\mathscr{R}})-(n-2)(3n-7){{\varepsilon}}\big)C({\mathring{D}}\xi^{u})_{AB}

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{11X23.11})}{=}a_{n}{\mathring{D}}_{C}{\mathring{D}}^{A}\overset{(\frac{n-5}{2})}{\tilde{\psi}}(\mathring{\Delta},\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C)\,(n-2)(\mathring{\Delta}-(2n-5){{\varepsilon}}){\mathring{D}}^{B}C({\mathring{D}}\xi^{u})_{AB}

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{26IX23.1})}{=}a_{n}{\mathring{D}}_{C}{\mathring{D}}^{A}\overset{(\frac{n-5}{2})}{\tilde{\psi}}\big(\mathring{\Delta},\tfrac{n-2}{n-1}(\mathring{\Delta}+{{\varepsilon}})\big)\,\tfrac{(n-2)^{2}}{n-1}(\mathring{\Delta}-(2n-5){{\varepsilon}})(\mathring{\Delta}+{{\varepsilon}}){\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi^{u}

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{19X23.1})}{=}a_{n}\tfrac{(n-2)^{2}}{n-1}\overset{(\frac{n-5}{2})}{\tilde{\psi}}\big(\mathring{\Delta},\tfrac{n-2}{n-1}(\mathring{\Delta}+{{\varepsilon}})\big)\,(\mathring{\Delta}-(2n-5){{\varepsilon}})(\mathring{\Delta}+{{\varepsilon}})(\mathring{\Delta}-(n-2){{\varepsilon}}){\mathring{D}}_{C}\xi^{u}

(D.5)

where we made use of the fact that
[({\mathring{D}}\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(1)}}),\mathring{\Delta}]=0
in the last equality.

Meanwhile, for the operator
\operatorname{L_{n}}
, we had from (
4.51
),

\operatorname{L_{n}}=\overset{(\frac{n-5}{2})}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\underline{\operatorname{L_{n}}}\,,

(D.6)

where the operator
\underline{\operatorname{L_{n}}}
was given in (
4.52
). In particular, for

\xi^{A}\in S
,

\displaystyle{\mathring{D}}^{A}\underline{\operatorname{L_{n}}}(\xi)_{AB}

\displaystyle=b_{n}{\mathring{D}}^{A}\big[(\mathring{\Delta}+2\mathring{\mathscr{R}}-2(n-2){{\varepsilon}})(\mathring{\Delta}+2\mathring{\mathscr{R}}+(1-n){{\varepsilon}})C(\xi)_{AB}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad-\frac{2(n-3)}{n-2}(\mathring{\Delta}+2\mathring{\mathscr{R}}+(5-2n){{\varepsilon}})\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}{\mathring{D}}_{C}\xi^{C}]\big]

\displaystyle=b_{n}{\mathring{D}}^{A}\big[(\mathring{\Delta}+2\mathring{\mathscr{R}}-2(n-2){{\varepsilon}})(\mathring{\Delta}+2\mathring{\mathscr{R}}+(1-n){{\varepsilon}})C(\xi)_{AB}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad-\frac{2(n-3)}{n-2}(\mathring{\Delta}+2\mathring{\mathscr{R}}+(5-2n){{\varepsilon}})(\mathring{\Delta}+2\mathring{\mathscr{R}}-2(n-2){{\varepsilon}})C(\xi)_{AB}\big]

\displaystyle=-\frac{(n-4)b_{n}}{(n-2)}(\mathring{\Delta}-(2n-5){{\varepsilon}})(\mathring{\Delta}-(n-2){{\varepsilon}}){\mathring{D}}^{A}C(\xi)_{AB}

\displaystyle=-\frac{(n-4)b_{n}}{(n-1)}(\mathring{\Delta}-(2n-5){{\varepsilon}})(\mathring{\Delta}-(n-2){{\varepsilon}})(\mathring{\Delta}+{{\varepsilon}})\xi_{B}\,,

(D.7)

where

\displaystyle b_{n}=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{8}\,,&n=5\,,\\
\frac{1}{(n-1)(n-5)}\,,&n>5\,.\end{cases}

(D.8)

Thus, for
\xi^{A}\in S
,

\displaystyle{\mathring{D}}^{A}\operatorname{L_{n}}(\xi)_{AB}

\displaystyle=-\frac{(n-4)b_{n}}{(n-2)}\overset{(\frac{n-5}{2})}{\tilde{\psi}}\big(\mathring{\Delta},\tfrac{n-2}{n-1}(\mathring{\Delta}+{{\varepsilon}})\big)(\mathring{\Delta}-(2n-5){{\varepsilon}})(\mathring{\Delta}-(n-2){{\varepsilon}})(\mathring{\Delta}+{{\varepsilon}})\xi_{B}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n-1}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}}\circ\underline{\operatorname{\!\!\check{\,\,L}}_{n}}(\xi)

(D.9)

A similar calculation shows that (
D.9
) continues to hold for
n=5
.

Next, for
i\in\mathbbm{Z},i\geq 0
, it can be shown inductively that under residual gauge transformations, the
r
-independent part of the gauge transformation of the fields
\overset{(i)}{H}_{uA}
reads,

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{H}_{uA}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\begin{cases}\overset{(i)}{H}_{uA}+n\partial_{u}^{i+1}\xi_{A}+\sum_{j=1}^{i/2}\alpha^{2j}n\overset{(i,j)}{D}(\partial_{u}^{i+1-2j}\xi_{A})+\alpha^{i+2}n\overset{(i,\frac{i+2}{2})}{D}\circ{\mathring{D}}(\xi^{u})\,,&i\,\text{even}\\
\overset{(i)}{H}_{uA}+n\partial_{u}^{i+1}\xi_{A}+\sum_{j=1}^{(i+1)/2}\alpha^{2j}n\overset{(i,j)}{D}(\partial_{u}^{i+1-2j}\xi_{A})\,,&i\,\text{odd}\,.\end{cases}

(D.10)

The operators

\overset{(p,q)}{D}

appearing in (
D.10
) are sums of products of operators of the form
\tilde{\operatorname{L}}_{a,c,b}
of (
6.169
) and preserve the spaces
S
and
V
. They commute with
\mathring{\Delta}
when restricted to
S
or to
V
.

Thus, for
{{\varepsilon}}>0
, we have the gauge-invariant radial charge: for even
2\leq i\leq k-\frac{n+1}{2}
,

\displaystyle\overset{[5,i]}{Q}{}^{[X]}_{B}

\displaystyle:={\mathring{D}}^{C}\operatorname{L_{n}}(\overset{(i)}{H}{}^{[X\cap{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}\cap\ker(\overset{(i)}{\chi}\circ C)]}_{uA})_{CB}-n({\mathring{D}}^{C}\overset{(\frac{n+1}{2}+i)}{q}_{CB}){}^{[X\cap{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}\cap\ker(\overset{(i)}{\chi}\circ C)]}

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{j=1}^{i/2}\alpha^{2j}n\overset{(i,j)}{D}({\mathring{D}}^{A}\overset{(\frac{n+1}{2}+i-2j)}{q}_{AB}){}^{[X\cap{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}\cap\ker(\overset{(i)}{\chi}\circ C)]}

\displaystyle\quad-\alpha^{i+2}\frac{n}{n-2}\overset{(i,\frac{i+2}{2})}{D}\Big({\mathring{D}}_{B}(\overset{(3)}{Q}){}^{[X\cap{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}\cap\ker(\overset{(i)}{\chi}\circ C)]}\Big)\,,

(D.11)

for
X=S,V
, with the last term vanishing for
X=V
. We have made use of (
D.9
) for the last term. For odd
1\leq i\leq k-\frac{n+1}{2}
, the charge is given by a similar expression,

\displaystyle\overset{[5,i]}{Q}{}^{[X]}_{B}

\displaystyle:=\begin{cases}{\mathring{D}}^{C}\operatorname{L_{n}}(\overset{(1)}{H}{}^{[X\cap{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}\cap\ker(\overset{(1)}{\chi}\circ C)]}_{uA})_{CB}-n({\mathring{D}}^{C}\overset{(\frac{n+3}{2})}{q}_{CB}){}^{[X\cap{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}\cap\ker(\overset{(i)}{\chi}\circ C)]}&\\
\quad-\alpha^{2}n\overset{(1,1)}{D}(\overset{(4)}{Q}_{B}){}^{[X\cap{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}\cap\ker(\overset{(1)}{\chi}\circ C)]}\,,&i=1\\
{\mathring{D}}^{C}\operatorname{L_{n}}(\overset{(i)}{H}{}^{[X\cap{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}\cap\ker(\overset{(i)}{\chi}\circ C)]}_{uA})_{CB}-n({\mathring{D}}^{C}\overset{(\frac{n+1}{2}+i)}{q}_{CB}){}^{[X\cap{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}\cap\ker(\overset{(i)}{\chi}\circ C)]}&\\
\quad-\sum_{j=1}^{(i+1)/2}\alpha^{2j}n\overset{(i,j)}{D}({\mathring{D}}^{A}\overset{(\frac{n+1}{2}+i-2j)}{q}_{AB}){}^{[X\cap{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}\cap\ker(\overset{(i)}{\chi}\circ C)]}\,,&i\geq 3\,.\end{cases}

(D.12)

The gauge-invariance of
\overset{[5,i]}{Q}{}_{B}
can be verified by making use of the fact that
[\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\operatorname{L_{n}},\overset{(p,q)}{D}]=0
.

Interlude: the case
n=3
.

From the higher dimensional (HD) case, we see that to obtain the gauge-invariant radial charges associated to
\overset{(i)}{H}_{uA}
, it is useful to first obtain the radial charges whose gauge transformations do not involve fields
\partial^{i}_{u}\xi
with different
i
’s. These are provided by the fields
{\mathring{D}}^{B}\overset{(i)}{q}_{AB}
when
n>3
.

However, when
n=3
, from (
A.65
) we have for
i\geq 3
, using (
A.62
),

\displaystyle({\mathring{D}}^{B}\overset{(i)}{q}_{AB})^{[X]}\rightarrow\begin{cases}({\mathring{D}}^{B}\overset{(i)}{q}_{AB})^{[V]}+{\mathring{D}}^{B}\operatorname{L}_{3}(\partial^{i-1}_{u}\xi^{[V]})_{AB}\,,&\\
({\mathring{D}}^{B}\overset{(i)}{q}_{AB})^{[S]}+{\mathring{D}}^{B}\operatorname{L}_{3}(\partial^{i-1}_{u}\xi^{[S]})_{AB}+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{2}(\mathring{\Delta}-{{\varepsilon}}){\mathring{D}}^{B}\operatorname{L}_{3}(\partial_{u}^{i-3}\xi^{[S]})_{AB}\,,&\end{cases}

(D.13)

where the upper case holds for
X=V
and the lower case for
X=S
.
See Remark
A.1
for the origin of the
\alpha^{2}
-term in the last line above, not present in the HD case.

The case
X=V
agrees with the HD case and thus the charges
\overset{[5,i]}{Q}^{[V]}
as defined in (
D.11
)-(
D.12
) are also gauge-invariant obstructions when
n=3
.

For
n=3
and
X=S
,
we define recursively the radial charges
\overset{(i)}{q}_{A}
, for integers
i\geq 3
, as

\displaystyle\overset{(3)}{q}_{A}

\displaystyle:=({\mathring{D}}^{B}\overset{(3)}{q}_{AB})^{[S]}-\frac{\alpha^{2}}{2}(\mathring{\Delta}-{{\varepsilon}})\overset{[4]}{Q}{}_{A}^{[S]}\,,\quad\overset{(i)}{q}_{A}:=({\mathring{D}}^{B}\overset{(i)}{q}_{AB})^{[S]}-\frac{\alpha^{2}}{2}(\mathring{\Delta}-{{\varepsilon}})\overset{(i-2)}{q}_{A}\,,

(D.14)

where the
\alpha^{2}
-terms are included to compensate for the last term in the second line of (
D.13
).
It then follows from (
A.63
) and (
D.13
) that, under gauge-transformations,

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{q}_{A}\rightarrow\overset{(i)}{q}_{A}+{\mathring{D}}^{B}\operatorname{L}_{3}(\partial_{u}^{i-1}\xi^{[S]})_{AB}\,.

(D.15)

Thus,
when
X=S
, we define the charges
\overset{[5,i]}{Q}^{[S]}
as in (
D.11
)-(
D.12
), but with all
{\mathring{D}}^{A}\overset{(i)}{q}_{AB}
’s replaced by
\overset{(i)}{q}_{B}
’s.

\Box

Returning to the higher dimensional case, we end this with the following proposition:

Proposition
D.2
.

When
{{\varepsilon}}>0
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}\cap{\ker}({}\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\operatorname{L_{n}})=\{0\}\,.

(D.16)

Proof.

We start by noting that for
n>5
,
{{\varepsilon}}>0
and
\xi_{A}\in{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}
, we have (cf. (
D.6
))

\displaystyle\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\operatorname{L_{n}}(\xi)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\overset{(\frac{n-5}{2})}{\psi}\circ\underline{\operatorname{L_{n}}}(\xi)

\displaystyle=\overset{(\frac{n-5}{2})}{\tilde{\psi}}\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\underline{\operatorname{L_{n}}}(\xi)

\displaystyle=\prod_{j=2}^{i}\mathcal{\tilde{K}}\left(\tfrac{n-5-2j}{2},\mathring{\Delta},\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C\right)\circ\overset{(1)}{\tilde{\psi}}\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\underline{\operatorname{L_{n}}}(\xi)

(D.17)

For
n=5
, the first equality holds with
\overset{(0)}{\psi}:=1
.

We note next that
\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\underline{\operatorname{L_{n}}}
, and each of the remaining operators appearing in (
D.17
), is formally self-adjoint and preserves
{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}
. We will show that these operators are isomorphisms on
{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}
, for this it suffices to show that they have trivial kernels on
{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}
.

Recall that
{{\varepsilon}}>0
throughout this proof by assumption.

We begin with the operator
\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\underline{\operatorname{L_{n}}}(\xi)
.
A similar calculation to that of (
D.7
) shows that for
\xi^{A}\in V\cap{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}
,

\displaystyle{\mathring{D}}^{A}\underline{\operatorname{L_{n}}}(\xi)_{AB}=c_{n}(\mathring{\Delta}-{{\varepsilon}})(\mathring{\Delta}-(n-2){{\varepsilon}}){\mathring{D}}^{A}C(\xi)_{AB}\,,

(D.18)

where
c_{n}
is a non-vanishing constant depending on
n
. The negativity of the Laplacian, together with (
D.7
), implies that
{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}\cap\ker\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\underline{\operatorname{L_{n}}}=\emptyset
.
This completes the proof for
n=5
.

Next, for the purpose of investigating the kernel of
\overset{(p)}{\tilde{\psi}}
,
p=\frac{n-5}{2}
,
n>5
,
it follows from the commutation relations in Appendix
J
, in particular from (
J.30
) and (
J.31
) with appropriate indices,
that

\displaystyle\mathcal{\tilde{K}}\left(\frac{n-5-2j}{2},\mathring{\Delta},\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C\right)\xi_{A}=\begin{cases}\frac{(2j-n+3)(2j+n-5)}{8(j-1)(2j-n-1)(2j+n-1)}\left(4\mathring{\Delta}-c_{S}{{\varepsilon}}\right)\xi_{A}\,,\\
\frac{(2j-n+1)(2j+n-3)}{8(j-1)(2j-n-1)(2j+n-1)}(4\mathring{\Delta}-c_{V}{{\varepsilon}})\xi_{A}\,,&\end{cases}

(D.19)

with

\displaystyle c_{S}:=\left(n^{2}-5-4(j-1)j\right)\,,\quad c_{V}:=((n-4)n+7-4(j-1)j)\,,

(D.20)

and where the upper case holds for
\xi_{A}\in{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}\cap S
and the lower for
\xi_{A}\in{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}\cap V
. Now for
j\leq\frac{n-5}{2}
and
n>5
, we have

\displaystyle c_{S}

\displaystyle\geq n^{2}-5-4\Big(\frac{n-5}{2}-1\Big)\frac{n-5}{2}=12n-40>0\,,

\displaystyle c_{V}

\displaystyle\geq(n-4)n+7-4\Big(\frac{n-5}{2}-1\Big)\frac{n-5}{2}=8n-28>0\,,

(D.21)

and thus for
0\neq\xi\in{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{\tilde{K}}\left(\frac{n-5-2j}{2},\mathring{\Delta},\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C\right)\xi_{A}\neq 0

(D.22)

for
{{\varepsilon}}>0
and
j\leq\frac{n-5}{2}
. Finally, we have (cf. (
4.24
)), again for
n>5
,
{{\varepsilon}}>0
and
0\neq\xi\in{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}
,

\displaystyle\overset{(1)}{\tilde{\psi}}(\mathring{\Delta},\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C)\xi_{A}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\frac{(n-5)(n-3)}{8\left(n^{2}-1\right)}\left(4\mathring{\Delta}-\left(n^{2}-5\right){{\varepsilon}}\right)\xi_{A}\,,&\xi_{A}\in S\\
\frac{(n-3)}{8(n+1)}(4\mathring{\Delta}-((n-4)n+7){{\varepsilon}})\xi_{A}\,,&\xi_{A}\in V\end{cases}

\displaystyle\neq 0\,.

(D.23)

The proof is complete.

\Box

Appendix E
Operators on spheres

In this appendix we describe in detail the operators and obstructions appearing in the main text in the case when
\mathbf{S}=S^{d}
. In what follows, unless explicitly indicated otherwise we assume that
d\equiv n-1>2
, and for simplicity we assume that
n>5
.

E.1
Decomposition of tensors on
S^{d}

First, recall from (
C.8
) the decomposition of a
2
-covariant, symmetric trace-free tensor
h
according to

h=h^{[V]}+h^{[S]}+h^{[TT]}\,.

(E.1)

On
S^{d}
, the three parts in (
E.1
) can be expanded into eigenfunctions of the Laplacian as in
(
KodamaIshibashiMaster
, Sections 2.1, 5.1 and 5.2)

\displaystyle h^{[S]}_{AB}

\displaystyle=\sum_{I}H_{I}^{[S]}\mathbb{S}^{I}_{AB}\,,\quad h^{[V]}_{AB}=\sum_{I}H_{I}^{[V]}\mathbb{V}^{I}_{AB}\,,\quad h^{[{\mathrm{TT}}]}_{AB}=\sum_{I}H_{I}^{T}\mathbb{T}^{I}_{AB}\,,

(E.2)

with expansion coefficients
H_{I}^{*}
, where

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}^{I}_{AB}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{k^{2}}TS[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\mathbb{S}^{I}]=\frac{1}{k^{2}}{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\mathbb{S}^{I}+\frac{1}{d}{\mathring{\gamma}}_{AB}\mathbb{S}^{I}\,,\quad k\neq 0\,,

(E.3)

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}^{I}_{AB}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2k_{V}}({\mathring{D}}_{A}\mathbb{V}^{I}_{B}+{\mathring{D}}_{B}\mathbb{V}^{I}_{A})\,,\quad k_{V}\neq 0\,,\quad{\mathring{D}}^{A}\mathbb{V}^{I}_{A}=0\,,

(E.4)

with the coefficients
H_{I}^{*}
vanishing if
k=0
or
k_{V}=0
.
The fields
\mathbb{S}^{I}
,
\mathbb{V}_{A}^{I}
,
\mathbb{T}_{AB}^{I}
appearing above are scalar, vector, and
{\mathrm{TT}}
tensor-harmonics, i.e.

(\mathring{\Delta}+k^{2})\mathbb{S}^{I}=0\,,\quad(\mathring{\Delta}+k_{V}^{2})\mathbb{V}^{I}_{A}=0\,,\quad(\mathring{\Delta}+k_{T}^{2})\mathbb{T}^{I}_{AB}=0\,,

(E.5)

\mathbb{T}^{I}_{AB}=\mathbb{T}^{I}_{BA}\,,\qquad{\mathring{\gamma}}^{AB}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\mathbb{T}^{I}_{BC}=0\,,\qquad{\mathring{\gamma}}^{AB}\mathbb{T}^{I}_{AB}=0\,,

(E.6)

with eigenvalues
k^{2}=k^{2}(I)
,
k_{V}^{2}=k_{V}^{2}(I)
,
k_{T}^{2}=k_{T}^{2}(I)
.
On
S^{d}
the eigenvalues are
(
KodamaIshibashiMaster
, Sections 2.1, 5.1 and 5.2)

\displaystyle k^{2}

\displaystyle=\ell(\ell+d-1)\,,

\displaystyle\ell

\displaystyle=0,1,2,\dots\,,

(E.7)

\displaystyle k_{V}^{2}

\displaystyle=\ell(\ell+d-1)-1\,,

\displaystyle\ell

\displaystyle=1,2,3\dots\,,

(E.8)

\displaystyle k_{T}^{2}

\displaystyle=\ell(\ell+d-1)-2\,,

\displaystyle\ell

\displaystyle=2,3,4,\dots\,,\quad d>2\,.

(E.9)

For the purposes of the main body of this paper we need to calculate
Ph^{[S]}
,
Ph^{[V]}
, and
Ph^{[TT]}
. We start by noting that for any symmetric traceless 2-covariant tensor
h
, we have

Ph^{[TT]}\equiv C\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}h^{[TT]}=0\,.

Next, in order to determine
Ph^{[S]}
and
Ph^{[V]}
, the following identities will be useful (the reader might find useful the commutation relations of Appendix
I
in their derivation):

\displaystyle\mathring{\Delta}\mathbb{S}_{AB}^{I}

\displaystyle=(-k^{2}+2d)\mathbb{S}_{AB}^{I}\,,

(E.10)

\displaystyle\mathring{\Delta}\mathbb{V}_{AB}^{I}

\displaystyle=(-k_{V}^{2}+d+1)\mathbb{V}_{AB}^{I}\,,

(E.11)

\displaystyle{\mathring{D}}^{A}\mathbb{S}_{AB}^{I}

\displaystyle=\frac{(d-k^{2})(d-1)}{k^{2}d}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\mathbb{S}^{I}\,,\quad k^{2}>0\,,

(E.12)

\displaystyle\mathring{\Delta}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\mathbb{S}^{I}

\displaystyle=(d-1-k^{2}){\mathring{D}}_{A}\mathbb{S}^{I}\,,\quad k^{2}\geq 0\,,

(E.13)

\displaystyle{\mathring{D}}^{A}\mathbb{V}_{AB}^{I}

\displaystyle=\frac{k_{V}^{2}-d+1}{2k_{V}}\mathbb{V}_{B}\,,\quad k_{V}>0\,.

(E.14)

Then:

\displaystyle P(h^{[V]})_{EF}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{I}H_{I}^{[V]}\frac{1}{2k_{V}}C\Big[{\mathring{D}}^{A}({\mathring{D}}_{A}\mathbb{V}^{I}_{B}+{\mathring{D}}_{B}\mathbb{V}^{I}_{A})\Big]_{EF}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{I}H_{I}^{[V]}\frac{1}{2k_{V}}C\Big[-k_{V}^{2}\mathbb{V}^{I}_{B}+{\mathring{D}}^{C}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\mathbb{V}^{I}_{C}\Big]_{EF}

\displaystyle=\sum_{I}H_{I}^{[V]}\frac{1}{2k_{V}}C\Big[(k_{V}^{2}-(d-1))\mathbb{V}^{I}_{B}\Big]_{EF}

\displaystyle=\sum_{I}-H_{I}^{[V]}(k_{V}^{2}-(d-1))\mathbb{V}^{I}_{EF}\,,

(E.15)

and

\displaystyle P(h^{[S]})_{EF}

\displaystyle=\sum_{I}H_{I}^{[S]}C\Big[{\mathring{D}}^{A}\Big(\frac{1}{k^{2}}{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\mathbb{S}^{I}+\frac{1}{d}{\mathring{\gamma}}_{AB}\mathbb{S}^{I}\Big)\Big]_{EF}

\displaystyle=\sum_{I}H_{I}^{[S]}C\Big[\Big(\frac{1}{k^{2}}\mathring{\Delta}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\mathbb{S}^{I}+\frac{1}{d}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\mathbb{S}^{I}\Big)\Big]_{EF}

\displaystyle=\sum_{I}H_{I}^{[S]}C\Big[\Big(\frac{1}{k^{2}}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\mathring{\Delta}\mathbb{S}^{I}+\frac{d-1}{k^{2}}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\mathbb{S}^{I}+\frac{1}{d}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\mathbb{S}^{I}\Big)\Big]_{EF}

\displaystyle=\sum_{I}H_{I}^{[S]}C\Big[\Big(-{\mathring{D}}_{B}\mathbb{S}^{I}+\frac{d-1}{k^{2}}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\mathbb{S}^{I}+\frac{1}{d}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\mathbb{S}^{I}\Big)\Big]_{EF}

\displaystyle=\sum_{I}H_{I}^{[S]}\Big(-1+\frac{d-1}{k^{2}}+\frac{1}{d}\Big)C[{\mathring{D}}_{B}\mathbb{S}^{I}]_{EF}

\displaystyle=\sum_{I}H_{I}^{[S]}\Big(-k^{2}+d-1+\frac{k^{2}}{d}\Big)\mathbb{S}_{EF}^{I}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{I}H_{I}^{[S]}\left({\frac{(d-k^{2})(d-1)}{d}}\right)\mathbb{S}_{EF}^{I}\,.

(E.16)

Note that if we write
k^{2}=\ell(\ell+d-1)
, then

\displaystyle{\frac{(d-k^{2})(d-1)}{d}}={\frac{(\ell-1)(d-1)(\ell+d)}{d}}\,.

(E.17)

E.2
Conformal Killing vectors on the sphere

We have the following properties of conformal Killing vectors on
S^{d}
:

Proposition
E.1
.

Consider
S^{d}
,
d\geq 2
, with the canonical metric.

1.

The space of proper conformal Killing vectors is spanned by gradients of
\ell=1
spherical harmonics.

2.

Let
C_{A}
be a conformal Killing vector, then
{\mathring{D}}^{A}C_{A}
is an
\ell=1
spherical harmonic.

Proof.

1.
Equation (
E.12
) implies that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbf{S}}C({\mathring{D}}_{C}\mathbb{S}^{I})^{AB}C({\mathring{D}}_{C}\mathbb{S}^{I})_{AB}d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbf{S}}{\mathring{D}}^{A}\mathbb{S}^{I}{\mathring{D}}^{B}\underbrace{C({\mathring{D}}_{C}\mathbb{S}^{I})_{AB}}_{k^{2}\mathbb{S}^{I}_{AB}}d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbf{S}}{\mathring{D}}^{A}\mathbb{S}^{I}\frac{(d-k^{2})(d-1)}{d}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\mathbb{S}^{I}d\mu_{\mathring{\gamma}}\,,

(E.18)

which vanishes if and only if
k^{2}=d
; equivalently
\ell=1
. The result follows by noting that the dimensions of both spaces coincide.

2.Taking the divergence of (
C.2
) on a
d
-dimensional sphere with the canonical metric and using the commutation relation (
I.3
), one finds

\frac{2(d-1)}{d}\left(\mathring{\Delta}+d\right){\mathring{D}}^{A}C_{A}=0\,,

(E.19)

which is the scalar spherical-harmonic equation (
E.7
) with
\ell=1
.

E.3
\hat{D}

We now determine the kernels of the operators
\overset{(i)}{\chi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\circ\,C
and
\overset{(i)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)
appearing in (
4.6
) and (
4.21
) on
S^{n-1}
(and hence
{{\varepsilon}}=1
,
\mathring{\mathscr{R}}(h)_{AB}=-h_{AB}
).

We begin with
\overset{(i)}{\chi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\circ\,C
, for which we need the the operator
\mathcal{K}\left(-(i+3),\mathring{\Delta},P\right)
acting on symmetric traceless 2-covariant tensors
h
on
S^{n-1}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}\left(-(i+3),\mathring{\Delta},P\right)h_{AB}\equiv\frac{1}{2i+n-1}\bigg(\frac{2(n-1)}{i(i+n)}P+\mathring{\Delta}+i(i+n)+n-3\bigg)h_{AB}\,.

(E.20)

Using the decomposition (
C.8
), we shall analyse the action of these operators
on
\mathbb{S}^{I}_{AB}
and
\mathbb{V}_{AB}^{I}
separately (we leave out the analysis for
\mathbb{T}^{I}_{AB}
as these are irrelevant here).

First, using (
E.10
) and (
E.16
) we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}\left(-(i+3),\mathring{\Delta},P\right)\mathbb{S}^{I}_{AB}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2i+n-1}\bigg(\frac{2(n-1)}{i(i+n)}P+\mathring{\Delta}+i(i+n)+n-3\bigg)\mathbb{S}^{I}_{AB}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2i+n-1}\bigg(-\frac{2(\ell-1)(n-2)(\ell+n-1)}{i(i+n)}-\ell(\ell+n-2)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+2(n-1)+i(i+n)+n-3\bigg)\mathbb{S}^{I}_{AB}

\displaystyle=\frac{(i+2)(i-\ell+1)(i+n-2)(i+\ell+n-1)}{i(i+n)(2i+n-1)}\mathbb{S}^{I}_{AB}\,,

(E.21)

which gives zero if and only if
\ell=1+i
under our restrictions on the parameters involved. Thus the spherical harmonic tensor
\mathbb{S}^{I}_{AB}
of mode
\ell=1+i
lies in the kernel of
\mathcal{K}\left(-(i+3),\mathring{\Delta},P\right)
. Similarly, using (
E.11
) and (
E.15
) one obtains,

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}\left(-(i+3),\mathring{\Delta},P\right)\mathbb{V}^{I}_{AB}

\displaystyle=\frac{(i+1)(i-\ell+1)(i+n-1)(i+\ell+n-1)}{i(i+n)(2i+n-1)}\mathbb{V}^{I}_{AB}\,,

(E.22)

which gives zero if and only if
\ell=1+i

.

We conclude that:

(i)

spherical harmonic vectors
{\mathring{D}}_{A}\mathbb{S}^{I}
and
\mathbb{V}^{I}_{A}
of modes
\ell=1,2,...,i+1
span the kernel of
\overset{(i)}{\chi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\circ\,C
for
i\geq 0
.

We move on now to
\overset{(1)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)
acting on symmetric traceless 2-covariant tensors
h
on
S^{n-1}
:

\displaystyle\overset{(1)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,h_{AB}

\displaystyle\equiv-\bigg[\frac{4}{n+1}P+1-\frac{1}{2}\mathring{\Delta}+\frac{(n-3)(n-1)}{8}\bigg]h_{AB}\,.

(E.23)

The first term in (
E.23
) gives zero when acting on
h^{[TT]}_{AB}
in which case we are left with

\displaystyle\overset{(1)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,h^{[TT]}_{AB}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\bigg(-2+\mathring{\Delta}-\frac{(n-3)(n-1)}{4}\bigg)h^{[TT]}_{AB}\,.

(E.24)

Since
\mathring{\Delta}
is negative, it follows that
\overset{(1)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)
has no kernel when acting on
{\mathrm{TT}}
tensors.

Next, we consider the action of
\overset{(1)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)
on
\mathbb{V}^{I}_{AB}
. From (
E.11
) and (
E.15
), we have,

\displaystyle\overset{(1)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\mathbb{V}_{AB}^{I}

\displaystyle\equiv-\bigg[\frac{4}{n+1}P+1-\frac{1}{2}\mathring{\Delta}+\frac{(n-3)(n-1)}{8}\bigg]\mathbb{V}_{AB}^{I}

\displaystyle=-\frac{(n-3)(2\ell+n-3)(2\ell+n-1)}{8(n+1)}\mathbb{V}_{AB}^{I}\,,

(E.25)

which gives zero for
n>3
only when
\ell=\frac{1-n}{2},\frac{3-n}{2}
, both of which are negative. Thus the harmonic tensors
\mathbb{V}_{AB}^{I}
do not lie in the kernel of
\overset{(1)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)
.
Meanwhile, a similar calculation using (
E.10
) and (
E.16
) gives,

\displaystyle\overset{(1)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\mathbb{S}_{AB}^{I}

\displaystyle=-\frac{(n-5)(n-3)(2\ell+n-3)(2\ell+n-1)}{8\left(n^{2}-1\right)}\mathbb{S}_{AB}^{I}\,.

(E.26)

Once again, the right-hand side evaluates to zero for
n>5
only when
\ell=\frac{1-n}{2},\frac{3-n}{2}
, both of which are negative. We conclude that, when
n>5
, the operator
\overset{(1)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)
has trivial kernel.

Finally, in order to analyse
\overset{(i)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)
,
i>1
, we need to understand the operators

\mathcal{K}\left(\frac{n-5-2i}{2},\mathring{\Delta},P\right)\equiv\frac{1}{i-1}\bigg(\frac{4(n-1)}{\left((1-2i)^{2}-n^{2}\right)}P+\frac{1}{2}\mathring{\Delta}-1+\frac{(2i-n+1)(2i+n-3)}{8}\bigg)\,.

(E.27)

Proceeding as before, making use of (
E.10
), (
E.7
) and (
E.16
) gives,

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}\left(\frac{n-5-2i}{2},\mathring{\Delta},P\right)

\displaystyle\,\mathbb{S}^{I}_{AB}

\displaystyle\hskip-42.67912pt=\frac{(2i-n+3)(2i+n-5)(2i-2\ell-n+1)(2i+2\ell+n-3)}{8(i-1)(2i-n-1)(2i+n-1)}\mathbb{S}^{I}_{AB}\,.

(E.28)

Keeping in mind that
i>1
and
\ell\geq 0
, the right-hand side vanishes if and only if
i=\frac{n-3}{2}
or
\ell=i-\frac{n-1}{2}
. In addition, making use of (
E.11
), (
E.8
) and (
E.15
) gives,

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}\left(\frac{n-5-2i}{2},\mathring{\Delta},P\right)

\displaystyle\,\mathbb{V}^{I}_{AB}

\displaystyle\hskip-42.67912pt=\frac{(2i-n+1)(2i+n-3)(2i-2\ell-n+1)(2i+2\ell+n-3)}{8(i-1)(2i-n-1)(2i+n-1)}\mathbb{V}^{I}_{AB}\,;

(E.29)

the right-hand side vanishes if and only if
i=\frac{n-1}{2}
or
\ell=i-\frac{n-1}{2}
. Next, making use of (
E.9
), we have,

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}\left(\frac{n-5-2i}{2},\mathring{\Delta},P\right)\mathbb{T}^{I}_{AB}

\displaystyle=\frac{(2i-2\ell-n+1)(2i+2\ell+n-3)}{8(i-1)}\mathbb{T}^{I}_{AB}\,,

(E.30)

the right-hand side of which is zero if and only if
\ell=i-\frac{n-1}{2}
.

Recall that when
n
is odd and
i=\frac{n+1}{2}
we have

\overset{(\frac{n+1}{2})}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)=\mathring{\mathcal{K}}(-3,P)\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2})}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\,.

(E.31)

It can be verified by a similar calculation as above
that
\mathring{\mathcal{K}}(-3,P)
has trivial kernel.

Summarising (cf. Proposition
C.11
):

Proposition
E.2
.

On
S^{d}
,
d\geq 2
, with the canonical metric,

(i)

When
n
is even, the operators
\overset{(i)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)
have trivial kernel for all
i\geq 1
.

(ii)

When
n
is odd, the operators
\overset{(i)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)
have trivial kernel for
i<\frac{n-3}{2}
;
the kernel of the operator
\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)
is spanned by all spherical harmonic tensors
\mathbb{S}^{I}_{AB}
;
the kernels of the operators
\overset{(\frac{n\pm 1}{2})}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)
are spanned by all spherical harmonic tensors
\mathbb{V}^{I}_{AB}
and
\mathbb{S}^{I}_{AB}
; the kernels of the operators
\overset{(\frac{n+1}{2}+j)}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)
for
j\geq 1
are spanned by the spherical harmonic tensors
\mathbb{V}^{I}_{AB}
and
\mathbb{S}^{I}_{AB}
, and spherical harmonic tensors
\mathbb{T}^{I}_{AB}
of modes
\ell\leq j+1
.

E.4
\operatorname{\widehat{L}}

We now consider the gauge operators
\operatorname{\widehat{L}}
appearing in column
5
of Table
5.1
, obtaining their kernels and the kernels of their adjoints.

On
S^{n-1}
the kernel of the operator
\operatorname{\!\!\check{\,\,L}}_{n}
of (
4.43
) is spanned by
\ell=0,1
spherical harmonic functions. This follows from the negativity of the operator
P
and the fact that
\overset{(\frac{n-5}{2})}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)
has trivial kernel by Proposition
E.2
.

For the adjoint operator, again if
n\neq 5
we have

\operatorname{\!\!\check{\,\,L}}_{n}^{\dagger}=c_{n}\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(1)}}\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\bigg((n-1)P-2(n-2)^{2}\bigg)\overset{(\frac{n-5}{2})}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,,

for some constant
c_{n}\neq 0
.
For the tensor fields
\mathbb{S}^{I}_{AB}=\frac{1}{k^{2}}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}\mathbb{S}^{I}]
, we have, after making use of (
E.12
),

\displaystyle\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(1)}}\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\mathbb{S}_{AB}^{I}

\displaystyle=\frac{(k^{2}-n+1)(n-2)}{n-1}\mathbb{S}^{I}\,,

(E.32)

the right-hand side of which vanishes for
k^{2}=n-1
, corresponding to
\ell=1
. We also have

\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(1)}}\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\mathbb{V}_{AB}^{I}=0=\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(1)}}\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\mathbb{T}_{AB}^{I}\,.

(E.33)

We conclude that the kernel of
\operatorname{\!\!\check{\,\,L}}_{n}^{\dagger}
is spanned by the tensors
\mathbb{S}_{AB}^{I}
of mode
\ell=1
, as well as
\mathbb{V}_{AB}^{I}
and
\mathbb{T}_{AB}^{I}
of all modes.

Next, using
(
4.51
)-(
4.52
) the operator
\operatorname{L_{n}}
appearing in (
4.49
) can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\operatorname{L_{n}}(\xi)_{AB}

\displaystyle=\frac{\overset{(\frac{n-5}{2})}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)}{(n-1)(n-5)}\bigg((\mathring{\Delta}-2(n-1))(\mathring{\Delta}-(n+1))C(\xi)_{AB}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-\frac{2(n-3)}{n-2}(\mathring{\Delta}+3-2n)\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}{\mathring{D}}_{C}\xi^{C}]\bigg)\,.

(E.34)

Letting
\xi_{A}=\frac{1}{k^{2}}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\mathbb{S}^{I}
in (
E.34
) gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{k^{2}}\operatorname{L_{n}}({\mathring{D}}_{C}\mathbb{S}^{I})_{AB}=-\overset{(\frac{n-5}{2})}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\frac{\ell(l+1)(n-4)(\ell+n-3)(\ell+n-2)}{(n-5)(n-2)(n-1)}\mathbb{S}^{I}_{AB}\,,

(E.35)

the right-hand side of which vanishes for
\ell=0
, and is non-vanishing for
\ell>0
when
n>3
is odd.
Letting
\xi_{A}=-\frac{1}{k_{V}}\mathbb{V}^{I}_{A}
in (
E.34
) gives

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{k_{V}}\operatorname{L_{n}}(\mathbb{V}^{I})_{AB}=\overset{(\frac{n-5}{2})}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\,\frac{\ell(\ell+1)(\ell+n-3)(\ell+n-2)}{(n-5)(n-1)}\mathbb{V}^{I}_{AB}\,,

(E.36)

the right-hand side of which is non-vanishing for
\ell>0
. We conclude that the kernel of
\operatorname{L_{n}}
consists of conformal Killing vectors.

In order to determine the adjoint operator
\operatorname{L_{n}^{\dagger}}=\underline{\operatorname{L_{n}^{\dagger}}}\circ\overset{(\frac{n-5}{2})}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)
,
the notation of Appendix
J
together with the commutation relations therein are useful; one finds

\displaystyle(\mathring{\Delta}-2(n-1))(\mathring{\Delta}-(n+1))C(\xi)_{AB}=\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}\Big[{\mathring{D}}_{A}(\mathring{\Delta}_{V}+n-3)\mathring{\Delta}_{V}\xi_{B}\big]\,,

(E.37)

\displaystyle(\mathring{\Delta}+3-2n)\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}{\mathring{D}}_{C}\xi^{C}]=\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}\big[{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}_{B}{\mathring{D}}_{C}(\mathring{\Delta}_{V}+1)\xi^{C}\big]\,,

(E.38)

which immediately leads to

\displaystyle\underline{\operatorname{L_{n}^{\dagger}}}(h)_{C}

\displaystyle=\frac{2(n-3)(\mathring{\Delta}_{V}+1)}{(n-5)(n-2)(n-1)}{\mathring{D}}_{C}{\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}-\frac{\mathring{\Delta}_{V}(\mathring{\Delta}_{V}+n-3)}{(n-5)(n-1)}{\mathring{D}}^{A}h_{AC}

\displaystyle=\frac{2(n-3)(\mathring{\Delta}+3-n)}{(n-5)(n-2)(n-1)}{\mathring{D}}_{C}{\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}-\frac{(\mathring{\Delta}-(n-2))(\mathring{\Delta}-1)}{(n-5)(n-1)}{\mathring{D}}^{A}h_{AC}\,.

(E.39)

Clearly,
h^{[TT]}
belongs to the kernel of
\underline{\operatorname{L_{n}^{\dagger}}}
.

Setting
h_{AB}=\mathbb{S}^{I}_{AB}
we have, after making use of (
E.12
)-(
E.13
),

\displaystyle\underline{\operatorname{L_{n}^{\dagger}}}(\mathbb{S}^{I})_{C}

\displaystyle=-\frac{(n-4)\left(k^{2}-n+1\right)\left(k^{2}+n-3\right)}{(n-5)(n-1)^{2}}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\mathbb{S}^{I}\,,

(E.40)

the right-hand side of which is only vanishing for
k^{2}=n-1
for
n
’s of current interest, which corresponds to the mode
\ell=1
.

Setting, next, setting
h_{AB}=\mathbb{V}_{AB}^{I}
in (
E.39
) and making use of (
E.14
) gives

\displaystyle\underline{\operatorname{L_{n}^{\dagger}}}(\mathbb{V}^{I})_{C}

\displaystyle=\frac{\left(2-k_{V}^{2}\right)\left(k_{V}^{2}-n+1\right)\left(k_{V}^{2}-n+2\right)}{2k_{V}(n-5)(n-1)}\mathbb{V}^{I}_{C}\,,

(E.41)

the right-hand side of which vanishes for
k_{V}^{2}=2,n-1,n-2
, the last of which corresponding to the mode
\ell=1
.
We conclude that the kernel of
\underline{\operatorname{L_{n}^{\dagger}}}
, and hence of
\operatorname{L_{n}^{\dagger}}
, is spanned by
{\mathrm{TT}}
tensors,
together with
\mathbb{V}^{I}_{AB}
’s and
\mathbb{S}^{I}_{AB}
’s of mode
\ell=1
.

Appendix F
The matrices
\Lambda^{[X]}_{k}

F.1
Ellipticity

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial_{u}^{k_{n}}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}_{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial_{u}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}_{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}_{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\upsilon_{k,2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overset{[\frac{9-n}{2}]}{w}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overset{[1]}{w}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overset{[5]}{w}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overset{[4+k]}{w}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overset{(k)}{r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{n+1}{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ddots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overset{(\frac{n+1}{2})}{r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{n+1}{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{n+1}{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{n+1}{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2})}{r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
\frac{n+1}{2}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{n-1}{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{2}}

Figure F.2:
Submatrix of
\Lambda_{k}
corresponding to block
C
of figure
F.1
. A choice of
\frac{s_{i}}{2}
and
\frac{t_{j}}{2}
’s required to show ellipticity of
\Lambda_{k}
is also indicated in the first column and first row respectively. For convenience and without loss of generality, we have assumed a sufficiently large
k
(
k>n-1
) here.

In this appendix we show that for each
X\in\{S,V\}
, the matrix of operators
\Lambda^{[X]}_{k}
of (
6.166
) is elliptic in the sense of Agmon, Douglis and Nirenberg.

First, we recall the definition of ellipticity in the sense of Agmon, Douglis and Nirenberg (ADN). Consider a differential system on
\mathbf{S}
of the form

\displaystyle\Lambda({\mathring{D}})v(x)=u(x)

(F.1)

where
v(x)
and
u(x)
are
N
-vector valued functions on
\mathbf{S}
and
\Lambda
is a
N\times N
matrix of linear partial differential operators, with each
(\Lambda({\mathring{D}}))^{i}{}_{j}
being a polynomial in
{\mathring{D}}_{A}
. Here and below, the notation
(A)^{i}{}_{j}
denotes the
ij
-entry of a matrix
A
. The system (
F.1
) is said to be
elliptic
if there exists integers
s_{i},t_{j}
with
i,j\in[1,N]
, such that the order of the operator
(\Lambda)^{i}{}_{j}
does not exceed
s_{i}+t_{j}
and furthermore

\displaystyle\det\Lambda^{0}(k)\neq 0\ \text{for all}\ k\neq 0\ \text{vectors}\,.

(F.2)

In (
F.2
),
\Lambda^{0}
denotes the matrix obtained from
\Lambda
by keeping only those operators of order exactly
s_{i}+t_{j}
in each
(\Lambda)^{i}{}_{j}
entry.

To show that the matrix of operators
\Lambda^{[X]}_{k}
of (
6.166
) is elliptic in the sense just defined we choose
s_{i}\,,t_{j}
as (see also Figure
F.1
and
F.2
)

\displaystyle(s_{i})

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}2\times(\frac{n-1}{2}\,,2\,,3\,,\dots\,,\frac{n-3}{2}\,,2\,,3\,,\dots\,,k+1)\,,&k=\frac{n-3}{2}\,,\\
2\times(k+1\,,k\,,k-1\,,...\,,\frac{n-1}{2}\,,2\,,3\,,\dots\,,\frac{n-3}{2}\,,2\,,3\,,\dots\,,k+1)\,,&k\geq\frac{n-1}{2}\,,\end{cases}

(F.3)

\displaystyle(t_{j})

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}(0\,,\dots\,,0)\,,&k=\frac{n-3}{2}\\
2\times(\underbrace{-k_{n}\,,-(k_{n}-1)\,,\dots\,,-2,-1}_{k_{n}\text{ terms}}\,,\underbrace{0\,,\dots\,,0}_{2k-k_{n}\text{ terms}})\,,&k\geq\frac{n-1}{2}\,,\end{cases}

(F.4)

(recall that
k_{n}
has been defined in (
6.165
)).
Let
\Lambda_{k}^{[0,X]}
denote the matrix obtained by
keeping in
(\Lambda_{k}^{[X]})^{i}{}_{j}

only those operators which are of order
s_{i}+t_{j}
.
We wish to show
that
\Lambda_{k}^{[0,X]}
is a lower triangular matrix,
with the diagonal entries being

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\big(d_{2}^{X}\,,(\overset{(1)}{\tilde{\psi}}\,,\overset{(2)}{\tilde{\psi}}\,,\dots\,,\overset{(\frac{n-5}{2})}{\tilde{\psi}}\,,\overset{(1)}{\tilde{\chi}}\,,\overset{(2)}{\tilde{\chi}}\,,\dots\,\overset{(k)}{\tilde{\chi}})\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C\big)\,,\\
\big(\underbrace{\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\operatorname{L_{n}}\,,\dots\,,\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\operatorname{L_{n}}}_{k_{n}\text{ terms}}\,,\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ{\operatorname{L_{n}}}\,,d_{2}^{X}\,,(\overset{(1)}{\tilde{\psi}}\,,\overset{(2)}{\tilde{\psi}}\,,\dots\,,\overset{(\frac{n-5}{2})}{\tilde{\psi}}\,,\overset{(1)}{\tilde{\chi}}\,,\overset{(2)}{\tilde{\chi}}\,,\dots\,\overset{(k)}{\tilde{\chi}})\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C\,,\big)\,,\end{cases}

(F.5)

with the first case for
k=\frac{n-3}{2}
and the second case for
k\geq\frac{n-1}{2}
, and with

d_{2}^{X}:=\begin{cases}\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\underline{\operatorname{\!\!\check{\,\,L}}}_{n}\,,&X=S\,,\\
\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{\tilde{\psi}}\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C\,,&X=V\,,\end{cases}

(F.6)

where the operators
\overset{(p)}{\tilde{\psi}}=\overset{(p)}{\tilde{\psi}}(\mathring{\Delta},\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C)
,
\overset{(p)}{\tilde{\chi}}=\overset{(p)}{\tilde{\chi}}(\mathring{\Delta},\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C)
and
\underline{\operatorname{\!\!\check{\,\,L}}}_{n}\,,\operatorname{L_{n}}
are the gauge operators defined in Section
4.3
. We emphasise that the operators appearing in (
F.5
) are all elliptic (see Appendix
C
).

By definition of
u_{k}
and
\upsilon_{k}
, it is clear that the terms in (
F.5
) will appear in the respective spots on the diagonal of
\Lambda_{k}^{[0,X]}
. Indeed, this follows directly from the transport equations and the gauge transformation of
\overset{(p)}{q}_{AB}
for
p\geq\frac{n-3}{2}
. It remains to show that
\Lambda_{k}^{[0,X]}
is lower-triangular.

First, consider the submatrix of
\Lambda^{[X]}_{k}
denoted as
A_{1}
in Figure
F.1
. For these entries, the analysis is similar to the convenient case – in each row, the operators involved are either
\overset{(i)}{\tilde{\psi}}_{n}\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ C
or
\overset{(i)}{\tilde{\chi}}_{n}\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ C
for some
n\in\{\frac{9-n}{2},4+k\}
. By the same reasoning as items (ii) of Section
(i)
, p.
(i)
and Section
6.4.2
, p.
6.4.2
, the operator of highest order amongst the
\overset{(i)}{\tilde{\psi}}_{n}
’s, resp.
\overset{(i)}{\tilde{\chi}}_{n}
’s, is

\overset{(i)}{\tilde{\psi}}(\mathring{\Delta},\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C)\,,\quad\text{resp.}\quad\overset{(i)}{\tilde{\chi}}(\mathring{\Delta},\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C)\,,

(F.7)

which is of order
2i
; all other
\overset{(i)}{\tilde{\psi}}_{n}
and
\overset{(i)}{\tilde{\chi}}_{n}
operators are of lower order. We note for later use that this is also true for the block
A_{2}
in Figure
F.1
.
The above justifies that the submatrix
A_{1}
of
\Lambda^{[0,X]}_{k}
is lower triangular.

Next, we consider the operators acting on the various “gauge fields” appearing in (
6.155
). Most
gauge transformations of the fields
{\mathring{D}}^{A}\overset{(i)}{q}_{AB}
. From (
6.148
), we have

\displaystyle{\mathring{D}}^{A}\overset{(i)}{q}_{AB}

\displaystyle=\partial_{u}{\mathring{D}}^{A}\overset{(i-1)}{q}_{AB}-\underbrace{\overset{(i-1)}{\tilde{\psi}}(\mathring{\Delta},\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C)}_{{\text{order $2(i-1)$}}}\,{\mathring{D}}^{A}\hat{q}_{AB}^{(n-5-2i)/2}-m^{i-1}\underbrace{\overset{(i-1)}{\psi}_{[m]}}_{{\text{order $0$}}}{\mathring{D}}^{A}\hat{q}^{(\frac{n-7-2(i-1)(n-1)}{2})}_{AB}

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{j=1}^{p-1}m^{j}\underbrace{\overset{(i-1)}{\tilde{\psi}}_{j,0}(\mathring{\Delta},\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C)}_{{\text{order $2(i-1-j)$}}}\,{\mathring{D}}^{A}\hat{q}^{(\frac{1}{2}(n-7-2(i-1)-2j(n-2)))}_{AB}\,,\quad 1\leq i\leq k\,.

(F.8)

Using (
3.101
), p.
3.101
and (
A.51
), p.
A.51
it can be shown inductively
that for each
1\leq i\leq\frac{n-3}{2}
, in the gauge transformation of
{\mathring{D}}^{A}\overset{(i)}{q}_{AB}
,

1.

the operators acting on the gauge fields
{\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi{}^{u}
and
\xi_{A}
are both

of order
2(i+1)
,

2.

the order of the operators acting on
\partial^{j}_{u}\xi_{A}
,
1\leq j\leq i-1
, is
2(i-j-1)

(relevant for region
B
in Figure
F.1
).

To see this, note that the gauge transformation law of
{\mathring{D}}^{A}\hat{q}^{(n)}_{AB}
, given in (
A.51
), involves only the gauge fields
{\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi^{u}
,
\xi_{A}
and
\partial_{u}\xi_{A}
. The associated operators acting on these fields are of orders four, four and two
respectively. Thus, modulo the first term in (
F.8
), the operators of the highest order in the gauge transformation of
{\mathring{D}}^{A}\overset{(i)}{q}_{AB}
must come from the second term in (
F.8
), and are given by that acting on
{\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi^{u}
and
\xi_{A}
. Furthermore, they are of order
2(i+1)
.
Next, the fact that the operators acting on
\partial^{j}_{u}\xi_{A}
,
1\leq j\leq i-1
, are of order
2(i-(j-1))

follows easily by induction; we leave the details to the reader, after using (
3.101
) for initialisation.

Concerning the first line of region
B
of Figure
F.1
, we note that the part of items 1. and 2. above concerning
\xi_{A}
and its
u
-derivatives continues to hold for the gauge transformation of
\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2})}{q}_{AB}
.
By a similar analysis for the gauge transformation of the
\overset{(i)}{H}_{uA}
fields,
using the recursion formula (
4.11
), and (
3.57
) and (
3.62
) for initialisation, we have:
for each
1\leq i\leq k
, the operators acting on the gauge fields
{\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi{}^{u}
and
\xi_{A}
are

of order
2(i+1)
,
while those acting on
\partial^{j}_{u}\xi_{A}
,
1\leq j\leq i+1
, are of order

2(i-(j-1))
.
Thus, in the region
B
, the order of the operator appearing at an
(ij)
-entry is lower than or equal to
s_{i}+t_{j}
.

For the entry immediately to the left of
D_{2}
, we have
(\Lambda^{[X]}_{k})^{k_{n}+1}{}_{k_{n}+3}=0
, which follows from (
6.149
) with
p=\frac{n-1}{2}
.
Next,
we also have
(\Lambda^{[V]}_{k})^{k_{n}+1}{}_{k_{n}+2}=0
, while
(\Lambda^{[S]}_{k})^{k_{n}+1}{}_{k_{n}+2}

is of order
n-1
.
The first equality follows from the fact that the field
\overset{[2]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}_{AB}
does not appear in the integrated transport equation (
6.155
) of
\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2})}{q}_{AB}
. The second statement follows from the following calculation of the gauge term associated to
{\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi^{u}
in the gauge transformation of
\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2})}{q}_{AB}
:
Namely, using (
A.51
), (
F.8
), and what has been said so far, this term is of the form

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{(n-2)r}

\displaystyle\underbrace{\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)\circ P\circ\,C({\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi^{u})}_{=0}

\displaystyle+{\text{an operator of order $(n-1)$ acting on}}\ {\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi^{u}\,,

(F.9)

where the second term comes from the last term on the right-hand side of (
F.8
) with
i=\frac{n-3}{2}
and
j=1
.
The first term vanishes by the fact that

\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{\psi}(\mathring{\Delta},P)h^{[S]}=0\,,

(F.10)

see (
C.5
).

That the operators appearing in region
D_{2}
in Figure
F.1
are all of orders
\leq\frac{n-1}{2}
follows from the same argument as that around (
F.7
) (for regions
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
). For the region
D_{1}
, the orders of the operators appearing in each row are lower than or equal to
2i
, since the would-be-of-highest-order-
2(i+1)
operator
\overset{(i)}{\tilde{\psi}}\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ C
vanishes for
i>\frac{n-1}{2}

by (
4.31
), Proposition
4.1
.

Let us turn our attention now to the first
k_{n}
-lines of Figure
F.1
(region
C
).
The vanishing of the first term in (
F.9
) modifies the order of the operators appearing in the gauge transformation of
\overset{(i)}{q}_{AB}
,
i\geq\frac{n+1}{2}
,
as compared to point 2. below (
F.8
). Indeed, restarting the induction from
\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2})}{q}_{AB}
, it follows from (
F.8
) (where now the second term at the right-hand side vanishes) that

3.

the operators acting on the gauge fields
{\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi^{u}
and
\xi_{A}
are both

of order
2i
;

4.

that acting on
\partial_{u}^{j}\xi_{A}
is

of order
2(i-j)

for
1\leq j\leq i-\frac{n+1}{2}
, and

of order
2\big(i-(j-1)\big)

for
i-\frac{n-1}{2}\leq j\leq i-1
.

One readily verifies that the choice (
F.3
) and (
F.4
) results in a lower triangular matrix block
C
of
Figure
F.1
. See also Figure
F.2
.

Next, we consider the “gauge fields” appearing in the integrated transport equations (
6.155
) for
\overset{(i)}{r}
and (
6.156
) for
\overset{(i)}{s}
, arising from the gauge corrections (
5.2
) at
r_{2}
of the field
\tilde{h}_{AB}
. These involve only the gauge fields
\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}_{A}
and
{\mathring{D}}_{A}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{u}
. The highest order operators acting on these fields are (cf. (
6.147
) and (
6.60
)),
up to multiplicative constants,

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\tilde{\psi}}\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ C\quad\text{and}\quad\overset{(i)}{\tilde{\chi}}\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ C

(F.11)

respectively for
\overset{(i)}{r}
,
1\leq i\leq\frac{n-3}{2}
, and
\overset{(i)}{s}
,
1\leq i\leq k
, both of order
2(i+1)
. For
\overset{(i)}{r}
,
i>\frac{n-3}{2}
the highest order operators are

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\tilde{\psi}}_{1,0}\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ C\,,

(F.12)

of order
2i
.

Finally, we come to the row associated to the field
\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{r}
. Specifically, for the term on the diagonal, it follows directly from the transport equation (
6.155
) with
i=\frac{n-3}{2}

that this term is of order
{n-1}
for

X=V
. This remains true for
X=S

(cf. previous paragraph item 1 below (
F.8
)).

We now justify the first
k_{n}+1
entries of equation (
F.5
). The analysis in Section
4.3
of the main text shows that in the gauge transformation formulae
of
\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2}+j)}{r}
for
j=0,...,k_{n}
, respectively
\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{r}
, the operators
\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\operatorname{L_{n}}
acting on the field
\partial^{j}_{u}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{A}
, resp.
\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\underline{\operatorname{\!\!\check{\,\,L}}}_{n}
acting on
{\mathring{D}}_{A}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{u}
,
appear on the diagonal of
\Lambda_{k}
.
It is not difficult to see that these are
the only operators of order
{n+1}
acting on
\partial^{j}_{u}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{A}
, respectively of order
{n-1}
acting on
{\mathring{D}}_{A}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{u}
.
Indeed, a comparison of length-dimensions of the fields
\partial^{j}_{u}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{A}
and
\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2}+j)}{r}
, resp.
{\mathring{D}}_{A}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}^{u}
and
\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{r}
, shows that
the gauge operators must be dimensionless. Thus each term contributing to these operators must a) either be
r
-independent, in which case we obtain

\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\underline{\operatorname{\!\!\check{\,\,L}}}_{n}
, of order
{n+1}
, for

\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2}+j)}{r}
and
\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\operatorname{\!\!\check{\,\,L}}_{n}
, of order
{n-1}
, for
\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{r}
; or
b)
come with the dimensionless combination
m^{i}\alpha^{2\ell}r^{-i(n-2)+2\ell}
for some
0\leq i,\ell
, and
i+\ell\geq 1
; the analysis involving
\alpha
is included for later reference in Appendix
G
. However, looking at (
A.35
)-(
A.37
) and (
A.51
) we see that each factor of
m
or
\alpha^{2}
will decrease the order of the operator by two.
It ensues that a gauge operator associated with the field
\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2}+j)}{r}
, respectively with the field
\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{r}
, and containing a prefactor
m^{i}\alpha^{2\ell}
is of order
{n+1}-2i-2\ell
, respectively
{n-1}-2i-2\ell
. This justifies the first
k_{n}+1
entries of equation (
F.5
).

It follows that
\Lambda_{k}^{[0,X]}
is lower triangular, with elliptic operators lying on the diagonal.

F.2
Mode solvability

In this section, we explain the mode solvability of the system (
6.166
).
Suppose that all entries of
u_{k}^{[X]}
,
\upsilon_{k}^{[X]}
and
\rho_{k}^{[X]}
are eigenvectors of the Laplace operator with the same eigenvalue
-\lambda_{\ell}\geq 0
. We then have

\Lambda_{k}^{[X]}\upsilon_{k}^{[X]}=B_{k,\ell}^{[X]}\upsilon_{k}^{[X]}\,,\qquad\mathcal{N}^{[X]}_{k}\,\rho_{k}^{[X]}=D_{k,\ell}^{[X]}\rho_{k}^{[X]}\,,

with real-valued matrices
B_{k,\ell}^{[X]}
and
D_{k,\ell}^{[X]}
. The
(ij)
entries
(B_{k,\ell}^{[X]})^{i}{}_{j}
of the matrix
B_{k,\ell}^{[X]}
are polynomials in
\lambda_{\ell}
of order lower than or
equal to

(s_{i}+t_{j})/2
(recall that all
s_{i}
’s and
t_{j}
’s are even), or vanish. The system

\displaystyle u_{k}^{[X]}=\Lambda_{k}^{[X]}\upsilon_{k}^{[X]}+\mathcal{N}_{k}^{[X]}\rho_{k}^{[X]}

(F.13)

becomes

\displaystyle u_{k}^{[X]}=B_{k,\ell}^{[X]}\upsilon_{k}^{[X]}+D_{k,\ell}^{[X]}\rho_{k}^{[X]}\,.

(F.14)

For
\lambda_{\ell}\neq 0
define the (invertible) diagonal matrices
A_{\ell}
and
C_{\ell}
as

A_{\ell}=\mbox{\rm diag}(\lambda_{\ell}^{-s_{i}/2})\,,\quad C_{\ell}=\mbox{\rm diag}(\lambda_{\ell}^{-t_{i}/2})\,.

The equation (
F.14
) can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\check{u}^{[X]}:=A_{\ell}u_{k}^{[X]}=\underbrace{A_{\ell}B_{k,\ell}^{[X]}C_{\ell}}_{=:\check{B}^{[X]}}\underbrace{C_{\ell}^{-1}\upsilon_{k}^{[X]}}_{=:\check{\upsilon}^{[X]}}+A_{\ell}D_{k,\ell}^{[X]}\rho_{k}^{[X]}\,.

(F.15)

The
(ij)
entry of the matrix
A_{\ell}B_{k,\ell}^{[X]}C_{\ell}
equals

(A_{\ell}B_{k,\ell}^{[X]}C_{\ell})^{i}{}_{j}=\lambda_{\ell}^{-s_{i}/2}(B_{k,\ell}^{[X]})^{i}{}_{j}\lambda_{\ell}^{-t_{j}/2}\,.

Since
|\lambda_{\ell}|\to_{\ell\to\infty}\infty
, from what has been said the matrix

A_{\ell}B_{k,\ell}^{[X]}C_{\ell}
tends to a lower-triangular matrix with non-zero entries at the diagonal when
\ell
tends to infinity. It follows that there exists
N
so that, for any given
\check{u}^{[X]}
and
\rho_{k}^{[X]}
, the system (
F.15
)

\displaystyle\check{u}^{[X]}=\check{B}^{[X]}\check{\upsilon}^{[X]}+A_{\ell}^{[X]}D_{k,\ell}^{[X]}\rho_{k}^{[X]}

(F.16)

has a solution
\check{\upsilon}^{[X]}
for all
\ell\geq N
:

\displaystyle\check{\upsilon}^{[X]}=(\check{B}^{[X]})^{-1}(\check{u}^{[X]}-A_{\ell}D_{k,\ell}^{[X]}\rho_{k}^{[X]})\,.

(F.17)

Setting

\displaystyle u_{k}^{[X]}=(A_{\ell})^{-1}\check{u}^{[X]}\,,\quad\upsilon_{k}^{[X]}=C_{\ell}\check{\upsilon}^{[X]}\,,

(F.18)

one obtains a solution of the original system (
3.57
).

F.3
ADN estimates

Writing

(u_{k})_{i}
for the components of the vector
u_{k}
, similarly for
\upsilon_{k}
and

\rho_{k}
, we set

\|u_{k}\|_{{\mycal H}^{\ell}}=\sum_{i}\|(u_{k})_{i}\|_{H^{s-s_{i}+\ell}(\mathbf{S})}\,,\quad\|\upsilon_{k}\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\ell}}=\sum_{j}\|(\upsilon_{k})_{j}\|_{H^{s+t_{j}+\ell}(\mathbf{S})}\,,\quad s=\max\{s_{i}\}\,.

(F.19)

The spaces
{\mycal H}^{\ell}
and
{\mathcal{H}}^{\ell}
are defined as completions of
C^{\infty}
in the above norms.

From what has been said we infer:

Theorem
F.1
.

Let
(s_{i},t_{j})
be as defined above, and let
X\in\{S\cap{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}},V\cap{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}\}
(cf. Section
C.3
).
For

\ell\geq 0

the maps

\displaystyle\big({\mycal H}^{\ell}\cap X\big)\times\big(C^{\infty}\cap X\big)\ni(\upsilon^{[X]}_{k},\rho^{[X]}_{k})\mapsto\Lambda^{[X]}_{k}\upsilon^{[X]}_{k}+\mathcal{N}^{[X]}_{k}\rho^{[X]}_{k}\in{\mathcal{H}}^{\ell}\cap X

(F.20)

are surjective, and for every
k\geq 0
there exists a constant
C
so that the Agmon, Douglis, Nirenberg elliptic estimates hold:

\|\upsilon^{[X]}_{k}\|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{\ell}}\leq C\big(\|u^{[X]}_{k}\|_{{\mycal H}^{\ell}}+\|\rho^{[X]}_{k}\|_{{\mycal H}^{\ell}}+\|\upsilon^{[X]}_{k}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbf{S})}\big)\,.

(F.21)

Proof.

Recall that the entries of the
\Lambda^{[X]}_{k}
-matrices are operators acting on vector fields. The ADN principal symbol of
\Lambda^{[X]}_{k}
is obtained by replacing each entry
(\Lambda_{k}^{[0,X]})^{i}{}_{j}
of
\Lambda_{k}^{[0,X]}
by a square matrix, namely the principal symbol of the operator
(\Lambda_{k}^{[0,X]})^{i}{}_{j}
.

Now, the principal part of each of the operators acting on these blocks is proportional to some power of the Laplacian, in particular the principal symbol of each block lying on the diagonal is diagonal. Hence we obtain a lower-triangular matrix, where the determinant of each block on the diagonal is non-zero for
k_{A}\neq 0
by ellipticity of the Laplace operator. Ellipticity in the ADN sense follows. The estimate (
F.21
) follows from
(
MorreyNirenberg
, Theorem C)
.

\Box

We note:

Corollary
F.2
.

Let
k_{\gamma}\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
, suppose that
n
is odd and let
k_{n}
be as in (
6.165
).
Under the hypotheses of Theorem
F.1
, assume moreover that
k_{\gamma}-2k-1\geq 0
. Then

\displaystyle\forall\ j=1,\ \cdots,k_{n}:\ \partial_{u}^{j}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}_{A}\in H^{k_{\gamma}+1-2j}(\mathbf{S})\,,

(F.22)

\displaystyle\xi^{A}\in H^{k_{\gamma}+1}(\mathbf{S})\,,\quad\xi^{u}\in H^{k_{\gamma}+2}(\mathbf{S})\,,\quad\overset{[p]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}{}^{[{\mathrm{TT}^{\perp}}]}\in H^{k_{\gamma}}(\mathbf{S})\,,

(F.23)

for
p\in([\frac{9-n}{2},1]\cup[5,4+k])\cap\mathbbm{Z}
.

Proof.

Recall that

\overset{(i)}{r},\overset{(i)}{s}\in H^{k_{\gamma}-2i-1}\,.

(F.24)

Meanwhile, the associated
s_{i}
for each field
\overset{(i)}{r}
or
\overset{(i)}{s}
appearing in
\Lambda_{k}
equals
2(i+1)
(cf. (
F.4
)). Thus
s=\max{s_{i}}=2(k+1)
. To make use of the estimate (
F.21
), we require
u_{k}\in{\mycal H}^{\ell}
, which would be true for finite
k_{\gamma}
if

s-s_{i}+\ell\leq k_{\gamma}-2i-1\implies\ell\leq k_{\gamma}-2k-1\,.

Thus in particular, we can take
\ell=k_{\gamma}-2k-1
. The estimate (
F.21
) then gives

(v_{k})_{j}\in H^{s+t_{j}+\ell}=H^{k_{\gamma}+1+t_{j}}\,.

(F.25)

Finally, the choice of
t_{j}
’s (cf. (
F.3
)) for each
\partial_{u}^{j}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}_{A}
,
j\in[0,k_{n}]
, is
t_{j}=-2j
, while that for each
\overset{[p]}{w}
is
t_{j}=0
. The regularity (
F.22
)-(
F.23
) follows after recalling that
\overset{[p]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}{}^{[{\mathrm{TT}^{\perp}}]}=C(\overset{[p]}{w})
.

Appendix G
(n,k)
inconvenient,
m\alpha\neq 0

The conditions for the interpolating fields
\overset{[i]}{\hat{\varphi}}{}_{AB}
and gauge fields in the case of inconvenient pairs
(n,k)
and
m\alpha\neq 0
are very similar to that for
\alpha=0
. For completeness we briefly explain the procedure required here; notations are as defined in, or analogous to those of Section
6.4.3
, and will be used without further comments.

To begin, for inconvenient pairs
(n,k)
and
m\alpha\neq 0
,
it follows from (
6.108
) (cf. also (
4.17
)) that (
6.155
) gains additional terms arising from
\alpha
:

\displaystyle\underbrace{{\mathring{D}}^{A}\overset{(p)}{r}{}^{[{\mathrm{TT}^{\perp}}]}_{AB}}_{=:\overset{(p)}{r}_{B}}

\displaystyle=\text{gauge fields }+\overset{(p)}{\tilde{\psi}}(\mathring{\Delta},\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C)\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C(\overset{[\frac{7-n+2p}{2}]}{w})

\displaystyle\quad+\alpha^{2p}\overset{(p)}{\psi}_{[\alpha]}\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C(\overset{[\frac{7-n-2p}{2}]}{w})+m^{p}\overset{(p)}{\psi}_{[m]}\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C(\overset{[\frac{7-n+2p(n-1)}{2}]}{w})

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j,\ell}^{p_{**}}m^{j}\alpha^{2\ell}\overset{(p)}{\tilde{\psi}}_{j,\ell}(\mathring{\Delta},\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C)\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C(\overset{[\frac{7-n+2p}{2}+j(n-2)-2\ell]}{w})\,,\quad 1\leq p\leq k\,,

(G.1)

with
\overset{(p)}{\psi}_{[\alpha]}=0
for
p>\frac{n-1}{2}

(which follows from (
4.12
) and (
4.21
)),
and with the field
\overset{(p)}{r}_{AB}
defined analogously as (
6.149
).

In what follows it will be relevant to keep track of the
{\mathrm{CKV}}
- and
{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}
-parts of the equation, so we note that (
G.1
) has been obtained by applying
\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}
to (
6.108
), hence all the terms there are in
{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}
. Further note that both in (
G.1
) and in the equations that follow, the
{\mathrm{CKV}}
-part of the
\overset{[\cdot]}{w}
fields drops out because

\big(\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C\big)\big|_{{\mathrm{CKV}}}=0\,.

(G.2)

Similarly to the
\alpha=0
case, in order to simplify notation we group terms together and
rewrite (
G.1
) as

\displaystyle\overset{(p)}{r}_{B}=\text{gauge fields}+\sum_{j=4-n}^{\frac{7-n+2p(n-1)}{2}}\overset{(p)}{\tilde{\psi}}_{j}\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C(\overset{[j]}{w})\,,\quad 1\leq p\leq k\,,

(G.3)

with some operators
\overset{(p)}{\tilde{\psi}}_{j}
. As easily seen from (
G.1
), for each
1\leq p\leq\frac{n-1}{2}
, we have

\overset{(p)}{\tilde{\psi}}_{\frac{7-n-2p}{2}}=\alpha^{2p}\overset{(p)}{\psi}_{[\alpha]}\neq 0\,.

(G.4)

Similarly, the integrated transport equation for
\overset{(p)}{H}{}^{[{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}]}_{uA}
, namely the
{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}
-projection of (
6.60
), gains additional terms arising from
\alpha
(compare (
6.157
)), and reads:

\displaystyle\overset{(p)}{s}_{A}

\displaystyle=(\text{gauge fields})^{[{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}]}+\overset{(p)}{\tilde{\chi}}(\mathring{\Delta},\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C)\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C(\overset{[p+4]}{w})

\displaystyle\quad+m^{p}\overset{(p)}{\tilde{\chi}}_{[m]}\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C(\overset{[p(n-1)+4]}{w})

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j,\ell}^{p_{*}}m^{j}\alpha^{2\ell}\overset{(p)}{\chi}_{j,\ell}(\mathring{\Delta},\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C)\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C(\overset{[(p+4)+j(n-2)-2\ell]}{w})\,,

(G.5)

where
0\leq p\leq k
.
As above, we rewrite (
G.5
) as:

\displaystyle\overset{(p)}{s}_{A}

\displaystyle=(\text{gauge fields})^{[{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}]}+\sum_{j=4}^{p(n-1)+4}\overset{(p)}{\tilde{\chi}}_{j}\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C(\overset{[j]}{w})\,.

(G.6)

with some operators
\overset{(p)}{\tilde{\chi}}_{j}
.

Recall that the
\overset{(p)}{s}_{A}
’s are in
{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}
by definition.

Before continuing, we summarise the main differences between the case here and that when
\alpha=0
:

1.

the addition of the new
\alpha
terms in (
G.1
) leads to the coupling of the equations for
\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{r}_{A}
, which now has an
\alpha^{n-3}\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{\psi}_{[\alpha]}\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C(\overset{[5-n]}{w})
term, with that for
\chi
(cf. (
6.58
)). In addition, the equation for
\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2})}{r}_{A}
, which now has an
\alpha^{n-4}\overset{(\frac{n-4}{2})}{\psi}_{[\alpha]}\,\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C(\overset{[4-n]}{w})
term, is now coupled with that for
\overset{(*)}{H}{}^{[{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}]}_{uA}
(cf. (
6.56
)).

2.

The occurrence of new
\alpha
terms in (
G.1
) and (
G.5
), in particular of the non-vanishing terms involving the field
C(\overset{[4]}{w})
, requires us to include the case
p=0
to (
G.5
), whereas
p=0
was decoupled in the
\alpha=0
case.

In view of point 1. above, we now rewrite the equations for the fields
\chi
and
\overset{(*)}{H}_{uA}
into a form that is compatible with (
G.1
) and (
G.5
). For the field
\chi
(cf. (
6.58
)), we continue to use (
6.33
)-(
6.34
)
to take care of the projection
\chi^{[\text{im}(\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}})^{\perp}]}
.
For the projection
\chi^{[\text{im}(\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}})]}
, we have the obvious inclusion
{\mathrm{TT}}\subseteq\ker\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}}
. This, together with (
J.12
) and (
J.13
) shows that

V\oplus{\mathrm{TT}}\subseteq\ker\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}}\implies\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}}(h_{AB})=\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}}(h^{[S]}_{AB})\,.

Furthermore, we have

{\mathring{D}}_{A}\chi^{[\text{im}(\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}})]}\in{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}
, since for any
\xi\in{\mathrm{CKV}}
and any field
\phi\in\text{im}\,\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}}
, thus
\phi=\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}}h={\mathring{D}}^{A}{\mathring{D}}^{B}h_{AB}
for some tensor
h
, it holds that

\int_{\mathbf{S}}\xi^{A}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\phi=\int_{\mathbf{S}}\xi^{A}{\mathring{D}}_{A}{\mathring{D}}^{C}{\mathring{D}}^{D}h_{CD}=\int_{\mathbf{S}}\mathop{\mathrm{TS}}[{\mathring{D}}^{C}{\mathring{D}}^{D}{\mathring{D}}_{A}\xi^{A}]h_{CD}=0\,,

(G.7)

where the last equality follows the calculations in Lemma
C.3
(cf. (
C.7
)).
Thus, taking the gradient of the projection of (
6.58
) onto
\text{im}\,\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}}
gives,

\displaystyle\underbrace{{\mathring{D}}_{A}((\chi|_{\mathbf{S}_{2}}-\chi|_{{\mathbf{S}}_{1}})^{[\text{im}\,\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}}]})}_{=:\overset{(*)}{r}_{A}}=\text{gauge fields}+\frac{n-3}{n-1}\hskip-17.07182pt\underbrace{{\mathring{D}}_{A}\big(\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}}\circ\,C(\overset{[5-n]}{w}{}^{[S]})\big)}_{\big((\mathring{\Delta}-(n-2){{\varepsilon}})\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C(\overset{[5-n]}{w}{}^{[S]})\big)_{A}}\hskip-17.07182pt\,.

(G.8)

As can be readily verified, we have
\text{im}\,\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}}\subseteq(\ker\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(1)}})^{\perp}
. Thus solving (
G.8
) is equivalent to solving the transport equation for the projected field
(\chi^{[\text{im}\,\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}}]})^{[(\ker\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(1)}})^{\perp}]}=\chi^{[\text{im}\,\!\operatorname{\mathring{\,L}}]}
and completes the gluing of
\chi
.

Next, we move on to the field
\overset{(*)}{H}{}^{[{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}]}_{uA}
.
For this, we take the
{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}
projection of (
6.56
), which results in

\displaystyle\underbrace{(\overset{(*)}{H}_{uA}|_{\mathbf{S}_{2}}-\overset{(*)}{H}_{uA}|_{\mathbf{S}_{1}})^{[{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}]}}_{=:\overset{(*)}{s}_{A}}=(\text{gauge fields})^{[{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}]}-(n-1)\ \operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C(\overset{[4-n]}{w})\,.

(G.9)

We are ready now to write the full coupled system in matrix form. Indeed,
the system (
G.3
), (
G.6
), (
G.8
) and (
G.9
) can be written as (cf. (
6.166
) in the
\alpha=0
case)

\displaystyle u_{k}^{[X]}=A^{[X]}_{k}\Theta^{[X]}_{k}+\Lambda^{[X]}_{k}\upsilon_{k}^{[X]}+\mathcal{N}^{[X]}_{k}\rho_{k}^{[X]}\,,\quad X\in\{V,S\}\,,

(G.10)

for some matrix of operators
A_{k}^{[X]}
, whose exact form is not important, and where
\rho_{k}
is as given in (
6.167
); the vectors
u_{k}
and
\upsilon_{k}
now gain additional terms arising from
\overset{(*)}{r}
,
\overset{(*)}{s}
and
\overset{(0)}{s}
(cf. (
6.4.3
)-(
6.4.3
)): for
n>5
,

\displaystyle u^{[X]}_{k}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}(\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{r}\,,\hskip-14.22636pt\underbrace{\overset{(*)}{r}}_{\text{for }X=S\text{ only}}\hskip-14.22636pt\,,\overset{(1)}{r}\,,\dots\,,\overset{(\frac{n-5}{2})}{r}\,,\overset{(0)}{s}\,,\overset{(1)}{s}\,,\dots\,,\overset{(k)}{s})^{T[X]},&\hbox{$k=\frac{n-3}{2}$;}\\
(\underbrace{\overset{(k)}{r}\,,\overset{(k-1)}{r}\,,\dots\,,\overset{(\frac{n+1}{2})}{r}}_{k_{n}\text{ terms}}\overset{(\frac{n-1}{2})}{r}\,,\overset{(\frac{n-3}{2})}{r}\,,\overset{(*)}{s}\,,\hskip-14.22636pt\underbrace{\overset{(*)}{r}}_{\text{for }X=S\text{ only}}\hskip-14.22636pt\,,\overset{(1)}{r}\,,\dots\,,\overset{(\frac{n-5}{2})}{r}\,,\overset{(0)}{s}\,,\overset{(1)}{s}\,,\dots\,,\overset{(k)}{s})^{T[X]},&\hbox{$k>\frac{n-3}{2}$,}\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\upsilon_{k}^{[X]}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}(\upsilon_{k,2}\,,\hskip-14.22636pt\underbrace{\overset{[5-n]}{w}}_{\text{for }X=S\text{ only}}\hskip-14.22636pt\,,\overset{[\frac{9-n}{2}]}{w}\,,\dots\,,\overset{[1]}{w}\,,\overset{[4]}{w}\,,\overset{[5]}{w}\,,\dots\,\overset{[4+k]}{w})^{T[X]},&\hbox{$k=\frac{n-3}{2}$;}\\
(\text{gauge fields}\,,\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}_{A}\,,\upsilon_{k,2}\,,\overset{[4-n]}{w}\,,\hskip-14.22636pt\underbrace{\overset{[5-n]}{w}}_{\text{for }X=S\text{ only}}\hskip-14.22636pt\,,\overset{[\frac{9-n}{2}]}{w}\,,\dots\,,\overset{[1]}{w}\,,\overset{[4]}{w}\,,\overset{[5]}{w}\,,\dots\,\overset{[4+k]}{w})^{T[X]},&\hbox{$k>\frac{n-3}{2}$.}\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\Theta_{k}^{[X]}

\displaystyle:=\begin{cases}\big(\ \overset{[\frac{7-n-2i}{2}]}{w}\ \big)_{1\leq i\leq\frac{n-5}{2}}\,,&X=S\,,\\
\big(\ \overset{[\frac{7-n-2i}{2}]}{w}\ \big)_{1\leq i\leq\frac{n-3}{2}}\,,&X=V\,;\end{cases}

(G.15)

the “gauge fields” in (
G
) are the same as in (
6.4.3
). The case
n=5
is handled in the same way after performing a trivial elimination of some of the terms from the system (compare the last paragraph of Section
6.4.3
).

The analysis for solving (
G.10
) then proceeds in a similar way to the
\alpha=0
case.
First, we write (
G.10
) in a mode decomposition, which can be done since, as before, all operators appearing in the matrices are sums of products of operators of the form
\tilde{L}_{a,b,c}
. Next, using the same arguments as in Appendix
F.1
, one can verify that the matrix
\Lambda_{k}^{[0,X]}
remains lower triangular and hence
\Lambda_{k}^{[X]}
is elliptic in the sense of Agmon, Douglis and Nirenberg. See
Figure
G.1

for a submatrix of
\Lambda_{k}^{[S]}
which includes all important additions. Thus, following the same arguments as in the
\alpha=0
case, there exists
N(k)\in\mathbb{N}
such that for all modes
\ell\geq N(k)
, the system can be solved by setting
\Theta_{k,\ell}^{[X]}=0=\rho_{k,\ell}^{[X]}
.

,

; see figure

for the meaning of the entries. The same arguments in Appendix

can be used to show that the block
A_{1}
is diagonal and that the entries in the blocks labeled “D” and “
A_{2}
” are lower than the highlighted entries of the first and second rows respectively. The remaining rows and columns associated to
\overset{(i)}{r}\,,1\leq i\leq\frac{n-5}{2}
, which are not displayed here, are identical to that in figure
F.1
. The
t_{j}
’s are zero and the
s_{i}
’s are twice the figures highlighted on the diagonal.

Next, to show that, for each
\ell
with
\eta_{\ell}\in{\mathrm{CKV}^{\perp}}

and
\ell<N(k)
, the system
admits a solution, we perform a permutation which now brings
u_{k}
and
\upsilon_{k}
to (cf. (
6.175
)-(
6.4.3
))

\displaystyle\hat{u}^{[X]}_{k}

\displaystyle:=(\overset{(*)}{s}\,,\hskip-17.07182pt\underbrace{\overset{(*)}{r}}_{\text{for }X=S\text{ only}}\hskip-17.07182pt\,,\overset{(0)}{s}\,,\overset{(1)}{r}\,,\overset{(1)}{s}\,,\overset{(2)}{r}\,,\overset{(2)}{s}\,,\dots\,,\overset{(k)}{r}\,,\overset{(k)}{s})^{T[X]}\,,

(G.16)

\displaystyle\hat{\upsilon}^{[X]}_{k}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}(\upsilon_{k,2}\,,\overset{[4-n]}{w}\,,\hskip-17.07182pt\underbrace{\overset{[5-n]}{w}}_{\text{for }X=S\text{ only}}\hskip-17.07182pt\,,\overset{[4]}{w}\,,\overset{[\frac{9-n}{2}]}{w}\,,\dots\,,\overset{[1]}{w}\,,\overset{[5]}{w}\,,\dots\,\overset{[4+k]}{w})^{T[X]},&\hbox{$k=\frac{n-3}{2}$;}\\
(\text{first terms}\,,\overset{[4-n]}{w}\,,\hskip-17.07182pt\underbrace{\overset{[5-n]}{w}}_{\text{for }X=S\text{ only}}\hskip-17.07182pt\,,\overset{[4]}{w}\,,\overset{[\frac{9-n}{2}]}{w}\,,\dots\,,\overset{[1]}{w}\,,\overset{[5]}{w}\,,\dots\,\overset{[4+k]}{w})^{T[X]},&\hbox{$k>\frac{n-3}{2}$,}\end{array}\right.

where “first terms” denotes the fields
\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}_{A}\,,\upsilon_{k,2}\,,\partial^{k_{n}}_{u}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}_{A}\,,\partial^{k_{n}-1}_{u}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}_{A}\,,\dots\,,\partial_{u}\overset{(2)}{\xi}{}_{A}
, as in (
6.4.3
).

Finally, we need to show that the matrix
(A^{[X]}_{k}\,\Lambda^{[X]}_{k}\,\mathcal{N}^{[X]}_{k})
is surjective or, equivalently, that its adjoint has trivial kernel. As in the
\alpha=0
case, the matrix
(\Lambda^{[X]}_{k}\,\mathcal{N}^{[X]}_{k})^{\dagger}
has trivial kernel iff

(\hat{\Lambda}^{[X]}_{k}\,\hat{\mathcal{N}}^{[X]}_{k})^{\dagger}

does.
Additionally, it can again be verified that

(\hat{\Lambda}^{[X]}_{k}\,\hat{\mathcal{N}}^{[X]}_{k})^{\dagger}=\begin{pmatrix}F^{[X]}\\
G^{[X]}\end{pmatrix}\circ\operatorname{{}\mathring{\text{d}iv}_{(2)}}\circ\,C\,,

(G.19)

where

can be found in Figure

for

and
k>\frac{n-3}{2}
. All operators appearing in (

are understood to be their restrictions onto the relevant
